On the irregular Hodge filtration of exponentially twisted mixed Hodge modules (C.S.) This research was supported by the grants ANR-08-BLAN-0317-01 and ANR-13-IS01-0001-01 of the Agence nationale de la recherche. (J.-D.Y.) This work was partially supported by the NCTS and the MoST of Taiwan.
Claude Sabbah, Jeng-Daw Yu

Abstract
. Given a mixed Hodge module \EuScript ​ N \EuScript{N} and a meromorphic function f f on a complex manifold, we associate to these data a filtration (the irregular Hodge filtration) on the exponentially twisted holonomic module \EuScript ​ N ⊗ \EuScript ​ E f \EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f} , which extends the construction of [ ESY15 ] . We show the strictness of the push-forward filtered 𝒟 \mathscr{D} -module through any projective morphism π : X → Y \pi:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y , by using the theory of mixed twistor 𝒟 \mathscr{D} -modules of T. Mochizuki. We consider the example of the rescaling of a regular function f f , which leads to an expression of the irregular Hodge filtration of the Laplace transform of the Gauss-Manin systems of f f in terms of the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of the Kontsevich bundles associated with f f .

1.
Introduction

1.a.
The irregular Hodge filtration

The category of mixed Hodge modules on complex manifolds, as constructed by M. Saito
[
Sai90
]
, is endowed with the standard operations (push-forward by projective morphisms, pull-back by holomorphic maps, duality, etc.). In particular, the structure of the Hodge filtration in this category is well-behaved through these operations. For a meromorphic function
f
on a complex manifold
X
, holomorphic on the complement
U
of a divisor
D
of
X
, and for a mixed Hodge module with underlying filtered
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
, we will define an “irregular Hodge filtration”, which is a filtration on the exponentially twisted holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f}
, where
\EuScript{E}^{f}
denote the
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-module
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)
equipped with the twisted integrable connection
{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f
, that we regard as a left holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module. We note that, although
\EuScript{N}
is known to have regular singularities,
\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f}
has irregular singularities along the components of the divisor
D
where
f
takes the value
\infty
, hence cannot underlie a mixed Hodge module. Therefore, the irregular Hodge filtration we define on
\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f}
, generalizing the definition of Deligne
[
Del07
]
, and then
[
Sab10
]
,
[
Yu14
]
,
[
ESY15
]
, cannot be the Hodge filtration of a mixed Hodge module in the sense of
[
Sai90
]
. There is an algebraic variant of this setting, where we assume that
f
is a rational function on a complex smooth variety
X
.

Remark 1.1
.

Such a filtration has been constructed in
[
ESY15
]
in the following cases:

(a)

f
extends as a morphism
X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
D
is a normal crossing divisor and the filtered
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
is equal to
(\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D),F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D))
, where the filtration is given by the order of the pole
[
Del70
]
. In such a setting, the filtration was denoted
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\EuScript{E}^{f}(*H))
, where
H
is the union of the components of
D
not in
f^{-1}(\infty)
;

(b)

X=Y\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
,
f
is the projection to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
underlies an arbitrary mixed Hodge module.

Definition 1.2
.

By a
good filtration
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
indexed by
\mathbb{Q}
of a
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
\EuScript{N}
, we mean a finite family
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{N}
of good filtrations
1
1

1

As usual, this is understood with respect to the filtration by the order
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X}
, and goodness means that
F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{N}=0

p\ll 0
locally on
X
, and
\gr^{F}_{\alpha+{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}\EuScript{N}
is
\gr^{F}\!\mathscr{D}_{X}
-coherent.
indexed by
\mathbb{Z}
, parametrized by
\alpha
in a finite subset
A
of
[0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}
, such that
F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{N}\subset F_{\beta+q}\EuScript{N}
for all
\alpha,\beta\in A
and
p,q\in\mathbb{Z}
satisfying
\alpha+p\leqslant\beta+q
.

We can thus regard it as a single increasing filtration indexed by
\mathbb{Q}
, such that
F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{N}/F_{<\alpha+p}\EuScript{N}=0
for any
\alpha,p
, except for
\alpha
in a finite set
A
of
[0,1)\cap\mathbb{Q}
.

For each
\alpha
, the
Rees module

R_{F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}\EuScript{N}
is the graded module defined as
\sum_{p}F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{N}z^{p}
, where
z
is a new (Laurent) polynomial variable. Then we set
R_{F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{N}:=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\alpha\in A}R_{F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}\EuScript{N}
. We can then regard a (usual) good filtration indexed by
\mathbb{Z}
as a good filtration indexed by
\mathbb{Q}
.

Theorem 1.3
\pointrait

Let
f
be a meromorphic function on
X
, holomorphic on
U=X\smallsetminus\penalty D
, where
D
is a divisor in
X
. For each filtered holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
underlying a mixed Hodge module one can define canonically and functorially a good
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X}
-filtration
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})
indexed by
\mathbb{Q}
which satisfies the following properties:

(1)

Through the canonical isomorphism
(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})_{|U}=\EuScript{N}_{|U}
, we have
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\penalty\EuScript{E}^{f})_{|U}=F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N}_{|U}
.

(2)

For each morphism
\varphi:(\EuScript{N}_{1},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N}_{1})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\EuScript{N}_{2},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N}_{2})
underlying a morphism of mixed Hodge modules, the corresponding morphism

\varphi^{f}:(\EuScript{N}_{1}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f},F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\EuScript{N}_{1}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f}))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\EuScript{N}_{2}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f},F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\EuScript{N}_{2}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f}))

is strictly filtered.

(3)

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
, the push-forward
\pi_{+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\penalty\EuScript{E}^{f},F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\penalty\EuScript{E}^{f}))
by any projective morphism
\pi:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
is strict.

(4)

Let
\pi:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
be a projective morphism and let
h
be a meromorphic function on
Y
, holomorphic on
V=Y\smallsetminus D_{Y}
for some divisor
D_{Y}
in
Y
. Assume that
D_{X}:=\penalty\pi^{-1}(D_{Y})
is a divisor, and set
U=\pi^{-1}(V)
and
f=h\circ\pi
. Then the cohomology of the filtered complex
\pi_{+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\penalty\EuScript{E}^{f},F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\penalty\EuScript{E}^{f}))
, which is strict by (
3
), satisfies

\mathscr{H}^{j}\pi_{+}R_{F^{\mathrm{irr}}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})=R_{F^{\mathrm{irr}}}\big[(\mathscr{H}^{j}\pi_{+}\EuScript{N})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{h}\big].

(5)

In cases
1.1
(
a
) and
1.1
(
b
) above, the filtration
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
coincides with the filtration
F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
constructed in
[
ESY15
]
.

The proof of the theorem is given in
§
5.a
and relies much on the theory of mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules of T. Mochizuki
[
Moc11b
]
. This theory allows one to simplify and generalize some of the arguments given in
[
ESY15
]
, by giving a general framework to treat, from the Hodge point of view, irregular
\mathscr{D}
-modules like
\EuScript{E}^{f}
. By specializing (
3
) to the case where
Y
is a point we obtain:

Corollary 1.4
\pointrait

For
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
underlying a mixed Hodge module on a smooth projective variety
X
, the spectral sequence attached to the hypercohomology of the filtered de Rham complex
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\DR(\EuScript{N}\otimes\penalty\EuScript{E}^{f})
degenerates at
E_{1}
for each
\alpha\in[0,1)
.∎

Remark 1.5
.

The assumption that
D:=X\smallsetminus U
is a divisor is not mandatory, but simplifies the statement. In general, higher cohomology modules supported on
X\smallsetminus U
may appear for
\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f}
.

1.b.
Rescaling a function

The case
1.1
(
a
) is essentially the only case where we can give an explicit expression for
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{irr}}\EuScript{E}^{f}(*H)
(see
[
ESY15
]
, according to
1.3
(
5
)). Recall that we consider a smooth complex projective variety
X
together with a morphism
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We set
P_{\mathrm{red}}=f^{-1}(\infty)
and
P=f^{*}(\infty)
. We also introduce a supplementary divisor
H
(which could be empty) having no common components with
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, and we assume that
D:=P_{\mathrm{red}}\cup H
has normal crossings. We set
U=X\smallsetminus D
. We will also consider
X
as a complex projective manifold equipped with its analytic topology, that we will denote
X^{\mathrm{an}}
when the context is not clear.

Our main example in this article, that we consider in Part
II
, is that of the rescaling of the function
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
. The rescaled function with rescaling parameter
v
is the function
vf:U\times\mathbb{C}_{v}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
, defined by
(x,v)\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}vf(x)
. This function does not extend as a morphism to
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

We consider the projective line
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}
covered by two charts
\mathbb{C}_{v}
and
\mathbb{C}_{u}
whose intersection is denoted by
\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}
, and we regard
vf
as a rational function
vf:X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}\text{ - -}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We are therefore in the situation in the beginning of the previous subsection, with underlying space
\EuScript{X}:=X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}
and reduced pole divisor
\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}}:=(P_{\mathrm{red}}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v})\cup(X\times\infty)
, where
\infty\in\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}
denotes the point
u=0
. We will also set
\EuScript{P}=(P\times\mathbb{P}^{1})+(X\times\{\infty\})
,
\EuScript{H}=H\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}
and
\EuScript{D}=\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}}\cup\EuScript{H}
.

We denote by
\EuScript{E}^{(v{:}u)f}(*\EuScript{H})
the
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}}
-module
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}}(*\EuScript{D})
equipped with the connection
{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}(vf)
(on the open set
X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}
) and
{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}(f/u)
(on the open set
X\times\mathbb{C}_{u}
). We denote its restriction to the corresponding open subsets by
\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
and
\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})
respectively. According to Theorem
1.3
, it is equipped with an irregular Hodge filtration. We make it partly explicit in Theorem
9.1
(only partly, because around
u=0
we only make explicit its restriction to the Brieskorn lattice, see
§
9.b
).

1.c.
Variation of the irregular Hodge filtration and the Kontsevich bundles

Regarding now
v\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
as a parameter and considering the push-forward by
q:X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}
of the rescaling
\EuScript{E}^{(v:u)f}(*\EuScript{H})
, our aim is to describe the variation with
v
of the irregular Hodge filtration
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(U,(\Omega_{U}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}},{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)
considered in
[
ESY15
]
, and its limiting behaviour when
v\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0
or
v\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\infty
.

The irregular Hodge filtration is conveniently computed with the Kontsevich complex. Recall that M. Kontsevich has associated to
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
as in
§
1.b
and to
k\geqslant 0
the subsheaf
\Omega_{f}^{k}
of
\Omega_{X}^{k}(\log D)
consisting of logarithmic
k
-forms
\omega
such that
{\mathrm{d}}f\wedge\omega
remains a logarithmic
(k+1)
-form, a condition which only depends on the restriction of
\omega
to a neighbourhood of the reduced pole divisor
P_{\mathrm{red}}=f^{-1}(\infty)
. For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
, let us denote by
[\alpha P]
the divisor supported on
P_{\mathrm{red}}
with multiplicity
[\alpha e_{i}]
on the component
P_{i}
of
P:=f^{*}(\infty)
with multiplicity
e_{i}
. One can also define a subsheaf
\Omega_{f}^{k}(\alpha)
of
\Omega_{X}^{k}(\log D)([\alpha P])
by the condition that
{\mathrm{d}}f\wedge\omega
is a section of
\Omega_{X}^{k+1}(\log D)([\alpha P])
, so that the case
\alpha=0
is that considered by Kontsevich. Clearly, only those
\alpha
such that
\alpha e_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
for some
i
are relevant. If
f
is the constant map, then
\Omega_{f}^{k}=\Omega_{X}^{k}(\log D)
. One of the main results of
[
ESY15
]
, suggested and proved by Kontsevich when
P=P_{\mathrm{red}}
, is the equality, for each
k
,

\dim\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(U,(\Omega_{U}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}},{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)=\sum_{p+q=k}\dim\boldsymbol{H}^{q}(X,\Omega_{f}^{p}(\alpha)).

More precisely, for each pair
(u,v)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
and each
\alpha\in[0,1)
one can form a complex
(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha),u{\mathrm{d}}+\penalty v{\mathrm{d}}f)
and it is shown that the dimension of the hypercohomology
\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha),u{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)
is independent of
(u,v)\in\mathbb{C}^{2}
and
\alpha
and is equal to the above value. The
irregular Hodge numbers
are then defined as

h_{\alpha}^{p,q}(f)=\dim\boldsymbol{H}^{q}(X,\Omega_{f}^{p}(\alpha)).

(1.6)

We have
h_{\alpha}^{p,q}(f)\neq 0
only if
p,q\geqslant 0
and
p+q\leqslant 2\dim X
. (see Remark
8.20
(
3
) for the mirror symmetry motivations related to the irregular Hodge filtration.) If
f
is the constant map, we recover the results of Deligne
[
Del70
,
Del71
]
:

\displaystyle\dim\boldsymbol{H}^{k}(U,\mathbb{C})=\dim\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(U,(\Omega_{U}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}},{\mathrm{d}})\big)

\displaystyle=\dim\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{X}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\log D),{\mathrm{d}})\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{p+q=k}\dim H^{q}\big(X,\Omega_{X}^{p}(\log D)\big).

The Hodge numbers reduce here to
h^{p,q}(X,D)=\dim H^{q}\big(X,\Omega_{X}^{p}(\log D)\big)
.

Following the suggestion of M. Kontsevich, let us define the
Kontsevich bundles

\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}
. We set

\begin{split}\mathscr{K}^{k}_{v}(\alpha)&:=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\big),\\
\mathscr{K}^{k}_{u}(\alpha)&:=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\big).\end{split}

(1.7)

Using the isomorphism
\mathbb{C}[u,u^{-1}]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{C}[v,v^{-1}]
given by
u\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}v^{-1}
, we have a natural quasi-isomorphism

u^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}:(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v,v^{-1}],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[u,u^{-1}],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)

(1.8)

induced by the multiplication by
u^{p}
on the
p
th term of the first complex. Since we know by the above mentioned results that both modules
\mathscr{K}^{k}_{v}(\alpha),\mathscr{K}^{k}_{u}(\alpha)
are free over their respective ring
\mathbb{C}[v]
or
\mathbb{C}[u]
, the identification

\boldsymbol{H}^{k}(u^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}):\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v,v^{-1}],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)\simeq\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[u,u^{-1}],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)

allows us to glue these modules as a bundle
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}
. The
E_{1}
-degeneration property can be expressed by the injectivity

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,\sigma^{\geqslant p}(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\big),

(1.9)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,\sigma^{\geqslant p}(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\big),

where
\sigma^{\geqslant p}
denotes the stupid truncation. Since this truncation is compatible with the gluing
u^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}
, this defines a filtration
\sigma^{\geqslant p}\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
. When restricted to
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{v}
, this produces the family
F_{\alpha}^{{\mathrm{irr}},p}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(U,(\Omega_{U}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}},{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)
.

We also notice that the
p
th graded bundle is then isomorphic to
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(p)^{h_{\alpha}^{p,k-p}(f)}
, so this filtration is the Harder-Narasimhan filtration
F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
and the Birkhoff-Grothendieck decomposition of
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
reads

\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)\simeq\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{p=0}^{k}\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(p)^{h_{\alpha}^{p,k-p}(f)}.

(1.10)

In particular, all slopes of
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
are nonnegative and we have

\deg\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)=\sum_{p=0}^{k}p\cdot h_{\alpha}^{p,k-p}(f).

We will show (see Lemma
6.2
) that each
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
is naturally equipped with a meromorphic connection having a simple pole at
v=0
and a double pole at most at
v=\infty
. It follows from a remark due to Mochizuki (see Remark
6.3
) that the Harder-Narasimhan filtration satisfies the Griffiths transversality condition with respect to the connection. This is a concrete description of the variation of the irregular Hodge filtration (Corollary
6.6
).

Our main result concerns the limiting behaviour of the variation of the irregular Hodge filtration when
v\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0
, expressed in this model.

Theorem 1.11
\pointrait

(1)

The meromorphic connection
\nabla
on
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
has a logarithmic pole at
v=0
and the eigenvalues of its residue
\Res_{v=0}\nabla
belong to
[-\alpha,-\alpha+1)\cap\mathbb{Q}
.

(2)

On each generalized eigenspace of
\Res_{v=0}\nabla
the nilpotent part of the residue strictly shifts by
-1
the filtration naturally induced by the Harder-Narasimhan filtration.

The proof of Theorem
1.11
, which is sketched in
§
6
, does not remain however in the realm of Kontsevich bundles. It is obtained through an identification of the Kontsevich bundles with the bundles
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\alpha)
obtained from the push-forward
\mathscr{D}
-modules
\mathscr{H}^{k}
of
\EuScript{E}^{(v{:}u)f}(*\EuScript{H})
(see
§
1.b
) by the projection
q:X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}
. Recall that
\mathscr{H}^{k}:=R^{k}q_{*}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}/\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}}\EuScript{E}^{(v{:}u)f}(*\EuScript{H})
is a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}}
-module for each
k
. It is equipped with its irregular Hodge filtration
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{H}^{k}
obtained by push-forward, according to Theorem
1.3
(
3
). We define the bundles
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\alpha)
by using this filtration, and the main comparison tools with the Kontsevich bundles
\mathscr{K}(\alpha)
are provided by Theorems
6.4
and
6.5
.

1.d.
Motivations and open questions

We have already discussed in
[
ESY15
, Introduction]
the motivation coming from estimating
p
-adic eigenvalues of Frobenius (Deligne) and that coming from mirror symmetry (Kontsevich). We list below some more related questions and possible applications for further investigations.

Numerical invariants of mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules

The theory of mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules, as developed by T. Mochizuki
[
Moc11b
]
, is the convenient framework to treat wild Hodge theory. However, this theory produces very few numerical invariants having a Hodge flavor (like Hodge numbers, degrees of Hodge bundles, etc.). The irregular Hodge filtration, when it does exist, is intended to provide such invariants. Let us emphasize that, contrary to classical Hodge theory, the irregular Hodge filtration is only a by-product of the mixed twistor structure, but is not constitutive of its definition.

Is there a suitable well-behaved category of
wild Hodge
\mathscr{D}
-modules
with a forgetful functor to the category of mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules? What about the expected functorial and degeneration properties? The exponentially twisted Hodge modules should give rise to an object in such a category. Moreover, following the definition due to Simpson of systems of Hodge bundles, we can expect that the objects in this suitable category should carry an internal symmetry (a
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action in the case of tame twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules). A possible approach to this question would be to search for the desired category as the category of integrable mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules endowed with supplementary structures on the object obtained by rescaling the twistor variable.

Analogies with Hodge theory

Going further in the direction of Hodge theory, one may wonder whether the irregular Hodge filtration, when it exists, shares similar properties with the usual Hodge filtration on mixed Hodge modules. For example, for a morphism
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, the
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
\EuScript{E}^{f}
underlies a pure integrable twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module (see Proposition
3.3
(
2
)) and is equipped with an irregular Hodge filtration (see Theorem
1.3
with
\EuScript{N}=(\mathscr{O}_{X},{\mathrm{d}})
). Let
\pi:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
be a projective morphism. According to the decomposition theorem for pure twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules
[
Moc11a
]
, the push-forward
\pi_{+}\EuScript{E}^{f}
decomposes, together with its twistor structure, into a direct sum of possibly shifted simple holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules. One can wonder whether the analogues of Kollár’s conjectures (proved by M. Saito
[
Sai91
]
) hold for the irregular Hodge filtration of
\EuScript{E}^{f}
.

Also the question of the limiting behaviour, in the sense of Schmid, of the irregular Hodge filtration raises interesting questions. We treat the case of a tame degeneration (the case of
\EuScript{E}^{vf}
when
v\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0
) in
§
7
, but the case of a non-tame degeneration (like
u\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0
in
§
8
) remains unclear in general. We expect that the good behaviour (by definition) of the mixed twistor modules by taking irregular nearby cycles along a holomorphic function should lead to specific limiting properties for the irregular Hodge filtration, when it exists.

Extended motivic-exponential
\mathscr{D}
-modules

Recall that, following
[
BBD82
, 6.2.4]
, one defines the notion of a simple regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-module
of geometric origin
on a smooth complex algebraic variety
X
if it appears as a simple subquotient in a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module obtained by using only standard geometric functors starting from the case where the variety is a point. In particular, such a simple regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module is a simple summand of a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-module underlying a polarizable
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge module of some weight, as defined by M. Saito
[
Sai88
,
Sai90
]
. It therefore underlies a simple complex polarizable Hodge module. In other words, there exists an irreducible algebraic closed subvariety
Z\subset X
, a Zariski smooth open set
Z^{\circ}\subset Z
, and an irreducible local system on
Z^{\circ}
, underlying a polarizable complex variation of Hodge structure (see
[
Del87
]
), such that this regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module corresponds, via the inverse Riemann-Hilbert correspondence, to the intermediate extension of this local system by the inclusion
Z^{\circ}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}X
. In particular, it comes equipped with a good filtration (that induced by the polarizable
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge module) and the corresponding filtered
\mathscr{D}
-module is a direct summand of the filtered
\mathscr{D}
-module underlying the polarizable
\mathbb{Q}
-Hodge module.

M. Kontsevich
[
Kon09
]
has defined the category of
motivic-exponential
\mathscr{D}
-modules
by adding the twist by
\EuScript{E}^{f}
for any rational function
f
to the standard permissible operations on regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules of geometric origin on algebraic varieties. By
[
Moc11b
]
, any such motivic-exponential
\mathscr{D}
-module underlies a pure wild twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module (see
[
Moc11a
]
).

There is also the category of
extended motivic-exponential
\mathscr{D}
-modules
, by authorizing extensions of such objects, but we will not consider it here.

One can expect that any motivic-exponential
\mathscr{D}
-module on a complex algebraic
variety
is endowed with a canonical irregular Hodge filtration, and that this filtration has a good behaviour with respect to the various permissible functors (the six operations of Grothendieck, the nearby and vanishing cycles along a function, and the twist by some
\EuScript{E}^{f}
). Theorem
1.3
is a step toward this expected result.

Remark 1.12

(Hodge filtration in presence of very irregular singularities)
.

The holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-modules one obtains as
\mathscr{H}^{k}\pi_{+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})
when
\pi
is any projective morphism may have irregular singularities much more complicated than an exponential twist of a regular singularity. For example, if
Y
is a disc, it is shown in
[
Rou07
]
that any formal meromorphic connection at
0\in Y
can be produced as the formalization at the origin of a connection obtained by the procedure of Th.
1.3
(
3
) for some suitable
\EuScript{N}
on
X=Y\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

However, these
\mathscr{D}_{Y}
-modules come equipped with a good filtration
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{H}^{k}\pi_{+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\penalty\EuScript{E}^{f})
obtained by pushing-forward
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})
.
If
Y
is projective and if for example
\mathscr{H}^{k}\pi_{+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})=0
except for
k=k_{o}
then, according to Corollary
1.4
, we obtain the degeneration at
E_{1}
of the spectral sequence attached to the hypercohomology of the filtered de Rham complex
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\DR\mathscr{H}^{k_{o}}\pi_{+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})
. Examples of this kind can be obtained by the procedure of
[
Rou07
]
with arbitrary complicated irregular singularities.

Acknowledgments

We thank Maxim Kontsevich for suggesting us the properties stated in Theorem
1.11
and Takuro Mochizuki for explaining us some of his results on mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules and his useful comments. In particular, he suggested various improvements and simplifications to the first version of this article. We owe him the statement of Theorem
6.4
. Last but not least, we thank Hélène Esnault for the many discussions we had together and for many suggestions and questions on the subject of this article.

Part I

Irregular Hodge filtration and twist by
\EuScript{E}^{f}

2.
Exponentially regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules

2.a.
The graph construction

We refer to the expository book
[
HTT08
]
or the expository article
[
Meb04
]
for basic properties of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules.

Let
X
be a complex manifold and let
P_{\mathrm{red}}
be a reduced divisor in
X
. We set
U=X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}}
. Let
f
be a meromorphic function on
X
which is holomorphic on
U
whose pole divisor
P
is
exactly
supported by
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, i.e.,
f
takes the value
\infty
generically on each irreducible component of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
. By definition, locally analytically on
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, the function
f
can be written as the quotient of two holomorphic functions with no common factor, such that the zero divisor may intersect
P_{\mathrm{red}}
in codimension two in
X
at most. There exists a proper modification
\pi:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
with
X^{\prime}
smooth, which is an isomorphism over
U
, and a holomorphic map
f^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}
, such that
f^{\prime}_{|\pi^{-1}(U)}=f\circ\pi_{|\pi^{-1}(U)}
. The pole divisor
P^{\prime}
of
f^{\prime}
satisfies
P^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}\subset\pi^{-1}(P_{\mathrm{red}})=:D^{\prime}
, and the inclusion may be strict. Let
i_{f}:U\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}U\times\mathbb{C}_{t}
denote the graph inclusion of
f
. The closure
\overline{U}_{f}
of
U_{f}:=i_{f}(U)
in
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}
is a closed analytic set of codimension one, equal to the projection by the proper modification
\pi\times\id:X^{\prime}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}
of the graph
i_{f^{\prime}}(X^{\prime})
. The projection
p:X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
induces a proper modification
\overline{U}_{f}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
, and the pull-back of
U
in
\overline{U}_{f}
maps isomorphically to
U
. In particular, we have
(X\times\infty)\cap\overline{U}_{f}\subset(P_{\mathrm{red}}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t})\cap\overline{U}_{f}
. We summarize this in the following diagram.

3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{U\times\mathbb{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.3021pt\raise-43.38147pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 52.88367pt\raise-75.66219pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 118.3021pt\raise-75.66219pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 168.22708pt\raise-75.66219pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathbb{P}^{1}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

(2.1)

Let
\EuScript{N}
be a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module. We assume that
\EuScript{N}
is equal to its localization
\EuScript{N}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
(if not, replace
\EuScript{N}
with
\EuScript{N}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
, which is also a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module, by a theorem of Kashiwara). The localized pull-back
\EuScript{N}^{\prime}:=\pi^{+}\EuScript{N}(*D^{\prime})
consists of a single holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}}
-module. We then recover
\EuScript{N}
as the push-forward
\pi_{+}\EuScript{N}^{\prime}=\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\EuScript{N}^{\prime}
(see e.g.
[
Sab13
, Prop. 8.13]
).

Let us set
\EuScript{M}^{\prime}=i_{f^{\prime},+}\EuScript{N}^{\prime}
. Then
\EuScript{M}^{\prime}=\EuScript{M}^{\prime}(*(D^{\prime}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}))
and since
\Supp\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\cap(X^{\prime}\times\penalty\infty)\subset(D^{\prime}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t})
, we also have
\EuScript{M}^{\prime}=\EuScript{M}^{\prime}(*[(D^{\prime}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t})\cup(X^{\prime}\times\infty)])
. We clearly have
\EuScript{N}^{\prime}=p^{\prime}_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}=\mathscr{H}^{0}p^{\prime}_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}
.

We set
\EuScript{M}=(\pi\times\id)_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}=\mathscr{H}^{0}(\pi\times\id)_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}
. Then

\EuScript{M}=\EuScript{M}(*(P\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}))=\EuScript{M}(*[(P\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t})\cup(X\times\infty)]),

and
\EuScript{N}=p_{+}\EuScript{M}=\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}\EuScript{M}
. We notice that
\EuScript{M}
does not depend on the choice of
\pi:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
. We will use the notation
\EuScript{M}=i_{f,\oplus}\EuScript{N}
, for which we still have
p_{+}i_{f,\oplus}=\id
, and which coincides with
i_{f,+}\EuScript{N}
if
f
extends from
X
to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
(i.e., if we can take
\pi=\id
, so that
f^{\prime}=f
).

Lemma 2.2
\pointrait

If
\EuScript{N}
is regular holonomic, so is
\EuScript{M}=i_{f,\oplus}\EuScript{N}
.

Proof.

Indeed,
\EuScript{N}^{\prime}
is then regular, hence
\EuScript{M}^{\prime}
also, and then
\EuScript{M}
too.
∎

Remark 2.3

(The graph construction for mixed Hodge modules)
.

Let us now start with a filtered
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
underlying a mixed Hodge module
[
Sai90
]
. We still assume that
\EuScript{N}=\EuScript{N}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
(if this is not the case, we use the localization functor in the category of mixed Hodge modules to fulfill the assumption). The construction of §
2.a
can be done for mixed Hodge modules, by using the corresponding functors in the category of mixed Hodge modules. We therefore get a mixed Hodge module
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})
on
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}
such that
p_{+}(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})=\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})=(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
. If
f
extends as a morphism
X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, then
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})=i_{f,+}(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
.

2.b.
Exponential twist of holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules

The differential
{\mathrm{d}}f
of the function
f:U\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{t}
extends as a meromorphic
1
-form on
X
with poles along
P_{\mathrm{red}}
. We denote by
\EuScript{E}^{f}
the free
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
-module of rank one equipped with the connection
{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f
. For
\EuScript{N}
as in
§
2.a
(in particular,
\EuScript{N}=\EuScript{N}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
), we consider the holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
\EuScript{N}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\EuScript{E}^{f}
.

Lemma 2.4
\pointrait

For
\EuScript{M}=i_{f,\oplus}\EuScript{N}
, we have
\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}\simeq i_{f,\oplus}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})
.

This implies
\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f}\simeq p_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})=\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
.

Proof.

Assume first that
f
extends as a map
X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}
. We will work in the chart centered at
\infty
in
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, with coordinate
t^{\prime}
, and we will set
g=(t^{\prime}\circ f)^{-1}
, so that
f^{-1}(\infty)=g^{-1}(0)
. We denote by
\mathrm{e}^{1/g}
the generator of
\EuScript{E}^{1/g}
. We have

i_{g,+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/g})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/g})\otimes\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{k}\delta(t^{\prime}-g)

with its standard
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{t^{\prime}}}
-module structure. There exists thus a unique
\mathscr{O}_{X}[\partial_{t^{\prime}}]
-linear isomorphism
i_{f,+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/g})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}}
induced by

(n\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/g})\otimes\delta(t^{\prime}-g)\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}(n\otimes\delta(t^{\prime}-g))\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}}.

In other words, for each
k
,

(n\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/g})\otimes\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{k}\delta(t^{\prime}-g)\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{k}\big[(n\otimes\delta(t^{\prime}-g))\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}}\big].

By using the same argument as in the proof of
[
ESY15
, (1.6.5)]
, one shows that this isomorphism is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-linear.

Let us now consider the general case. By definition,

i_{f,\oplus}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})=(\pi\times\id)_{+}i_{f^{\prime},+}\big[\pi^{+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})(*D^{\prime})\big].

One then checks that

\pi^{+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})(*D^{\prime})=(\pi^{+}\EuScript{N})(*D^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f^{\prime}}=\EuScript{N}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f^{\prime}},

so
i_{f^{\prime},+}\big[\pi^{+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})(*D^{\prime})\big]=\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\penalty\EuScript{E}^{t}
by the argument above. Then, because
\EuScript{E}^{t}=(\pi\times\id)^{+}\EuScript{E}^{t}
, we have
(\pi\times\penalty\id)_{+}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\penalty\EuScript{E}^{t})=\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}
.
∎

2.c.
Exponentially regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules

Lemma 2.5
\pointrait

Assume that
\EuScript{M}
is any regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-module. Then the push-forward
p_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
has holonomic cohomology and satisfies
\mathscr{H}^{k}p_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})=0
for
k\neq 0
.

Proof.

The first statement follows from the holonomicity of
\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}
. We can assume that
\EuScript{M}=\EuScript{M}(*\infty)
. Let us set
M=p_{*}\EuScript{M}
. Then
M
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}[t]\langle\partial_{t}\rangle
-module, and
p_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
is the complex

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}M\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\partial_{t}+1\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{t}+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\underset{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0,

where the
{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
indicates the term in degree zero. Set
K=\mathscr{H}^{-1}p_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})=\ker(\partial_{t}+\penalty 1)
. It is
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-holonomic, and the
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-linear inclusion
K\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}M
extends as a natural
\mathscr{D}_{X}[t]\langle\partial_{t}\rangle
-linear morphism
K[t]\otimes E^{-t}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}M
. It is clear that
K[t]\otimes E^{-t}
is purely irregular along
t=\infty
(this is easily seen on the generic part of the support of
K
) hence, since
M
is regular, this image is zero, so
K=0
.
∎

Definition 2.6
.

We say that a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
\EuScript{N}_{\exp}
is
exponentially regular
if there exists a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-module
\EuScript{M}
such that
\EuScript{N}_{\exp}\simeq\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
.

Proposition 2.7
\pointrait

(1)

If
f
is meromorphic on
X
and holomorphic on
U=X\smallsetminus D
, and if
\EuScript{N}=\EuScript{N}(*D)
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module, then
\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f}
is exponentially regular.

(2)

Let
\pi:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
be a proper morphism and let
\EuScript{N}_{\exp}
be exponentially regular on
X
. Then for each
j
,
\mathscr{H}^{j}\pi_{+}\EuScript{N}_{\exp}
is exponentially regular on
Y
.

Proof.

The first point follows from Lemma
2.2
and Lemma
2.4
. For the second point, set
\EuScript{N}_{\exp}=\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
with
\EuScript{M}
regular on
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
. We have, according to Lemma
2.5
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}\pi_{+}\EuScript{N}_{\exp}=\mathscr{H}^{j}\pi_{+}(\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{X,+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}))

\displaystyle=\mathscr{H}^{j}(\pi_{+}p_{X,+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}))

\displaystyle=\mathscr{H}^{j}(p_{Y,+}(\pi\times\id)_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})).

Now,
(\pi\times\id)_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})=(\pi_{+}\EuScript{M})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}
, with
\pi_{+}\EuScript{M}
having regular holonomic cohomology. We thus have
\mathscr{H}^{k}p_{Y,+}\mathscr{H}^{j}(\pi\times\id)_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})=0
for
k\neq 0
according to Lemma
2.5
, hence

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}\big(p_{Y,+}(\pi\times\id)_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})\big)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{Y,+}\mathscr{H}^{j}(\pi\times\id)_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{Y,+}\big((\mathscr{H}^{j}\pi_{+}\EuScript{M})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}\big).\qed

3.
The mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module attached to
\EuScript{E}^{f}

If
f
is a rational function on
X
with pole divisor
P
, the twist of a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module by
\EuScript{E}^{f}
consists first in localizing this module along
P_{\mathrm{red}}
and then in adding
{\mathrm{d}}f
to its connection. The main property used is that the localization functor on holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-modules preserves coherence (hence holonomy).

For a filtered holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module, the stupid localization functor
(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
, which consists in localizing both the module and its filtration, does not preserve coherence since the localization of a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-module does not remain
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-coherent. In the theory of mixed Hodge modules, there is a localization functor which extends the one at the level of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules. We will now consider the case of
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}
-modules and mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules, in order to treat the Laplace transform of mixed Hodge modules.

We keep the analytic setting of
§
2.a
. Recall the following notation used in the theory of twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules (see
[
Sab05
,
Moc07
,
Moc11a
]
). For a complex manifold
X
, we denote by
\mathscr{X}
the product
X\times\mathbb{C}_{z}
of
X
with the complex line having coordinate
z
. The ring
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}
is the analytification of the Rees ring
R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{X}:=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k\in\mathbb{N}}F_{k}\mathscr{D}_{X}z^{k}
attached to the ring of differential operators equipped with its standard filtration by the order. It is locally expressed as
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\langle\eth_{x_{1}},\dots,\eth_{x_{n}}\rangle
, where
\eth_{x_{i}}:=z\partial_{x_{i}}
.

The smooth case

We denote by
\mathscr{E}_{U}^{f/z}
the
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{U}}
-module
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{U}}
equipped with the
z
-connection
z{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f
. By using the same argument as in
[
Sab04
,
§
2.2]
, one checks that
\mathscr{E}_{U}^{f/z}
underlies a smooth twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module; equivalently, it corresponds to a harmonic metric on the flat bundle
(\mathscr{O}_{U},{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)
. It follows that
\mathscr{E}_{U}^{f/z}
underlies a polarized variation of smooth twistor structure of weight
0
, equivalently a pure polarized smooth twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module.

The stupid localization

Similarly, writing for short
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}}):=\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\big({*}(P_{\mathrm{red}}\times\mathbb{C}_{z})\big)
, we consider
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}:=(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}}),z{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)
, where we denote the global section
1
of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
by
\mathrm{e}^{f/z}
. This is a coherent
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
-module (however, it is not necessarily
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}
-coherent). Note also that there is a natural action of
z^{2}\partial_{z}
, by setting
z^{2}\partial_{z}(\mathrm{e}^{f/z}):=-f\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}
in
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
. This action commutes with that of the
z
-connection. We say that
(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}}),z{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)
is
integrable
(see
[
Sab05
, Chap. 7]
).

Lemma 3.1
\pointrait

Assume that
f:U\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
extends as a holomorphic map
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Then
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}
is
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}
-coherent.

Proof.

The question is local near a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
and, up to shrinking
X
, we may assume that
f=1/g
for some holomorphic function
g:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
. Then
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\penalty\mathrm{e}^{f/z}=\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*\{g=0\})\mathrm{e}^{1/gz}
. If
P_{\mathrm{red}}
has normal crossings, we choose local coordinates such that
g=x^{\boldsymbol{e}}
, and the relation
x^{\boldsymbol{e}}\eth_{x_{i}}\mathrm{e}^{1/x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z}=(-e_{i}/x_{i})\mathrm{e}^{1/x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z}
gives the coherence. If
P_{\mathrm{red}}
is arbitrary, let
\pi:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
be a projective modification over a neighbourhood of the point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
we consider, such that
\pi^{-1}(P_{\mathrm{red}})
has normal crossings. Set
g^{\prime}=g\circ\pi
. Then

\pi_{+}\big(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}(*\{g^{\prime}=0\})\mathrm{e}^{1/g^{\prime}z}\big)=\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\big(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}(*\{g^{\prime}=0\})\mathrm{e}^{1/g^{\prime}z}\big)=\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*\{g=0\})\mathrm{e}^{1/gz},

since it can be seen that
g
is invertible on
\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\big(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}(*\{g^{\prime}=0\})\mathrm{e}^{1/g^{\prime}z}\big)
. By the properness of
\pi
,
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*\{g=0\})\mathrm{e}^{1/gz}
is then
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}
-coherent.
∎

Proposition 3.2
\pointrait

If
g:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
is holomorphic,
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*\{g=0\})\mathrm{e}^{1/gz}
underlies a pure wild twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module of weight zero.

As a consequence, the same property holds for
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}
if
f:U\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
extends as
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Proof of Proposition
3.2
.

This is essentially obvious from the theory of T. Mochizuki
[
Moc11a
]
, but we will make the argument precise. Firstly, one can reduce to the case where
g=0
has normal crossings, since pure wild twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module of weight zero are stable by
\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}
, if
\pi
is a projective morphism. Here we take
\pi
as in the proof of Lemma
3.1
.

Set now
U=\{g\neq 0\}\subset X
. Let
(\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{U},\overline{\partial},h)
be the trivial bundle with its standard holomorphic structure, equipped with its standard metric for which
h(1,1)=1
. Consider it as a harmonic Higgs bundle on
U
with holomorphic Higgs field
\theta={\mathrm{d}}(1/g)
. Since
g
is a monomial (in local coordinates), this produces a non-ramified good wild harmonic bundle on
X
, in the sense of
[
Moc11a
, Def. 7.1.7]
.

For a fixed
z
(denoted by
\lambda
in loc. cit.), denote by
E^{z}
the holomorphic bundle
(\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{U},\overline{\partial}+z{\mathrm{d}}(1/\overline{g}))
. The extension
\EuScript{P}E^{z}
defined in
[
Moc11a
, Not. 7.4.1]
is nothing but
\mathscr{O}_{X}\cdot\exp(\overline{z}/g-z/\overline{g})
. Together with its natural connection, it is isomorphic to
\EuScript{E}^{(1+|z|^{2})/gz}
(see Example 7.4.1.2 in loc. cit.). Since there is no Stokes phenomenon in rank one, the construction
\EuScript{Q}E^{z}
of §11.1 in loc. cit. consists only in dividing the irregular value by
1+|z|^{2}
, so
\EuScript{Q}E^{z}\simeq\EuScript{E}^{1/gz}
(first point of Th. 11.1.2 in loc. cit.). Now,
\EuScript{E}^{1/gz}
is the canonical prolongation of
(\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{U},\overline{\partial},{\mathrm{d}}(1/g),h)
as a coherent
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}
-module. It is also equal to the
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}
-module denoted by
\mathfrak{E}
in loc. cit. (see §12.3.2). Then one concludes by using Prop. 19.2.1 of loc. cit. ∎

The twistor localization

Let
H
be a divisor in
X
, locally defined by a holomorphic function
h
and let
\mathscr{N}
be a coherent
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}(*H)
-module. According to
[
Moc11b
, Def. 3.3.1]
, one says that
\mathscr{N}
is twistor-specializable along
H
if there exists a coherent
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}
-submodule
\mathscr{N}[*H]\subset\mathscr{N}
such that, considering locally the graph inclusion
i_{h}:X\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}Y:=X\times\mathbb{C}
with the coordinate
t
on
\mathbb{C}
,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

the coherent
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{Y}}(*\{t=0\})
-module
i_{h,+}\mathscr{N}
is strictly specializable along
t=0
, in the sense of
[
Sab05
,
§
3.4.a]
,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

i_{h,+}(\mathscr{N}[*H])
is equal to the coherent
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{Y}}
-submodule of
i_{h,+}\mathscr{N}
generated by the
V^{t}_{1}
term of the
V
-filtration (with the convention taken in this article), denoted by
(i_{h,+}\mathscr{N})[*t]
.

If
\mathscr{N}[*H]
exists locally, it is unique, hence exists globally. The category
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}
is introduced in
§
7.2 of
[
Moc11b
]
, and the results of loc. cit. imply the following.

Proposition 3.3
\pointrait

Let
f
be any meromorphic function on
X
with pole divisor
P
.

(1)

The coherent
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
-module
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}
is twistor-specializable along
P_{\mathrm{red}}
and defines
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}[*P_{\mathrm{red}}]=:\mathscr{E}_{X}^{f/z}
.

(2)

Moreover,
\mathscr{E}_{X}^{f/z}
underlies an object of
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}(X)
extending the object of
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}(U)
that
\mathscr{E}_{U}^{f/z}
underlies.

(3)

If
f
extends as a morphism
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, then
\mathscr{E}_{X}^{f/z}=\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}
and the object of
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}(X)
it underlies is pure of weight zero.

(4)

Let
H
be any divisor in
X
. Then
\mathscr{E}_{X}^{f/z}(*H)
is twistor-specializable along
H
and the corresponding object
\mathscr{E}_{X}^{f/z}[*H]
underlies an object of
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}(X)
.

Proof.

Let us start by (
3
). Let
g
be a local equation of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
. Then

i_{g,+}\big(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{1/gz}\big)=\big(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\big)\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}z}

and
[
Sab09
, Prop. 2.2.5]
shows that the
V^{t^{\prime}}
-filtration is constant. Therefore,

i_{g,+}\big(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{1/gz}\big)[*t^{\prime}]=i_{g,+}\big(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{1/gz}\big)

and thus
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}[*P_{\mathrm{red}}]=\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}
, as wanted. The remaining assertion in (
3
) is then given by Proposition
3.2
.

Let us prove (
1
) and (
2
). If
f
does not extend as a morphism
X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, let
\pi:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
be as in (
2.1
). Set
D^{\prime}=P^{\prime}_{\mathrm{red}}\cup H^{\prime}
. Then, according to
[
Moc11b
, Prop. 11.2.1]
,
\mathscr{E}_{X^{\prime}}^{f^{\prime}/z}[*H^{\prime}]
underlies an object of
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}(X^{\prime})
. According to
[
Moc11b
, Prop. 11.2.6]
, its push-forward
\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\mathscr{E}_{X^{\prime}}^{f^{\prime}/z}[*H^{\prime}]
underlies an object of
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}(X)
. We also have
\mathscr{E}_{X^{\prime}}^{f^{\prime}/z}[*H^{\prime}]=(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}(*D^{\prime})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f^{\prime}/z})[*D^{\prime}]
and we can apply
[
Moc11b
, Lem. 3.3.17]
(because we work with objects of
\MTM(X^{\prime})
) to deduce that

\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\mathscr{E}_{X^{\prime}}^{f^{\prime}/z}[*H^{\prime}]=\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}(*D^{\prime})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f^{\prime}/z})[*P_{\mathrm{red}}].

On the other hand, we have
\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}(\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}(*D^{\prime})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f^{\prime}/z})=\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/z}
. Therefore the latter
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
-module is twistor-specializable along
P_{\mathrm{red}}
and we have
\mathscr{E}_{X}^{f/z}=\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\mathscr{E}_{X^{\prime}}^{f^{\prime}/z}[*H^{\prime}]
. This concludes (
1
) and (
2
).

Lastly, (
4
) follows from
[
Moc11b
, Prop. 11.2.1]
.
∎

The Laplace twist

Let
f:U\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
be as above and let
\tau
be a new variable. We now consider the function
\tau f:U\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
as a meromorphic function on
X\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}
. Proposition
3.3
implies that
\mathscr{E}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}}^{\tau f/z}
exists and underlies an object of
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}(X\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau})
. In
§
9
we will also have to consider another variable
v
and the object
\mathscr{E}_{X}^{\tau vf/z}
of
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}(X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau})
.

Proposition 3.4
\pointrait

If
f:U\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
extends as a morphism
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, then
\mathscr{E}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}}^{\tau f/z}=\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau f/z}
.

Proof.

The question is local near
P_{\mathrm{red}}
and, using the notation as above, we have to prove that
\mathscr{E}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}}^{\tau/gz}=\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau/gz}
. Equivalently, we should prove that the
V^{t^{\prime}}
-filtration of
(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}})(*\{t^{\prime}=0\})\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau/t^{\prime}z}
is constant. This is obtained through the equation
\delta(t^{\prime}-g)\otimes\mathrm{e}^{\tau/t^{\prime}z}=t^{\prime}\eth_{\tau}\delta(t^{\prime}-g)\otimes\mathrm{e}^{\tau/t^{\prime}z}
.
∎

4.
Strictness for exponentially twisted regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules

We will first prove a particular case of Theorem
1.3
. Let
p:X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\lx@nobreakspace X
denote the projection and let
t
be the coordinate on the affine line
\mathbb{C}=\mathbb{P}^{1}\smallsetminus\{\infty\}
. Recall that, for
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})
underlying a mixed Hodge module on
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we have constructed in
[
ESY15
,
§
3.1]
a filtration
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
indexed by
\mathbb{Q}
(see Definition
1.2
for the corresponding Rees construction).

Theorem 4.1
\pointrait

For
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})
underlying a mixed Hodge module, the complex
p_{+}R_{F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{Del}}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
is strict and has nonzero cohomology in degree zero at most.

In the case where
X
is a point, this is the statement of
[
Sab10
, Th. 6.1]
. If
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})=i_{f,+}(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
for some morphism
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
and some
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
underlying a mixed Hodge module on
X
, one can adapt the proof given in
[
ESY15
, Prop. 1.6.9]
for
\EuScript{N}=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)
, where
D
is a normal crossing divisor, and
f^{-1}(\infty)\subset D
, but this case is not enough for our purposes. The proof that we give below uses the full strength of the theory of mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules of T. Mochizuki
[
Moc11b
]
.

Proof of Theorem
4.1
.

We first note that the second assertion in the theorem (i.e., the vanishing of
\mathscr{H}^{j}
for
j\neq 0
) follows from the strictness assertion together with Lemma
2.5
. So let us consider the strictness assertion.

We refer to
[
ESY15
, §§2 & 3]
for the notation and results we use here. Given the filtered
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-module
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})
underlying a mixed Hodge module, we associate to it the Rees module
\mathscr{M}:=R_{F}\EuScript{M}=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{p}F_{p}\EuScript{M}\cdot z^{p}
, which is a graded
R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-module. Its analytification
\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
(with respect to the
z
-variable) is part of the data defining an integrable mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-module, according to
[
Moc11b
, Prop. 12.5.4]
.

Let us consider the graded
R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-module
R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
. Our aim is to prove the strictness (i.e., the absence of
z
-torsion) of the push-forward modules
\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
. Forgetting the grading,
R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
can be obtained by using an explicit expression of the
V
-filtration as in
[
ESY15
, Prop. 3.1.2]
. It is enough to check the strictness property on the corresponding analytic object, by flatness. Now, the analytification
\big(R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})\big)^{\mathrm{an}}
can be obtained by using the analytic
V
-filtration, by making analytic the formula of
[
ESY15
, Prop. 3.1.2]
. We then use that the
V
-filtration behaves well by push-forward for mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules, according to results of
[
Moc11b
]
. This is the main argument for proving Theorem
4.1
.

Let us denote by
\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}
the (stupidly) localized module
\mathscr{M}(*\infty)
and by
{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}
the (not graded)
(R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}})[\tau]\langle\eth_{\tau}\rangle
-module
\widetilde{\mathscr{M}}[\tau]\otimes\mathscr{E}^{t\tau/z}
. By Proposition
3.4
, this is also
\mathscr{M}[\tau]\otimes\mathscr{E}^{t\tau/z}
. Similarly,
({}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M})^{\mathrm{an}}
denotes its analytification with respect to both
\tau
and
z
. We can use Proposition
3.3
together with
[
Moc11b
, Prop. 11.3.4]
to ensure that
({}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M})^{\mathrm{an}}
underlies an integrable mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module.

Let
p:X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}
denote the projection. Then
p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
is strict, each
\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
is strictly specializable along
\tau=0
and the
V^{\tau}
-filtration satisfies
V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}=\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}(V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\tau}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}})
. Indeed, these properties are satisfied according to the main results of
[
Moc11b
]
.

We will now adapt the proof given in
[
ESY15
, §3.2]
, which needs a supplementary argument, since we cannot argue with (3.2.2) in loc. cit.

According to
[
ESY15
, Prop. 3.1.2]
, we have a long exact sequence

\cdots\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}V_{\alpha}^{\tau}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\tau-z\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau-z}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}V_{\alpha}^{\tau}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}(R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}))^{\mathrm{an}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots

that we can thus rewrite as

\cdots\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\tau-z\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau-z}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}\\
\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}(R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}))^{\mathrm{an}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots

(4.2)

Let us first check that
\tau-z
is injective on each
\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
. In the case considered in
[
ESY15
, §3.2]
, we could use (3.2.2) of loc. cit., and when
X
is reduced to a point, the argument in
[
Sab10
]
uses the solution to a Birkhoff problem given by M. Saito. We do not know how to extend the argument of
[
Sab10
]
to the case
\dim X\geqslant 1
.

Lemma 4.3
\pointrait

Let
Y
be a complex manifold,
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
an
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{Y}}
-module which underlies a mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module in the sense of
[
Moc11b
]
. Let
h
be a holomorphic function on
Y
. Then the action of
h-z
is injective on
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
.

Proof.

Since a mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module is in particular an object of the category
\mathrm{MTW}(Y)
(see
[
Moc11b
, §§7.1.1 & 7.2.1]
), a simple extension argument with respect to the weight filtration allows us to reduce to the case where
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
underlies a pure wild twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module (as defined in
[
Moc11a
]
). Since the question is local on
Y
, we fix some
y_{o}\in Y
and work locally near
y_{o}
.

Assume first that
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
underlies a smooth pure twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module. Then it is a locally free
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{Y}}
-module with
z
-connection, and the injectivity of
h-z
is clear.

In general, we know that
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
has a decomposition by the strict support (see
[
Sab05
, §3.5]
,
[
Moc11a
, §22.3.4]
,
[
Sab09
, §1.4]
) and we can therefore assume that, near
y_{o}
,
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
has strict support a germ of an irreducible closed analytic subset
Z\subset Y
at
y_{o}
. On a dense open set
Z^{o}
of the smooth part of
Z
, due by Kashiwara’s equivalence for pure twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules (see loc. cit.), we are reduced to the smooth case considered above and the injectivity holds. Therefore,
\ker[(h-z):\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}]
is supported on a proper closed analytic subset
Z^{\prime}
of
Z
in the neighbourhood of
y_{o}
. Let
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
be a good filtration of
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
as an
(R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{Y})^{\mathrm{an}}
-module (which exists since we work locally on
Y
). Then for each
k
,
\ker[(h-z):F_{k}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F_{k}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}]
is a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{Y\times\mathbb{C}_{z}}
-submodule of
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
supported on
Z^{\prime}
. The
(R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{Y})^{\mathrm{an}}
-submodule that it generates is a coherent
(R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{Y})^{\mathrm{an}}
-submodule of
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
supported on
Z^{\prime}
. It is therefore zero since
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
has strict support equal to
Z
. Since this holds for any
k
, we conclude that
\ker[(h-z):\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}]=0
.
∎

Since
\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
underlies a mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-module, we infer from Lemma
4.3
that
\tau-z
is injective on each
\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
. We conclude that the long exact
sequence
(
4.2
) splits into short exact sequences and therefore
\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}(R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}}\EuScript{M})^{\mathrm{an}}
is identified with
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}/(\tau-z)V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
for each
j
. Proving that the later module is strict is a local question, near points with coordinates
(\tau,z)
in the neighbourhood of
(\tau_{o},z_{o})
with
\tau_{o}=z_{o}
.

(1)

If
\tau_{o}=z_{o}=0
, we use that
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}/\tau V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
is strict, due to the strict specializability of
\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
along
\tau=0
and it is enough to prove that
z
is injective on
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}/(\tau-z)V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
. Due to the strictness above, if a local section
m
of
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
satisfies
zm=\tau m^{\prime}
for some local section
m^{\prime}
of
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
, then there exists a local section
m^{\prime\prime}
of
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
such that
m=\tau m^{\prime\prime}
, and since
\tau
is injective on
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
for
\alpha\in[0,1)
, we have
m^{\prime}=zm^{\prime\prime}
. As a consequence, if a local section
m
of
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
satisfies
zm=(\tau-z)m_{1}
for some local section
m_{1}
of
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
, then there exists a local section
m^{\prime\prime}
of
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
such that
m+m_{1}=\tau m^{\prime\prime}
and
m_{1}=zm^{\prime\prime}
, hence
m=(\tau-z)m^{\prime\prime}
, which gives the desired injectivity.

(2)

We now assume that
\tau_{o}=z_{o}\neq 0
. Near such a point, we have
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}=\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
. Let us remark, however, that the
V
-filtration of
{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
along
\tau-\tau_{o}=0
satisfies
V_{k}^{(\tau-\tau_{o})}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}={}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
for
k\geqslant 0
and
V_{k}^{(\tau-\tau_{o})}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}=(\tau-\tau_{o})^{-k}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
for
k\leqslant 0
, according to
[
Sab06b
, Prop. 4.1(iii)]
. Applying the push-forward argument as above, we conclude that the
V
-filtration of
\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
along
\tau-\tau_{o}=0
satisfies the same property. Therefore, setting
\tau^{\prime}=\tau-\tau_{o}
and
z^{\prime}=z-z_{o}
, we are reduced to proving the injectivity of
z^{\prime}
on
V_{\alpha}^{\tau^{\prime}}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}/(\tau^{\prime}-z^{\prime})V_{\alpha}^{\tau^{\prime}}\mathscr{H}^{j}p_{+}{}^{\mathscr{F}}\!\!\!\!\mathscr{M}^{\mathrm{an}}
. We can then use the same argument as we used for the case
\tau_{o}=0
.
∎

5.
The irregular Hodge filtration

In this section, we come back to the setup of Theorem
1.3
. Let
f
be a meromorphic function on
X
with pole divisor
P
and let
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
be a filtered
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module underlying a mixed Hodge module such that
\EuScript{N}=\EuScript{N}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
. Let
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})
be the mixed Hodge module on
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
associated to
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
by the construction of Remark
2.3
. We know by Theorem
4.1
that the complex
p_{X,+}R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
is strict and has cohomology in degree zero at most, hence
\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{X,+}R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
is equal to the Rees module of
\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f}
with respect to some good filtration, which we precisely define as
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})
.

Definition 5.1
.

The filtration
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{irr}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})
is the filtration obtained by push-forward from
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
.

5.a.
Proof of Theorem
1.3

(1)

This is clear since it already holds for
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})
.

(2)

Because the category of mixed Hodge modules is abelian, we have an exact sequence of filtered
\mathscr{D}
-modules underlying mixed Hodge modules:

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\EuScript{N}_{0},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N}_{0})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\EuScript{N}_{1},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N}_{1})\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\varphi\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}(\EuScript{N}_{2},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N}_{2})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\EuScript{N}_{3},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N}_{3})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

which gives rise to an exact sequence of filtered
\mathscr{D}
-modules underlying mixed Hodge modules:

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\EuScript{M}_{0},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M}_{0})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\EuScript{M}_{1},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M}_{1})\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\varphi\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}(\EuScript{M}_{2},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M}_{2})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\EuScript{M}_{3},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M}_{3})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

and therefore, according to
[
ESY15
, Th. 3.0.1(2)]
, to an exact sequence of filtered
\mathscr{D}
-modules:

0\rightarrow(\EuScript{M}_{0}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t},F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\rightarrow(\EuScript{M}_{1}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t},F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\rightarrow(\EuScript{M}_{2}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t},F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\rightarrow(\EuScript{M}_{3}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t},F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\rightarrow 0.

Applying
\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}
we keep an exact sequence, according to the second statement in Theorem
4.1
.

(3)

We consider the following diagram:

\textstyle{X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{p_{X}}
\textstyle{\pi\times\id_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}}
\textstyle{Y\times\mathbb{P}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{Y}

We thus have

\pi_{+}R_{F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})\simeq(\pi\circ p_{X})_{+}R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})\simeq(p_{Y}\circ(\pi\times\id))_{+}R_{F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}).

On the other hand, according to

, Prop. 3.2.3]
,

is strict and for each

Applying now Theorem
4.1
to
\mathscr{H}^{j}(\pi\times\id)_{+}(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})
we obtain the assertion.

.

(5)

The case

(

) follows from

, Prop. 1.6.12]
. Let us show the case

(

). If
i:\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}
denotes the diagonal inclusion

and
p:\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{s}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}_{t}
denotes the projection (and similarly after taking the product with

), we have an isomorphism

\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}\simeq\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}(i_{+}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}))\simeq\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{s}\big).

We claim that, for each
\alpha\in[0,1)
,

F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{irr}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})=F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t}).

(5.2)

It is enough to check

i_{+}\big(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t},F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t})\big)=\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{s},F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}((i_{+}\EuScript{M})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{s})\big),

(5.3)

and the question is non obvious in the charts
t^{\prime}=1/t
and
s^{\prime}=1/s
. Let us set
\delta=\delta(s^{\prime}-t^{\prime})
. Let us first recall that, by definition,

\displaystyle i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k\geqslant 0}i_{*}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{k}\delta,

\displaystyle F_{p}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k\geqslant 0}i_{*}F_{p-k-1}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{k}\delta,

(5.4)

(the shift by one comes from the left-to-right transformation on
R_{F}\mathscr{D}
-modules) and, concerning the
V
-filtration, one checks that
2
2

2

The formula in the published version is not correct. We thank Takahiro Saito for noticing the mistake

\displaystyle V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{k}(V_{\alpha}^{t^{\prime}}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\delta).

We set
G_{p}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{0\leqslant k\leqslant p}i_{*}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{k}\delta
. Let
\sum_{k=0}^{\ell}\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{k}(m_{\alpha,k}\otimes\delta)\in V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})
. Then this term is contained in
G_{\ell}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})
, and its image in
G_{\ell}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})/G_{\ell-1}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})
is the class of

, hence it is nonzero if and only if

. In particular,
V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\cap G_{0}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})=V_{\alpha}^{t^{\prime}}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\delta
.

We recall (see
[
ESY15
, (3.1.1)]
):

F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{k}t^{\prime-1}\bigl[(F_{p-k}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\cap V_{\alpha}^{t^{\prime}}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}}\bigr],

and, by the analogue of (
