),

i_{+}\big(F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)_{p}=F_{\alpha+p-1}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\otimes\delta+\partial_{s^{\prime}}\big[i_{+}\big(F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)_{p-1}\big].

On the other hand,

\displaystyle F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{k}s^{\prime-1}\bigl[(F_{p-k}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\cap V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}))\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\bigr]

\displaystyle=s^{\prime-1}\big(F_{p}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\cap V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\big)\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 108.12054pt+\partial_{s^{\prime}}F_{\alpha+p-1}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big).

We prove (
5.3
) by induction on
p
. Let
p_{o}
be such that
F_{p_{o}-2}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}=0
. Then
F_{p_{o}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})=F_{p_{o}-1}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\delta\subset G_{0}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})
,
F_{\alpha+p_{o}-1}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big)=0
and

\displaystyle F_{\alpha+p_{o}}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle=s^{\prime-1}\big(F_{p_{o}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\cap V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\big)\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=s^{\prime-1}\big(F_{p_{o}-1}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\cap V_{\alpha}^{t^{\prime}}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\big)\otimes(\delta\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/s^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\big(t^{\prime-1}(F_{p_{o}-1}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\cap V_{\alpha}^{t^{\prime}}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}}\big)\otimes\delta

\displaystyle=i_{+}\big(F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)_{p_{o}}.

We now assume that (
5.3
) holds for
p-1
. Let us first show that

i_{+}\big(F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)_{p}\subset F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big).

By induction and the above formula for
i_{+}\big(F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)_{p}
, it is enough to check

i_{*}F_{\alpha+p-1}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\otimes\delta\subset F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big).

We have

F_{\alpha+p-1}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})=t^{\prime-1}(F_{p-1}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\cap V_{\alpha}^{t^{\prime}}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}}+\partial_{t^{\prime}}\big(F_{\alpha+p-2}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big).

Then on the one hand, by induction,

\displaystyle i_{*}\partial_{t^{\prime}}\big(F_{\alpha+p-2}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\big)\otimes\delta\subset\partial_{t^{\prime}}\big[i_{*}\big(F_{\alpha+p-2}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)\otimes\delta\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 103.85237pt+\partial_{s^{\prime}}\big[i_{*}\big(F_{\alpha+p-2}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)\otimes\delta\big]

\displaystyle\subset\partial_{t^{\prime}}F_{\alpha+p-1}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big)+\partial_{s^{\prime}}F_{\alpha+p-1}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big)

\displaystyle\subset F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big).

On the other hand,
i_{*}\big[t^{\prime-1}(F_{p-1}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\cap V_{\alpha}^{t^{\prime}}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}}\big]\otimes\delta
is the degree zero term (w.r.t.
G_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
) in
F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big)
.

Let us now prove the reverse inclusion

F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big)\subset i_{+}\big(F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)_{p}.

By induction and the above formula for
F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Del}}\big((i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/s^{\prime}}\big)
, it is enough to prove

s^{\prime-1}\big(F_{p}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\cap V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\big)\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/s^{\prime}}\subset i_{+}\big(F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)_{p}.

Let
m=\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{j}(m_{\alpha,j}\otimes\delta)
be in
F_{p}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\cap V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})
. We wish to prove that it belongs to the right-hand side of
(*)
. Assume
m_{\alpha,\ell}\neq 0
, so that
m_{\alpha,\ell}\in F_{p-1-\ell}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\cap V_{\alpha}^{t^{\prime}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime})
. Then
m_{\alpha,\ell}\otimes\delta\in F_{p-\ell}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\cap V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})
and
\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{\ell}(m_{\alpha,j}\otimes\delta)\in F_{p}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\cap V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})
. It follows that each nonzero term in the sum expressing
m
also belongs to
F_{p}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\cap V_{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}(i_{+}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})
, and it is enough to prove the desired inclusion for
m=\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{\ell}(m_{\alpha,\ell}\otimes\delta)
, that is,

\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{\ell}t^{\prime-1}(m_{\alpha,\ell}\otimes\delta\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/s^{\prime}})\in i_{+}\big(F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)_{p}.

The left-hand side of
(**)
reads
\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{\ell}t^{\prime-1}\bigl[(m_{\alpha,\ell}\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}})\otimes\delta\bigr]
and is a sum of terms

\bigl[\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{\ell-j}t^{\prime-1}(m_{\alpha,\ell}\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}})\bigr]\otimes\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{j}\delta,\quad j=0,\dots,\ell.

We have

\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{\ell-j}t^{\prime-1}(m_{\alpha,\ell}\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}})\in\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{\ell-j}t^{\prime-1}(F_{p-1-\ell}\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\cap V_{\alpha}^{t^{\prime}}\EuScript{M}^{\prime})\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}}\subset F_{\alpha+p-1-j}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}}),

and thus

\bigl[\partial_{t^{\prime}}^{\ell-j}t^{\prime-1}(m_{\alpha,\ell}\otimes\mathrm{e}^{1/t^{\prime}})\bigr]\otimes\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{j}\delta\in F_{\alpha+p-1-j}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\otimes\partial_{s^{\prime}}^{j}\delta\subset i_{+}\big(F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}(\EuScript{M}^{\prime}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{1/t^{\prime}})\big)_{p},

as wanted.∎

Remark 5.5
.

Let
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
be a morphism and let
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
underlie a mixed Hodge module. It follows from
1.3
(
4
) and (
5
) that
i_{f,+}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{irr}})=(\EuScript{M}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{t},F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
, if we set as above
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})=i_{f,+}(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
.

5.b.
The irregular Hodge filtration in terms of
V^{\tau}_{\alpha}

With the notation as in the beginning of this section, we consider the pull-back module
\EuScript{N}[\tau]
by the projection
r:X\times\penalty\mathbb{C}_{\tau}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
and the corresponding Rees object
R_{F}\EuScript{N}[\tau]=:\mathscr{N}[\tau]
, where
\mathscr{N}:=R_{F}\EuScript{N}
. Denote by
\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
the analytification of
\mathscr{N}
and by
r^{+}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
that of
\mathscr{N}[\tau]
. We twist
r^{+}\mathscr{N}^{\mathrm{an}}
by
\mathscr{E}^{\tau f/z}
to obtain the object
{}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N}
, which underlies an object of
\MTM^{\mathrm{int}}(X\times\penalty\mathbb{C}_{\tau})
, according to Propositions
3.3
and
3.4
, and to
[
Moc11b
, Prop. 11.3.4 & 12.5.4]
.

Proposition 5.6
\pointrait

We have
R_{F_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{irr}}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})^{\mathrm{an}}=V^{\tau}_{\alpha}({}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N})/(\tau-z)V^{\tau}_{\alpha}({}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N})
.

Proof.

We associate to the mixed Hodge module
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
the mixed Hodge module
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})
as in Remark
2.3
(from which we keep the notation). It follows from (
5.2
) and
[
ESY15
, Prop. 3.1.2]
that the result holds for
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})
on
X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
and for
f
equal to the projection to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
(note that it holds without taking “
\mathrm{an}
”). Applying the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
4.1
, we conclude that the operation
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}/(\tau-z)V_{\alpha}^{\tau}
commutes with
\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}
. On the other hand, by definition and according to (
5.2
), the operation
R_{F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\alpha}}
is compatible with
\mathscr{H}^{0}p_{+}
. Therefore, the result holds for
(\EuScript{N},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{N})
.
∎

Remark 5.7
.

As a consequence, one can recover the graded module
R_{F_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{irr}}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})
from
V^{\tau}_{\alpha}({}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N})
in the following way. As an
\mathscr{O}_{X}[z]
-module, we have an inclusion
R_{F_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{irr}}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})\subset\EuScript{N}[z,z^{-1}]
and
R_{F_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{irr}}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})
is obtained from
R_{F_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{irr}}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})^{\mathrm{an}}
as the graded module with respect to the filtration on
R_{F_{\alpha}^{\mathrm{irr}}}(\EuScript{N}\otimes\EuScript{E}^{f})^{\mathrm{an}}
induced by the
z
-adic filtration of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[z]}\EuScript{N}[z,z^{-1}]
. By the proposition above, it is thus enough to identify the inclusion as
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
-modules

V^{\tau}_{\alpha}({}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N})/(\tau-z)V^{\tau}_{\alpha}({}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N})\subset\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[z]}\EuScript{N}[z,z^{-1}].

(5.7
\,*
)

By using the strict specializability of
{}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N}
along
\tau=0
, one checks as in
[
ESY15
, Proof of Prop. 3.1.2]
that
(\tau-z)V^{\tau}_{\alpha}({}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N})=V^{\tau}_{\alpha}({}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N})\cap(\tau-z){}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N}
, so that

V^{\tau}_{\alpha}({}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N})/(\tau-z)V^{\tau}_{\alpha}({}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N})\subset{}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N}/(\tau-z){}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N}.

Let us set (recall that
\EuScript{N}=\EuScript{N}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{N}}

\displaystyle=(R_{F}\EuScript{N})(*P_{\mathrm{red}})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{p}F_{p}\EuScript{N}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})z^{p}\subset\EuScript{N}[z,z^{-1}],

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathscr{N}}^{\mathrm{an}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[z]}\widetilde{\mathscr{N}}\subset\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[z]}\EuScript{N}[z,z^{-1}].

As an
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}}
-module we have
{}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N}=r^{*}\widetilde{\mathscr{N}}^{\mathrm{an}}
and thus as an
\mathscr{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
-module we have
{}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N}/(\tau-z){}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N}=\widetilde{\mathscr{N}}^{\mathrm{an}}
. This gives the desired inclusion (
5.7

∗

).

Part II

The case of a rescaled meromorphic function

6.
Kontsevich bundles via
\mathscr{D}
-modules

In Part
II
, we use the setting and notation of
§
1.b
. It will also be convenient to work algebraically with respect to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, in which case we will consider the
\mathscr{D}_{X}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle
-module
E^{vf}(*H):=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[v]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
and the
\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
-module
E^{f/u}(*H):=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}
, where
{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
and
{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}
are other notations for
1
which make clear the twist of the connection.

6.a.
The Laplace Gauss-Manin bundles
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\alpha)

The bundles
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\alpha)
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}
will be obtained by gluing bundles on
\mathbb{C}_{v}
and on
\mathbb{C}_{u}
, that we describe below.

Over the chart
\mathbb{C}_{v}

Let us denote by
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
the restriction of
\mathscr{H}^{k}
(see
§
1.c
) to the
v
-chart. This is nothing but the Laplace transform of the
(k-\dim X)
th Gauss-Manin system of
f
. It is known to have a regular singularity at
v=0
and no other singularity at finite distance (as follows by push-forward from the arguments recalled in
§
7.b
, or by a general result about Laplace transform of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules in one variable). Moreover,
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
is equipped with the push-forward filtration
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{irr}}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
by
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-coherent subsheaves, in a strict way according to Theorem
1.3
. On
\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}
we obtain in such a way a flat bundle
(\mathscr{H}^{k}_{|\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}},\nabla)
equipped with a filtration
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{irr}}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{|\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}}
indexed by
\mathbb{Q}
, which satisfies Griffiths transversality condition with respect to
\nabla
(see
§
7.f
, see also Remark
6.3
). This is the variation with respect to
v
of the irregular Hodge filtration of
H^{k}(X,\DR\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*H))
.

We consider the limiting filtration (in the sense of Schmid) when
v\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0
. For
\alpha\in[0,1)
, let us denote by
V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}
the
\alpha
th term of the Kashiwara-Malgrange
filtration
of
\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}
at
v=0
. Equivalently, due to the regularity property of the connection at
v=\penalty 0
,
V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}
is the Deligne extension of
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{|\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}}
on which
\nabla
has a simple pole with residue
\Res_{v=0}\nabla
having eigenvalues in
[-\alpha,-\alpha+1)
. We set
\gr^{V}_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}=V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}/V_{<\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}
.

Theorem 6.1
\pointrait

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
,

(1)

the jumps
\beta\in\mathbb{Q}
of the induced filtration

F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{irr}}\gr^{V}_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}:=\frac{F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{irr}}\mathscr{H}^{k}\cap V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}}{F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{irr}}\mathscr{H}^{k}\cap V_{<\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}}

belong to
\alpha+\mathbb{Z}
,

(2)

on each generalized eigenspace of
\Res_{v=0}\nabla
acting on
V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}/vV_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}
, the nilpotent part of the residue strictly shifts by one the filtration naturally induced by
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\mathrm{irr}}V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}
.

Our proof in
§
7.i
is obtained by showing (Proposition
7.19
) that the conditions needed for applying M. Saito’s criterion
[
Sai88
, Prop. 3.3.17]
are fulfilled. More precisely, we will work with a filtration
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
easy to define, and we postpone to
§
9
the proof that this is indeed the irregular Hodge filtration of
\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
. It would also be possible, as observed by T. Mochizuki
[
Moc15
]
, to directly refer to a similar property for twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules.

Over the chart
\mathbb{C}_{u}

Let us now consider the chart
\mathbb{C}_{u}
. We denote by
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{u}
the restriction of
\mathscr{H}^{k}
to this chart. If
j:\mathbb{C}_{u}\smallsetminus\{0\}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{C}_{u}
denotes the open inclusion, we have
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{u}=j_{+}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{|\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}}
. There is a natural
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{u}}
-lattice
G_{0}\mathscr{H}_{u}^{k}
of the free
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{u}}[u^{-1}]
-module
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{u}
called the Brieskorn lattice in analogy with the construction of Brieskorn in singularity theory
[
Bri70
]
. It can be defined in terms of the Hodge filtration of the Gauss-Manin system attached to
f
(see the appendix). It can also be defined (see
§
8.d
) as the push-forward by
q
in a suitable sense of an
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{u}}(*\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}})
-module
G_{0}\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})
equipped with a
u
-connection
u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f:G_{0}\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G_{0}\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})\otimes\Omega_{X}^{1}
and with a compatible action of
u^{2}\partial_{u}
.

The connection on
\mathscr{H}^{k}
has a pole of order at most two at
u=0
when restricted to
G_{0}\mathscr{H}_{u}^{k}
(see Remark
8.14
). In the context of
\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
-modules
\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})
corresponds to
E^{f/u}(*H)=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}]{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}
.

Gluing

We can then glue
G_{0}\mathscr{H}_{u}^{k}
with
V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}
and obtain an
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-bundle
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\alpha)
with a connection having a pole of order one at
v=0
and of order two at
u=0
.

6.b.
The Kontsevich bundles
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)

We now consider the Kontsevich bundles introduced in
§
1.c
. We can endow them with a natural meromorphic connection having a pole of order one at
v=0
and of order two at most at
v=\infty
, and no other pole.

In order to do so, we start
3
3

3

This was suggested to us by T. Mochizuki.
by considering the morphism of complexes

u^{2}\partial_{u}-f:\big(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f\big)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\big(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha+1)[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f\big).

Lemma 6.2
\pointrait

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
, the natural inclusion of complexes

(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha+1)[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)

is a quasi-isomorphism.

This lemma allows us to define an action of
u^{2}\partial_{u}
on each
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)_{|\mathbb{C}_{u}}
and therefore a meromorphic connection
\nabla
on
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
with a pole of order at most two at
u=0
. We will show that
\nabla
has at most a simple pole at
v=0
.

Remark 6.3

(due to T. Mochizuki)
.

Let
\mathscr{H}
be a vector bundle on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
equipped with a connection
\nabla
having a simple pole at
v=0
and a double pole (at most) at
v=\penalty\infty
. Then the Harder-Narasimhan filtration
F^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{H}
satisfies the Griffiths transversality property with respect to
\nabla
.

Indeed, the property is obviously true with respect to the connection
{\mathrm{d}}
on
\mathscr{H}
coming from
{\mathrm{d}}
on each summand in a Birkhoff-Grothendieck decomposition. We are thus reduced to proving a similar property for the
\mathscr{O}
-linear morphism
\nabla-{\mathrm{d}}
, and the result follows by noticing that
(\mathscr{H}/F^{p-1}\mathscr{H})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\{v=0\}+2\{u=0\})
has slopes
<p
while
F^{p}\mathscr{H}
has slopes
\geqslant p
.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

We will show that the quotient complex has zero cohomology. From
[
ESY15
, Prop. 1.4.2]
we know that the inclusion of complexes

(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha),{\mathrm{d}}f)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha+1),{\mathrm{d}}f)

is a quasi-isomorphism, and thus the quotient complex
(\mathscr{Q}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}},{\mathrm{d}}f)
has zero cohomology. Let
\omega=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\omega_{j}u^{j}
be a local section of
\mathscr{Q}^{p}[u]
such that
(u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)(\omega)=0
. Then
{\mathrm{d}}f\wedge\omega_{0}=0
and therefore there exists
\eta_{0}\in\mathscr{Q}^{p-1}
such that
\omega={\mathrm{d}}f\wedge\eta_{0}
. By replacing
\omega
with
\omega-(u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\eta_{0}
and iterating the process we can assume that
\omega=\omega_{k}u^{k}
and, dividing by
u^{k}
, that
\omega\in\mathscr{Q}^{p}
. It satisfies then
{\mathrm{d}}\omega=0
and
{\mathrm{d}}f\wedge\omega=0
, so
\omega={\mathrm{d}}f\wedge\eta
for some
\eta\in\mathscr{Q}^{p-1}
, and therefore
{\mathrm{d}}f\wedge{\mathrm{d}}\eta=0
. For any representative
\widetilde{\eta}\in\Omega_{f}^{p-1}(\alpha+1)
, we obtain

{\mathrm{d}}f\wedge{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{\eta}\in\Omega_{f}^{p+1}(\alpha)\subset\Omega_{X}^{p+1}(\log D)([\alpha P]).

On the other hand, we note that

\Omega_{f}^{p-1}(\alpha+1)={\mathrm{d}}f\wedge\Omega_{X}^{p-2}(\log D)([\alpha P])+\Omega_{X}^{p-1}(\log D)([\alpha P]),

so we can assume that
\widetilde{\eta}\in\Omega_{X}^{p-1}(\log D)([\alpha P])
. Then
{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{\eta}\in\Omega_{X}^{p}(\log D)([\alpha P])
, and therefore
{\mathrm{d}}\widetilde{\eta}\in\Omega_{f}^{p}(\alpha)
, that is,
{\mathrm{d}}\eta=0
, so
\omega=(u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\eta
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.11
.

We will compare the filtered complex
\sigma^{\geqslant p}(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+\penalty v{\mathrm{d}}f)
with the filtered relative de Rham complex of
\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
with respect to the projection to
\mathbb{C}_{v}
. We introduce in §
7.c
a filtration
F_{\alpha}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
, which will be shown to coincide with
F_{\alpha}^{{\mathrm{irr}},{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
in Theorem
9.1
.

Theorem 6.4

(see §
7.g
, modulo Th.
9.1
)
\pointrait

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism of filtered complexes

(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[v]}\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f,\sigma^{\geqslant p})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}),F_{\alpha}^{{\mathrm{irr}},p})

which is compatible with the meromorphic action of
\nabla_{\partial_{v}}
.

It follows from (
1.9
) that applying
\boldsymbol{R}q_{*}
to the filtered complex on the right-hand side gives a strict complex (i.e., we have a similar injectivity statement).

We apply
R^{k}q_{*}
to the quasi-isomorphism of Theorem
6.4
. The non-filtered statement gives the first point of Theorem
1.11
, since
V_{\alpha}
is compatible with proper push-forward. The second point is then obtained by applying the second point of Theorem
6.1
.
∎

In a way similar to Theorem
6.4
, but algebraically with respect to
u
, we introduce in
§
8.b
a filtration
F_{\alpha}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}G_{0}\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})
, which will be shown to coincide with
F_{\alpha}^{{\mathrm{irr}},{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}G_{0}\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})
in Theorem
9.1
, and we prove:

Theorem 6.5

(see §
8.c
, modulo Th.
9.1
)
\pointrait

There is a natural quasi-isomorphism of filtered complexes

(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f,\sigma^{\geqslant p})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\DR_{X}G_{0}E^{f/u}(*H),F_{\alpha}^{{\mathrm{irr}},p})

which is compatible with the action of
\nabla_{\partial_{u}}
.

As above, it follows from (
1.9
) that applying
\boldsymbol{R}q_{*}
to the filtered complex on the right-hand side gives a strict complex.

By applying a degeneration statement similar to that of
[
Sai88
, Prop. 3.3.17]
proved in the appendix, we obtain a concrete description of the irregular Hodge filtration of
\mathscr{H}^{k}
.

Corollary 6.6
\pointrait

The isomorphism
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}^{k}(\alpha)
obtained by pushing forward the quasi-isomorphisms of Theorems
6.4
and
6.5
identifies the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
\mathscr{K}^{k}(\alpha)
(hence of
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\alpha)
) with the image on
\mathscr{H}^{k}(\alpha)
of the irregular Hodge filtration
F^{\mathrm{irr}}\mathscr{H}^{k}
.

Remark 6.7
.

Another proof of Theorem
1.11
has recently been given by T. Mochizuki
[
Moc15
]
, by showing an analogue of Theorem
6.4
in the framework of mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules, but not referring explicitly to the irregular Hodge filtration.

7.
The
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module
\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})

7.a.
Setting

We will use the local setting and notation similar to that of
[
ESY15
,
§
1.1]
that we recall now, together with the notation introduced in
§
1.b
. In the local analytic setting, the space
X^{\mathrm{an}}
is the product of discs
\Delta^{\ell}\times\Delta^{m}\times\Delta^{m^{\prime}}
with coordinates
(x,y)=(x_{1},\dots,x_{\ell},y_{1},\dots,y_{m},y^{\prime}_{1},\dots,y^{\prime}_{m^{\prime}})
and we are given a multi-integer
{\boldsymbol{e}}=\penalty(e_{1},\dots,e_{\ell})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{>0})^{\ell}
for which we set:

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

f(x,y)=x^{-{\boldsymbol{e}}}:=\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{-e_{i}}
,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

g(x,y)=1/f(x,y)=x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

P_{\mathrm{red}}=\{\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}=0\}
,
H=\{\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{j=1}^{m}y_{j}=0\}
,
D=P_{\mathrm{red}}\cup H
.

Set
\mathscr{O}=\mathbb{C}\{x,y,y^{\prime}\}
and
\mathscr{D}=\mathscr{O}\langle\partial_{x},\partial_{y},\partial_{y^{\prime}}\rangle
is the ring of linear differential operators with coefficients in
\mathscr{O}
, together with its standard increasing filtration
F_{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\mathscr{D}
by the total order w.r.t.
\partial_{x},\partial_{y},\partial_{y^{\prime}}
:

F_{p}\mathscr{D}=\sum_{|\alpha|+|\beta|+|\gamma|\leqslant p}\mathscr{O}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{\beta}\partial_{y^{\prime}}^{\gamma},

where we use the standard multi-index notation with
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}
, etc. Similarly we will denote by
\mathscr{O}[t^{\prime}]
the ring of polynomials in
t^{\prime}
with coefficients in
\mathscr{O}
and by
\mathscr{D}[t^{\prime}]\langle\partial_{t^{\prime}}\rangle
the corresponding ring of differential operators.

Consider the left
\mathscr{D}
-modules

\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})=\mathscr{O}[x^{-1}],\quad\mathscr{O}(*H)=\mathscr{O}[y^{-1}],\quad\mathscr{O}(*D)=\mathscr{O}[x^{-1},y^{-1}]

with their standard left
\mathscr{D}
-module structure. They are generated respectively by
1/\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}
,
1/\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{j=1}^{m}y_{j}
and
1/\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{j=1}^{m}y_{j}
as
\mathscr{D}
-modules. We will consider on these
\mathscr{D}
-modules the increasing filtration
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
defined as the action of
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}
on the generator:

\displaystyle F_{p}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{a}|\leqslant p}\mathscr{O}\cdot\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{a}}(1/\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits\nolimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i})=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{a}|\leqslant p}\mathscr{O}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}-\bf 1},

\displaystyle F_{p}\mathscr{O}(*H)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{c}|\leqslant p}\mathscr{O}\cdot\partial_{y}^{\boldsymbol{c}}(1/\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits\nolimits_{j=1}^{m}y_{j})=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{c}|\leqslant p}\mathscr{O}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-\bf 1},

\displaystyle F_{p}\mathscr{O}(*D)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{a}|+|\boldsymbol{c}|\leqslant p}\mathscr{O}\cdot\partial_{x}^{\boldsymbol{a}}\partial_{y}^{\boldsymbol{c}}(1/\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits\nolimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits\nolimits_{j=1}^{m}y_{j})=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{a}|+|\boldsymbol{c}|\leqslant p}\mathscr{O}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}-\bf 1}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-\bf 1},

so that
F_{p}=0
for
p<0
. These are the “filtrations by the order of the pole” in
[
Del70
, (3.12.1) p. 80]
, taken in an increasing way. Regarding
\mathscr{O}(*H)
as a
\mathscr{D}
-submodule of
\mathscr{O}(*D)
, we have
F_{p}\mathscr{O}(*H)=F_{p}\mathscr{O}(*D)\cap\mathscr{O}(*H)
and similarly for
\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})
. On the other hand it clearly follows from the formulas above that

F_{p}\mathscr{O}(*D)=\sum_{q+q^{\prime}\leqslant p}F_{q}\mathscr{O}(*H)\cdot F_{q^{\prime}}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}}),

where the product is taken in
\mathscr{O}(*D)
.

7.b.
The
V
-filtration of the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module
\EuScript{E}=\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})

On
X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}
we consider the holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module that we denote by
\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
. It is defined by the formula

\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}):=\big(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}(*(D\times\mathbb{C}_{v})),{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}(vf)\big).

For the sake of simplicity, we will set
\EuScript{E}=\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
. Then
\EuScript{E}
has a global section, equal to
1
, that we denote by
{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
on
X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}
. Similarly, we will consider the
v
-algebraic version of the same object, regarded as a
\mathscr{D}_{X}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle
-module:

E=E^{vf}(*H):=(\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}(vf))=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[v]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}.

It is standard that the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module
\EuScript{E}
is holonomic. However, it is not of exponential type as considered in
[
ESY15
]
since
vf
is only a rational function, but is exponentially regular according to Proposition
2.7
(
1
), hence it enters the frame considered in
§
2.b
. It is however known to have regular singularities along
v=0
(in a sense made precise in
[
Sab06b
]
) which has been thoroughly analyzed in
[
Sab97
]
. On the other hand, it is easy to check that
F_{0}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}(*\EuScript{H}){\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
generates
\EuScript{E}
as a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module.

Let us recall the definition of the
V
-filtration (considered increasingly) along
v=\penalty 0
over
\mathbb{C}_{v}
. For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
V_{\alpha+k}\EuScript{E}
is a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module (by
regularity
) equipped with an action of
v\partial_{v}
, and the minimal polynomial of
v\partial_{v}
on
V_{\alpha+k}\EuScript{E}/V_{\alpha+k-1}\EuScript{E}
has roots contained in
[-\alpha-k,-\alpha-k+1)
. We have by definition, for
k\geqslant 1
,

V_{\alpha-k}\EuScript{E}=v^{k}V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E}\quad\text{and}\quad V_{\alpha+k}\EuScript{E}=\begin{cases}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\partial_{v}^{j-1}V_{1}\EuScript{E}&\text{if }\alpha=0,\\[5.0pt]
V_{k}\EuScript{E}+\partial_{v}^{k}V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E}&\text{if }\alpha\in(0,1).\end{cases}

Since
\EuScript{E}[v^{-1}]=j_{0_{+}}j_{0}^{+}\EuScript{E}
is also holonomic, it also has a
V
-filtration. It satisfies, for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,

V_{\alpha-k}(\EuScript{E}[v^{-1}])=v^{k}V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E}.

There is also a notion of
V
-filtration for holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle
-modules and we have
V_{\alpha+k}\EuScript{E}=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[v]}V_{\alpha+k}E
.

Lemma 7.1

(Description of
V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}E
)
\pointrait

Let us fix
\beta\geqslant 0
and
\alpha\in[0,1)
.

(1)

Near a point of
(X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}})
, we have

V_{\alpha+k}E=v^{\max(-k,0)}E\quad\text{for }k\in\mathbb{Z}.

(2)

Near a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, in the local setting of
§
7.a
, we have

V_{\beta}E=\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\big(F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\big)([\beta P])(*H)[v\partial_{v}]x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}P_{\boldsymbol{a},\beta}(v\partial_{v})\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}},

with (convention:
\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{k\in\varnothing}\star_{k}=1
)

P_{\boldsymbol{a},\beta}(s):=\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{k=1}^{a_{i}}\Big(s+\frac{[\beta e_{i}]+k}{e_{i}}\Big).

Proof.

The first point follows from the relation
\partial_{v}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}=f{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
. The second point is a reformulation of
[
Sab97
, Lem. 4.9]
.
∎

For each
\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0
, let us set
I(\boldsymbol{a})=\{i\mid a_{i}=0\}\subset\{1,\dots,\ell\}
and
x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})}=(x_{i})_{i\in I(\boldsymbol{a})}
. For
\lambda\geqslant 0
we also set
P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(s)=(s+\beta)^{\lambda}P_{\boldsymbol{a},\beta}(s)
. Then near a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, each local section of
V_{\beta}E
has a
unique
decomposition

\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\sum_{\lambda\geqslant 0}h_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y,y^{-1},y^{\prime})x^{-[\beta{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v}){\mathrm{e}^{vf}},

(7.2)

with
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y,y^{-1},y^{\prime})\in\mathbb{C}\{x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y,y^{\prime}\}[y^{-1}]
. Moreover (see loc. cit.), a section (
7.2
) belongs to
V_{<\beta}E
if and only if

\forall\,\boldsymbol{a},\lambda\geqslant 0,\quad h_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}\neq 0\mathchoice{\Longrightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}\begin{cases}\lambda\geqslant\#\{i\mid\beta e_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\}&\text{if }\beta>0,\\
\lambda\geqslant\ell+1&\text{if }\beta=0.\end{cases}

(7.3)

Let us make explicit the action of
\mathbb{C}[v]
on a section (
7.2
). For
j\geqslant 1
we have

v^{j}\cdot h_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}x^{-[\beta{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v}){\mathrm{e}^{vf}}=h_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}x^{-[\beta{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}+j{\boldsymbol{e}}}P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v}-j)v^{j}\partial_{v}^{j}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}.

Set
\boldsymbol{a}-j{\boldsymbol{e}}=\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}-\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime\prime}
, with
a^{\prime}_{i}=\max(a_{i}-je_{i},0)
,
a^{\prime\prime}_{i}=\max(je_{i}-a_{i},0)
. The polynomial

P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(s-j)=(s+\beta-j)^{\lambda}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{k=1}^{a_{i}}\big(s+([(\beta-j)e_{i}]+k)/e_{i}\big)

is a multiple of
P_{\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime},0,\beta}(s)
and there is a polynomial
R_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,j,\beta}(s)\in\mathbb{Q}[s]
such that

P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v}-j)v^{j}\partial_{v}^{j}=R_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,j,\beta}(v\partial_{v})P_{\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime},0,\beta}(v\partial_{v})=\sum_{\mu\geqslant 0}c_{\mu}P_{\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime},\mu,\beta}(v\partial_{v})

with
c_{\mu}\in\mathbb{Q}
. We thus obtain

v^{j}\cdot h_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}x^{-[\beta{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v}){\mathrm{e}^{vf}}\\
=\sum_{\mu\geqslant 0}c_{\mu}x^{\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime\prime}}h_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}x^{-[\beta{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}}P_{\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime},\mu,\beta}(v\partial_{v}){\mathrm{e}^{vf}},

(7.4)

and since
I(\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime})=\{i\mid a_{i}-je_{i}\leqslant 0\}\supset I(\boldsymbol{a})
, we obtain the result in the form of (
7.2
).

Lemma 7.5
\pointrait

For any monic polynomial
P(s)
of degree
p
, there exists a monic polynomial
Q(s)
of degree
p
such that
P(v\partial_{v}){\mathrm{e}^{vf}}=Q(v/x^{\boldsymbol{e}}){\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
in
\EuScript{E}
.∎

Let us then denote
Q_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(s)
the polynomial associated with
P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(s)
by Lemma
7.5
. We thus have

V_{\beta}E=\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\sum_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\big(F_{0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\big)([\beta P])(*H)x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}Q_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(vf)\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}},

(7.6)

and we note that
\deg Q_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}=|\boldsymbol{a}|+\lambda
. Moreover, each local section has a unique decomposition

\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\sum_{\lambda\geqslant 0}h_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y,y^{-1},y^{\prime})x^{-[\beta{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}Q_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(vf){\mathrm{e}^{vf}}.

(7.7)

Corollary 7.8
\pointrait

Let us denote by
\gr^{[v]}V_{\beta}E
the grading of
V_{\beta}E
with respect to the degree in
v
. Then, in a neighbourhood of a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, we have

\gr_{p}^{[v]}V_{\beta}E\simeq\big(F_{p}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\big)([(\beta+p)P])(*H)\cdot v^{p}.

7.c.
The filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E

Although the function
vf
does not extend as a map
X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we can nevertheless adapt in a natural way the definition given in
[
ESY15
, (1.6.1) & (1.6.2)]
for the case of the map
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Definition 7.9

(The filtration)
.

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
we set, over
\mathbb{C}_{v}
,

\displaystyle F_{\alpha+p}E^{vf}

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{k\leqslant p}F_{k}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})([(\alpha+p)P])v^{k}\Big)[v]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}},

\displaystyle F_{\alpha+p}E

\displaystyle=\sum_{q+q^{\prime}\leqslant p}F_{q}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H)\cdot F_{\alpha+q^{\prime}}E^{vf}.

The analytification of these filtrations with respect to
v
are denoted
F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{E}^{vf}
and
F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{E}
respectively.

Lemma 7.10
\pointrait

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
, the filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E
is an
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle
-filtration which satisfies the following properties.

(1)

F_{\alpha+p_{1}}E\subset F_{\beta+p_{2}}E
for all
p_{1},p_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\beta\in[0,1)
such that
\alpha+p_{1}\leqslant\beta+p_{2}
. Moreover,
F_{\alpha+p}E=0
for
p<0
.

(2)

When restricted to
\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}
, the filtration
F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{E}
is equal to
F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Del}}\EuScript{E}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}}
as defined in
[
ESY15
, (1.6.2)]
for the map
vf:X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and for each
v_{o}\in\mathbb{C}^{*}
, we have

F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{E}/(v-v_{o})F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{E}=F^{\mathrm{Del}}_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{E}^{v_{o}f}(*H).

(3)

The filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E
satisfies

F_{\alpha+p}E=F_{p}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}\cdot F_{\alpha}E\,;

in particular, it is good with respect to
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle
.

Proof.

The exhaustivity is clear from the expression of Definition
7.9
, and the first two points are straightforward. Let us check (
3
). It is enough to check it locally analytically on
X
.

Near a point of
X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}}

If
H=\varnothing
and
p\geqslant 0
, we have
F_{\alpha+p}E=\mathscr{O}[v]{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
and the generation by
F_{\alpha}E
is clear.

If
H=\{y_{1}\cdots y_{m}=0\}
and
p>0
, we have

F_{\alpha+p}E=\sum_{|\boldsymbol{a}|\leqslant p}y^{-\boldsymbol{a}-\bf 1}\mathscr{O}[v]{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}.

Since
\partial_{y}^{\boldsymbol{a}}(y^{-\bf 1}\mathscr{O}[v]{\mathrm{e}^{vf}})=\star y^{-\boldsymbol{a}-\bf 1}\mathscr{O}[v]{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}\bmod F_{\alpha+p-1}E
if
|\boldsymbol{a}|=p
, we get the generation by
F_{\alpha}E
near such a point.

Near a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}

From the equalities (for some nonzero constants
\star
):

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{i}}\big(x^{-([\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]+\bf 1)}\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}\big)

\displaystyle=\star\,x_{i}^{-1}x^{-([\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]+\bf 1)}\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}+\star\,x_{i}^{-1}x^{-([(\alpha+1){\boldsymbol{e}}]+\bf 1)}v\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\big(x^{-([\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]+\bf 1)}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}\big)

\displaystyle=x^{-([(\alpha+1){\boldsymbol{e}}]+\bf 1)}\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}},

we conclude

F_{1}\mathscr{D}_{X}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle\cdot F_{\alpha}E^{vf}=\big(F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})+F_{1}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})v\big)[v]\cdot x^{-[(\alpha+1){\boldsymbol{e}}]}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}

and by iterating the argument we get the generation property. The corresponding property for
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E
is proved similarly.
∎

We will rely on computations made in
[
Sab97
]
and we will first express differently the filtration
F_{\alpha+p}E
. Let us define
G_{p}E
as the filtration by
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-modules (but not
\mathscr{O}_{X}[v]
-modules), defined as

G_{p}E=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k=0}^{p}(F_{p-k}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}})v^{k}\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}.

The filtration
G_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}E
clearly satisfies

\displaystyle p<0

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Longrightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}G_{p}E=0,

(7.11)

\displaystyle q\geqslant 0

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Longrightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}G_{p}E\cap v^{q}E=v^{q}G_{p-q}E,

\displaystyle p-q<0

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\Longrightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}G_{p}E\cap v^{q}E=0.

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}
, we set

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E:=\sum_{k+j\leqslant p}(G_{k}E\cap V_{\alpha+j}E).

(7.12)

Then
F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E
is an
\mathscr{O}_{X}[v]
-module. Note also that
F^{\prime}_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E
is an
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle
-filtration. Indeed,
G_{k}E
is stable by
\partial_{y^{\prime}},\partial_{v}
, and we have
\partial_{x_{i}},\partial_{y_{j}}G_{k}E\subset G_{k+1}E
; moreover,
V_{\alpha+j}E
is stable by
\partial_{x},\partial_{y},\partial_{y^{\prime}}
, and we have
\partial_{v}V_{\alpha+j}E\subset V_{\alpha+j+1}E
.

Recall that, for
j\geqslant 0
, we have by definition
V_{\alpha-j}E=v^{j}V_{\alpha}E
, so that for
k\geqslant 0
,

v^{j}:G_{k}E\cap V_{\alpha}E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}G_{k+j}E\cap\penalty V_{\alpha-j}E.

(7.13)

Therefore, we can also write

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E:=\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+\sum_{j=1}^{p}(G_{p-j}E\cap V_{\alpha+j}E).

(7.14)

It follows that
F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E=0
for
p<0
(and
\alpha\in[0,1)
).

Lemma 7.15
\pointrait

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}
we have

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E=F_{\alpha+p}E.

Proof.

Let us first consider an analytic neighbourhood of a point of
X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}}
. Due to the relation
\partial_{v}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}=f{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
, we have, near such a point,
V_{\alpha+j}E=v^{\max(-j,0)}E
for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
G_{p}E=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k=0}^{p}F_{k}\mathscr{O}(*H)v^{p-k}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
. Then, near such a point, (
7.14
) reads

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{0}E)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[v]G_{p}E=F_{p}\mathscr{O}(*H)[v]{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}=F_{\alpha+p}E.

We now argue locally at a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
. We refine (
7.2
) in order to take into account the pole order along
H
, so a section of
V_{\beta}E
can be written in a unique way as

\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\sum_{\lambda\geqslant 0}\sum_{\boldsymbol{c}\geqslant 0}h_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{c},\lambda,\beta}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y_{J(\boldsymbol{c})},y^{\prime})x^{-[\beta{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-{\bf 1}}P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v}){\mathrm{e}^{vf}},

(7.16)

with
J(\boldsymbol{c})=\{j\mid c_{j}=0\}
and
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{c},\lambda,\beta}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y_{J(\boldsymbol{c})},y^{\prime})\in\mathbb{C}\{x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y,y^{\prime}\}
. Arguing as in the proof of
[
Sab97
, Lemma 4.11]
, we obtain that, for
\beta\geqslant 0
, a section (
7.16
) belongs to
G_{k}E\cap V_{\beta}E
if and only if the coefficients
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{c},\lambda,\beta}
are zero whenever
\deg P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}+|\boldsymbol{c}|=|\boldsymbol{a}|+|\boldsymbol{c}|+\lambda
is
>k
(note that this condition clearly defines an increasing filtration with respect to
k
).

We will first show that
F_{\alpha}E=F^{\prime}_{\alpha}E
for
\alpha\in[0,1)
. We have

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\alpha}E

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[v](G_{0}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)

\displaystyle F_{\alpha}E

\displaystyle=F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*D)([\alpha P])[v]{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}.

and

Here we are considering the case
k=0
and
\beta=\alpha
. Let us consider a section (
7.16
) in
G_{0}E\cap V_{\alpha}E
. The only possible term occurs for
\boldsymbol{a}=0
,
\lambda=0
and
\boldsymbol{c}=0
, so
G_{0}E\cap V_{\alpha}E=F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*D)([\alpha P]){\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
. Therefore,

F_{\alpha}E:=F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*D)([\alpha P])[v]{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}=\mathbb{C}[v](G_{0}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)=F^{\prime}_{\alpha}E.

(7.17)

Since
F_{\alpha+p}E=F_{p}\mathscr{D}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle\cdot F_{\alpha}E
(Lemma
7.10
(
3
)), and since
F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E
is an
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle
-filtration, it follows that
F_{\alpha+p}E\subset F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E
for all
p
.

Let us now show the reverse inclusion
F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\subset F_{\alpha+p}E
. Let us consider a term in
G_{k}E\cap V_{\alpha+p-k}E
(
0\leqslant k\leqslant p
) of the form

h(x_{I_{\beta}(\boldsymbol{a})},y_{J(\boldsymbol{c})},y^{\prime})x^{-[\beta{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-{\bf 1}}P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v}){\mathrm{e}^{vf}},

with
\beta=\alpha+p-k
,
\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0
,
\lambda+|\boldsymbol{a}|+|\boldsymbol{c}|\leqslant k
. Let us rewrite
P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v})
in terms of the monomials
v^{j}\partial_{v}^{j}
. For
j\leqslant\lambda+|\boldsymbol{a}|\leqslant k-|\boldsymbol{c}|
, the result of the action of
v^{j}\partial_{v}^{j}
on
h(x_{I_{\beta}(\boldsymbol{a})},y_{J(\boldsymbol{c})},y^{\prime})x^{-[\beta{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-{\bf 1}}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
is

h(x_{I_{\beta}(\boldsymbol{a})},y_{J(\boldsymbol{c})},y^{\prime})x^{-[(\beta+j){\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-{\bf 1}}v^{j}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}\\
=h(x_{I_{\beta}(\boldsymbol{a})},y_{J(\boldsymbol{c})},y^{\prime})x^{-[(\alpha+p){\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}+(k-j){\boldsymbol{e}}}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-{\bf 1}}v^{j}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}.

For
\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ell}
, let us set
|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}|_{+}=\sum_{i}\max(0,|a^{\prime}_{i}|)
. Since
|\boldsymbol{a}|_{+}=|\boldsymbol{a}|\leqslant k
, the reverse inclusion follows from the lemma below.
∎

Lemma 7.18
\pointrait

For
\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\ell}
and
k\geqslant 0
, assume that
|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}|_{+}\leqslant k
. Then for
j
such that
0\leqslant j\leqslant k
, we have
|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}-(k-j){\boldsymbol{e}}|_{+}\leqslant j
.

Proof.

We argue by decreasing induction on
j
and the result is true if
j=k
by assumption. We are reduced to proving that, if
|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}|_{+}\geqslant 1
, then
|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{e}}|_{+}\leqslant|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}|_{+}-1
. There exists
i_{o}
such that
a^{\prime}_{i_{o}}\geqslant 1
, so
\max(a^{\prime}_{i_{o}},0)=a^{\prime}_{i_{o}}\geqslant 1
and
\max(a^{\prime}_{i_{o}}-e^{\prime}_{i_{o}},0)\leqslant a^{\prime}_{i_{o}}-1
. Since
\max(a^{\prime}_{i}-e^{\prime}_{i},0)\leqslant\max(a^{\prime}_{i},0)
for any
i
, we get
|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}-{\boldsymbol{e}}|_{+}\leqslant|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}|_{+}-1
, as wanted.
∎

7.d.
Filtration on the nearby cycles of
\EuScript{E}
along
v=0

In this subsection, we analyze the filtration induced by
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{E}
on the nearby cycles of
\EuScript{E}
along
v=0
. Our objective is to show that M. Saito’s criterion
[
Sai88
, Prop. 3.3.17]
applies.

Proposition 7.19
\pointrait

(1)

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
, the filtered module
(\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}),F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}))
is strictly specializable and regular along
v=0
, in the sense of
[
Sai88
, (3.2.1)]
.

(2)

Let
V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
be the
V
-filtration of
\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})
along
v=0
and, for each
\alpha\in[0,1)
, let us set

\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\alpha)}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}):=\gr_{\alpha}^{V}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})=V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})/V_{<\alpha}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}).

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
the jumps of the induced filtration (considered as a filtration indexed by
\mathbb{Q}
)

F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\alpha)}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}):=\frac{F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cap V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})}{F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cap V_{<\alpha}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})}

occur at
\alpha+\mathbb{Z}
at most, and the filtration

F_{p}\psi_{v}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}):=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\alpha\in[0,1)}F_{\alpha+p}\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\alpha)}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})

is (up to a shift by
\dim X-1
on
\psi_{v,\neq 1}\EuScript{E}^{vf}
and by
\dim X
on
\psi_{v,1}\EuScript{E}^{vf}
) the Hodge filtration of a mixed Hodge module.

(3)

If moreover
H=\varnothing
, this mixed Hodge module is polarized by the nilpotent part of the monodromy naturally acting on
\psi_{v}\EuScript{E}^{vf}
, induced by the action of
\exp-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}v\partial_{v}
.

The latter statement means that the weight filtration of the corresponding mixed Hodge module is, up to a shift which depends on whether
\alpha=0
or
\alpha\neq 0
, the monodromy filtration of the nilpotent endomorphism induced by
v\partial_{v}+\alpha
on
\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\alpha)}\EuScript{E}^{vf}
.

Proof of Proposition
7.19
(
2
) and (
3
).

It is enough to work in the algebraic setting with respect to
v
. Recall that we set
E=E^{vf}(*H)
for short and that
F^{\prime}
was defined by (
7.12
). Let
\beta\in\penalty[0,1)
. We claim that

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\cap V_{\beta}E=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p-1}E\cap V_{\beta}E)&\text{if }\beta>\alpha,\\[5.0pt]
v\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p}E\cap V_{\beta}E)&\text{if }\beta\leqslant\alpha.\end{cases}

(7.20)

This implies that

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}\gr_{\beta}^{V}E=\begin{cases}G_{p-1}\gr_{\beta}^{V}E=F^{\prime}_{<\alpha+p}\gr_{\beta}^{V}E&\text{if }\beta>\alpha,\\[5.0pt]
G_{p}\gr_{\beta}^{V}E=F^{\prime}_{\beta+p}\gr_{\beta}^{V}E&\text{if }\beta\leqslant\alpha.\end{cases}

(7.21)

Let us prove (
7.20
). We have
F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\subset V_{\alpha+p}E
and

\displaystyle F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\cap V_{\alpha+p-1}E

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{p-1}(G_{p-\ell}E\cap V_{\alpha+\ell}E)+(G_{0}E\cap V_{\alpha+p-1}E)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{p-1}(G_{p-\ell}E\cap V_{\alpha+\ell}E),

and by decreasing induction one eventually finds

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\cap V_{\alpha+1}E=\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p-1}E\cap V_{\alpha+1}E).

Intersecting now with
V_{\beta}E
gives the first line of (
7.20
). The second one is obtained by showing in the same way

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E=\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)=v\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E).

Lemma
7.15
, together with (
7.21
) and
[
Sab97
, Th. 4.3]
, proves
7.19
(
2
) and (
3
).
∎

Proof of
7.19
(
1
).

Continuing the proof of (
7.20
) gives, for
\beta\in[0,1)
and
\ell\geqslant 1
,

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\cap V_{\beta-\ell}E=\begin{cases}v^{\ell}\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p+\ell-1}E\cap V_{\beta-\ell}E)&\text{if }\beta>\alpha,\\[5.0pt]
v^{\ell+1}\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p+\ell}E\cap V_{\beta-\ell}E)&\text{if }\beta\leqslant\alpha,\end{cases}

which amounts to

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\cap V_{\beta-\ell}E=\begin{cases}v^{\ell}\Big[\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p-1}E\cap V_{\beta}E)\Big]&\text{if }\beta>\alpha,\\[8.0pt]
v^{\ell}\Big[v\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p}E\cap V_{\beta}E)\Big]&\text{if }\beta\leqslant\alpha,\end{cases}

that is, in any case,

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\cap V_{\beta-\ell}E=v^{\ell}(F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\cap V_{\beta}E),

which is
[
Sai88
, (3.2.1.2)]
(up to changing the convention for the
V
-filtration), since
v
acts in an injective way on
V_{\beta}E
.

We now wish to prove that Property (3.2.1.3) of
[
Sai88
]
holds, that is, for each
\beta>0
and for each
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}
, the morphism

\partial_{v}:F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}\gr_{\beta}^{V}E\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p+1}\gr_{\beta+1}^{V}E

is an isomorphism. Assume first that there exists
p_{o}\geqslant 0
such that
\alpha+p_{o}\leqslant\beta<\alpha+p_{o}+1
(otherwise,
0<\beta<\alpha
, a case which will be treated separately). Then a computation similar to that for proving (
7.20
) gives

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}E\cap V_{\beta}E=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{p_{o}}(G_{p-\ell}E\cap V_{\alpha+\ell}E)+(G_{p-p_{o}-1}E\cap V_{\beta}E)\\[-5.0pt]
\hskip 128.0374pt\text{if }\alpha+p_{o}<\beta<\alpha+p_{o}+1,\\[5.0pt]
\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[v](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+\sum_{\ell=1}^{p_{o}}(G_{p-\ell}E\cap V_{\alpha+\ell}E)\quad\text{if }\beta=\alpha+p_{o}.\end{cases}

As a consequence, we find

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}\gr^{V}_{\beta}E=\begin{cases}G_{p-p_{o}-1}\gr^{V}_{\beta}E&\text{if }\alpha+p_{o}<\beta<\alpha+p_{o}+1,\\[5.0pt]
G_{p-p_{o}}\gr^{V}_{\beta}E&\text{if }\beta=\alpha+p_{o}.\end{cases}

If
0<\beta<\alpha
, we also get
F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}\gr^{V}_{\beta}E=G_{p}\gr^{V}_{\beta}E
. So we are reduced to proving that, for any
\beta>0
and any
k
, the morphism

\partial_{v}:G_{k}\gr^{V}_{\beta}E\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G_{k}\gr^{V}_{\beta+1}E

(7.22)

is an isomorphism.

Away from
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, we have
\gr_{\beta}^{V}E=0
for each
\beta>0
, so the assertion is empty. Let us prove the assertion in the neighbourhood of a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
. The left action of
\partial_{v}
on a term of the sum (
7.16
) gives, since
\partial_{v}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}=x^{-{\boldsymbol{e}}}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
and due to standard commutation rules,

P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v}+1)h_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{c},\lambda,\beta}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y_{J(\boldsymbol{c})},y^{\prime})x^{-[(\beta+1){\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-\bf 1}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}.

We have
P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta}(v\partial_{v}+1)=P_{\boldsymbol{a},\lambda,\beta+1}(v\partial_{v})
and we use (
7.3
) for
\beta>0
to conclude that (
7.22
) is an isomorphism.

It remains to be checked that
F^{\prime}_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\gr_{\beta}^{V}E
is a good filtration for any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
. The previous arguments reduces us to checking this for
\beta\in[0,1]
, and we are reduced to proving that, for any such
\beta
,
G_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\gr_{\beta}^{V}E
is a good filtration. This follows from
[
Sab97
, 4.14]
, since this filtration is identified (after grading by a finite filtration) to a filtration which is already known to be good (and which is the Hodge filtration of a mixed Hodge module).
∎

7.e.
Computation of
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E\cap V_{\alpha}E

The previous section shows that, for
\alpha,\beta\in[0,1)
, the computation of
F_{\beta+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E\cap V_{\alpha}E
is interesting mainly when
\beta=\alpha
. Note that
F_{\alpha+p}E=0
for
p<0
and that, away from
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, we have
F_{\alpha+p}E^{vf}\cap\penalty V_{\alpha}E^{vf}=E^{vf}=\mathscr{O}_{X}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
.

Lemma 7.23
\pointrait

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
p\geqslant 0
, we have

\begin{split}\gr_{p}^{F_{\alpha}}V_{\alpha}E&=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k\geqslant 0}\Big(\gr_{p+k}^{G}V_{\alpha-k}E/\gr_{p+k}^{G}V_{\alpha-k-1}E\Big)\\
&\simeq\mathbb{C}[v]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\Big(\gr_{p}^{G}V_{\alpha}E/\gr_{p}^{G}V_{\alpha-1}E\Big).\end{split}

(7.23
\,*
)

Note that, for
p=0
, we have
\gr_{0}^{G}V_{\alpha}E=G_{0}E\cap V_{\alpha}E
.

Proof.

On the one hand, the natural map
G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{p}^{F_{\alpha}}V_{\alpha}E
has kernel equal to
G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E\cap\big(\sum_{k\geqslant 0}(G_{p-1+k}E\cap V_{\alpha-k}E)\big)
, according to (
7.12
). The latter space is contained in
G_{p}E\cap(G_{p-1}E\cap V_{\alpha}E+V_{\alpha-1}E)
, that is, in
(G_{p-1}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha-1}E)
; but clearly the converse inclusion is also true. We thus have an inclusion

\frac{G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E}{(G_{p-1}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)+(G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha-1}E)}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\gr_{p}^{F_{\alpha}}V_{\alpha}E.

(7.24)

On the other hand,

G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E\cap\Big(\sum_{k\geqslant 1}G_{p+k}E\cap V_{\alpha-k}E\Big)\subset G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha-1}E\subset F_{\alpha+p-1}E,

so (
7.24
) is a direct summand in
\gr_{p}^{F_{\alpha}}V_{\alpha}E
, and one can continue to get the first expression in (
7.23

∗

). For the second expression, we use (
7.13
).
∎

Lemma 7.25
\pointrait

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
,
p\geqslant 0
, near a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, the following holds:

(1)

The natural morphism, induced by the inclusion of each summand in
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v]
:

\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0\\
|\boldsymbol{a}|\leqslant p\end{subarray}}\mathscr{O}_{I(\boldsymbol{a})}x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}Q_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(v/x^{\boldsymbol{e}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{p}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}

(7.25
\,*
)

is an isomorphism.

(2)

For each
i=1,\dots,\ell
, the morphism
\partial_{i}:\gr_{p}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{p+1}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}
induced by
-\partial_{x_{i}}/e_{i}
is given by

\partial_{i}h_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y^{\prime})x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}Q_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(v/x^{\boldsymbol{e}})\\
=\begin{cases}h_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y^{\prime})x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}+1_{i})}Q_{\boldsymbol{a}+1_{i},p+1-|\boldsymbol{a}+1_{i}|,\alpha}(v/x^{\boldsymbol{e}})&\text{if }i\notin I(\boldsymbol{a}),\\[5.0pt]
h_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(0_{i},y^{\prime})x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}+1_{i})}Q_{\boldsymbol{a}+1_{i},p+1-|\boldsymbol{a}+1_{i}|,\alpha}(v/x^{\boldsymbol{e}})\\
\hskip 42.67912pt{}+h^{(i)}_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y^{\prime})x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}Q_{\boldsymbol{a},p+1-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(v/x^{\boldsymbol{e}})&\text{if }i\in I(\boldsymbol{a}),\end{cases}

where, for
i\in I(\boldsymbol{a})
, we set

h_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y^{\prime})=h_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(0_{i},y^{\prime})+x_{i}h^{(i)}_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y^{\prime}),

and
0_{i}
means that
x_{i}
is set to be
0
in
x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})}
.

Proof.

The first point follows from
[
Sab97
, Lemma 4.11]
. For the second point we have, modulo
G_{p}E^{vf}\cap\penalty V_{\alpha}E^{vf}
,

\partial_{i}h_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y^{\prime})x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}Q_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(v/x^{\boldsymbol{e}})\\
=h_{\boldsymbol{a},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y^{\prime})x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}+1_{i})}Q_{\boldsymbol{a}+1_{i},p-|\boldsymbol{a}|,\alpha}(v/x^{\boldsymbol{e}}).

However, this is possibly not written in the form above if
i\in I(\boldsymbol{a})
(i.e.,
a_{i}=0
) and we modify this expression as indicated in the statement to obtain, modulo
G_{p}E^{vf}\cap\penalty V_{\alpha}E^{vf}
, the desired formula.
∎

7.f.
The filtered relative de Rham complex and the rescaled Yu complex

We consider the relative de Rham complex
\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}
which is nothing but the complex of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-modules

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Omega^{1}_{X}[v]\otimes\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots

and the action of
\partial_{v}
by
\partial/\partial v+f
induces a
\mathbb{C}[v]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle
-structure on each term compatible with the differentials.

We filter this complex as usual by subcomplexes of
\mathbb{C}[v]
-modules:

F_{\alpha+p}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}=\{F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Omega^{1}_{X}[v]\otimes F_{\alpha+p+1}\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots\}.

As usual we set
F_{\alpha}^{p}=F_{\alpha-p}
. The action of
\partial_{v}
on
\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}
induces a morphism

\partial_{v}:F_{\alpha+p}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F_{\alpha+p+1}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}.

We will use the following notation, as in
[
ESY15
]
:

\Omega^{k}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}(\log\EuScript{D})([(\alpha+j)\EuScript{P}])_{+}=\begin{cases}0&\text{if }j<0,\\
\Omega^{k}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}(\log\EuScript{D})([(\alpha+j)\EuScript{P}])&\text{if }j\geqslant 0.\end{cases}

We define the
rescaled Yu complex
as being the filtered complex (
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}
):

F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Yu}}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}\\
:=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}([(\alpha+p)\EuScript{P}])_{+}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Omega^{1}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}(\log\EuScript{D})([(\alpha+p+1)\EuScript{P}])_{+}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots

which is a complex of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-modules. The connection
\partial/\partial v+f
induces a morphism
\nabla_{\partial_{v}}:F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Yu}}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F_{\alpha+p+1}^{\mathrm{Yu}}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}
.

Proposition 7.26
\pointrait

The natural morphism

F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{Yu}}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F_{\alpha+p}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E}

is a quasi-isomorphism for each
\alpha\!\in\![0,1)
and
p\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}
compatible with the action of
\partial_{v}
.

Proof.

The existence of a natural morphism follows from Lemma
7.10
. The compatibility with respect to the action of
\partial_{v}
is then clear. The proof is then similar to that of
[
ESY15
, Prop. 1.7.4]
. We note that, away from
\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}}
, we use that the morphism

(\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}(\log\EuScript{H}),{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\Omega^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}(*\EuScript{H}),{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f),

(7.27)

is a filtered quasi-isomorphism. Here the analytic version of
\EuScript{E}
is needed in order to write
{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f=e^{-vf}\circ{\mathrm{d}}\circ e^{vf}
.
∎

7.g.
Proof of Theorem
6.4

We consider the complex
(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)
. We have a natural connection

\nabla_{\partial_{v}}:(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha+1)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)

induced by the action of
f+\partial/\partial v
on each term of the complex.

Lemma 7.28
\pointrait

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
, there is a natural filtered morphism

\big(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[v]}\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f,\sigma^{\geqslant p}\big)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E},F_{\alpha}^{p}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E})

which makes the following diagram commutative:

\textstyle{\big(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[v]}\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\nabla_{\partial_{v}}}
\textstyle{\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\nabla_{\partial_{v}}}
\textstyle{\big(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}[v]}\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(\alpha+1)[v],{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f\big)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}V_{\alpha+1}\EuScript{E}}

Proof.

Once the morphism is defined, the commutativity of the diagram is straightforward: let
{\mathrm{d}}
denote the differential with respect to
X
and
{\mathrm{d}}_{v}
that with respect to
v
; the verification reduces to checking that
e^{-vf}\circ{\mathrm{d}}\circ e^{vf}
commutes with
e^{-vf}\circ{\mathrm{d}}_{v}\circ e^{vf}
, a statement which follow from the commutation of
{\mathrm{d}}
with
{\mathrm{d}}_{v}
.

Away from
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, the morphism is given by (
7.27
). At a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, we will use the algebraic version
E
of
\EuScript{E}
for simplicity. For each
k\geqslant 0
, we have a natural inclusion

(F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\big)([\alpha P])(*H)v^{k}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}\subset V_{\alpha}E.

Indeed, it is enough to prove the inclusion

x^{k{\boldsymbol{e}}}(F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\big)([\alpha P])(*H)\cdot(v/x^{\boldsymbol{e}})^{k}{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}\subset V_{\alpha}E

and then the inclusion

x^{k{\boldsymbol{e}}}(F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\big)([\alpha P])(*H)Q_{{\bf 0},j,\alpha}(v/x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}})\subset V_{\alpha}E\quad\text{with }P_{{\bf 0},j,\alpha}(s)=(s+\alpha)^{j},

by expressing
(v/x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}})^{k}
in terms of the
Q_{{\bf 0},j,\alpha}(v/x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}})
with
j\leqslant k
. The assertion is then clear by taking the term with
\boldsymbol{a}=0
in the expression of Lemma
7.1
. We thus obtain the desired morphism.

In order to prove that it is filtered, we note that for each
k\geqslant 0
, the natural inclusion morphism
\Omega_{f}^{k}(\alpha)[v]\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{k}_{X}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}E
factorizes through the subsheaf
\Omega^{k}_{X}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}F_{\alpha}V_{\alpha}E
. Indeed, according to (
7.17
), we have
F_{\alpha}V_{\alpha}E=F_{\alpha}E=F_{0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)([\alpha P])[v]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{vf}}
, so we are reduced to proving the inclusion
\Omega_{f}^{k}(\alpha)\subset\Omega_{X}^{k}\otimes F_{0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)([\alpha P])
. This is clear away from
P_{\mathrm{red}}
since this reduces to
\Omega_{X}^{k}(\log H)\subset\Omega_{X}^{k}\otimes F_{0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H)
. In the local setting of §
7.a
near a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, the conclusion follows from
[
ESY15
, Formula (1.3.1)]
for
\alpha=0
, and the same formula multiplied by
x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]}
if
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
∎

We will show Theorem
6.4
with the filtration
F_{\alpha}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
introduced in Definition
7.9
. That this is the filtration
F_{\alpha}^{{\mathrm{irr}},{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
will be shown in Theorem
9.1
. Near a point of
(X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}})\times\mathbb{C}_{v}
we can write
{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f=e^{-vf}\circ{\mathrm{d}}\circ e^{vf}
to reduce the statement to the standard result proved by Deligne
[
Del70
]
.

We will thus focus on
P_{\mathrm{red}}\times\mathbb{C}_{v}
, and it will be enough to consider the
v
-algebraic version of the statement. It is also enough to prove that for each
p\geqslant 0
, the
p
th graded morphism is a quasi-isomorphism. We are thus lead to proving that for
p\geqslant 0
the following vertical morphism is a quasi-isomorphism:

\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 104.9412pt\raise-32.40059pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern (7.29)

Since the question is local, we can treat separately the variables
x
and
y
, and the main problem remains the case of the
x
variables, so that we will assume
H=\varnothing
. We will use the computations of
§
7.e
, from which we keep the notation.

Lemma 7.30
\pointrait

Near a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\alpha\in[0,1)
, the relative de Rham complex

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{q}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{q+1}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}\otimes\Omega^{1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{q+n}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}\otimes\Omega^{n}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

has zero cohomology in degrees
\geqslant-q+1
(recall that
\gr_{q}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}=0
for
q<0
).

Sketch of proof.

We will forget the variables
y^{\prime}
and work with the variables
x\in\mathbb{C}^{\ell}
, so we will replace
n
with
\ell
in the de Rham complex above. We then note that this complex is the simple complex associated with the hypercubical complex built on the cube in
\mathbb{R}^{\ell}
with vertices
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{\ell}
, whose vertex at
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
is
\gr_{q+|\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}|}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}
and whose arrows
(\varepsilon_{i}=0)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\varepsilon_{i}=1)
are the derivatives
\partial_{x_{i}}
. We may as well replace the arrow
\partial_{x_{i}}
with
\partial_{i}=-\partial_{x_{i}}/e_{i}
.

The formula of Lemma
7.25
(
2
) shows that, if
\varepsilon_{i}=0
, the arrow
\partial_{i}:\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}+1_{i}
is injective, with cokernel identified with

\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}\geqslant 0,\,a^{\prime}_{i}=0\\
|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}|=q+1\end{subarray}}\mathscr{O}_{I(\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime})}x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}}Q_{\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime},0,\alpha}(v/x^{e}).

We use the convention that a sum indexed by the empty set is zero, a case which occurs if
q+1<0
.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

If
\ell=1
, we only need to consider the case
q\geqslant 0
. The cokernel of
\partial_{1}
is then equal to zero, showing that
\partial_{1}
is bijective in this case, which implies the desired assertion.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

If
\ell\geqslant 2
, we replace (with a shift) the hypercubical
\ell
-complex with the
(\ell-\penalty 1)
-complex made of the cokernels of the injective arrows
\partial_{1}
, and the formula for the induced arrows
\partial_{2},\dots,\partial_{\ell}
is then that of the case
i\notin I(\boldsymbol{a})
in the formula of Lemma
7.25
(
2
). Now, the maps induced by
\partial_{2}
are injective, with cokernel

\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime\prime}\geqslant 0,\,a^{\prime\prime}_{1}=a^{\prime\prime}_{2}=0\\
|\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime\prime}|=q+2\end{subarray}}\mathscr{O}_{I(\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime\prime})}x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime\prime}}Q_{\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime\prime},0,\alpha}(v/x^{e}),\quad\text{etc.}\qed

From Lemma
7.30
we conclude that for
\alpha\in[0,1)
and each
p\geqslant 0
, the de Rham complex

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{0}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}\otimes\Omega^{p}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{1}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}\otimes\Omega^{p+1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots

has zero cohomology in degrees
\geqslant p+1
, while the de Rham complex

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0=\gr_{0}^{G}V_{\alpha-1}E^{vf}\otimes\Omega^{p}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\gr_{1}^{G}V_{\alpha-1}E^{vf}\otimes\Omega^{p+1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots

has zero cohomology in degrees
\geqslant p+2
since
\gr_{k}^{G}V_{\alpha-1}E^{vf}\simeq\gr_{k-1}^{G}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}
, according to (
7.13
). Therefore, the quotient complex
\eqref{eq:grFVOmega}_{\alpha}/\eqref{eq:grFVOmega}_{\alpha-1}
has zero cohomology in degrees
\geqslant p+1
. It follows then from Lemma
7.23
that the bottom line of (
7.29
) has zero cohomology in degrees
\geqslant p+1
. It remains to identify the degree
p
cohomology of this bottom line. As noted above, we have

F_{\alpha}V_{\alpha}E^{vf}=F_{\alpha}E^{vf}=F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})([\alpha P])[v]{\mathrm{e}^{vf}},

so the cohomology consists of sections of
F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})([\alpha P])[v]\otimes\Omega^{p}
whose image by
{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f
belong to
F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})([\alpha P])[v]\otimes\Omega^{p+1}
. This cohomology is then contained in
\Omega^{p}(\log P_{\mathrm{red}})([\alpha P])[v]
, according to Lemma
7.32
below, and it is then easy to identify it with
\Omega^{p}_{f}(\alpha)[v]
.∎

Lemma 7.32
\pointrait

For
k\geqslant 0
, a section of
F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\Omega^{k}
belongs to
\Omega^{k}(\log P_{\mathrm{red}})
if and only if its exterior product by
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}e_{i}{\mathrm{d}}x_{i}/x_{i}
belongs to
F_{0}\mathscr{O}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\Omega^{k+1}
.∎

7.h.
Some properties of the filtration
F_{\alpha}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}

Recall that the
\mathscr{D}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
is defined in
§
1.b
. For
\alpha\in[0,1)
, we denote by
V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
the free
\mathbb{C}[v]
-lattice of
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
on which the connection
\nabla
induced by the
\mathscr{D}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module structure has a simple pole, with residue as in Theorem
1.11
(
1
). This is also the part of indices in
[0,1)
of the Kashiwara-Malgrange
V
-filtration of
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
, which exists since it is a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module.

By a standard result on the strictness of the Kashiwara-Malgrange
V
-filtration with respect to proper push-forward, we have:

V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}=\image\Big[R^{k}q_{+}(\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}V_{\alpha}\EuScript{E})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R^{k}q_{+}(\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}/\mathbb{C}_{v}}\EuScript{E})\Big]

and the latter morphism is injective. We obtain, as a consequence of Proposition
7.19
:

Corollary 7.33

(of
[
Sai88
, Prop. 3.3.17]
)
\pointrait

For each
k,\alpha,p
,
F_{\alpha}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
satisfies the properties
[
Sai88
, (3.2.1)]
.∎

Let us consider the restriction
j^{*}F_{\alpha}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{H}_{v}^{k}
(
j:\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{C}_{v}
).

Corollary 7.34
\pointrait

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have an isomorphism of
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}}
-modules:

j^{*}F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}F_{\alpha}^{{\mathrm{Yu}},p}H^{k}_{\mathrm{dR}}(U,\nabla).

In particular,
j^{*}F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}=\mathscr{H}^{k}_{|\mathbb{C}_{v}^{*}}
for
p\leqslant 0
and
j^{*}F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}=0
for
p>k
.

Proof.

The first part follows from Lemma
7.10
(
2
) and the second part follows from the property
\gr_{F_{\alpha}^{{\mathrm{Yu}}}}^{p}H^{k}_{\mathrm{dR}}(U,\nabla)=0
for
p\notin[0,k]
, which is a consequence of
[
ESY15
, Cor. 1.5.6]
.
∎

Recall that the irregular Hodge numbers
h_{\alpha}^{p,q}(f)
are defined by (
1.6
). As a consequence of Corollary
7.34
we have

h_{\alpha}^{p,q}(f)=\rk\gr_{F_{\alpha}}^{p}\!\!j_{*}\mathscr{H}^{p+q}_{v}.

Corollary 7.35
\pointrait

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
, we have
F_{\alpha}^{0}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\supset V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
.

Proof.

We have seen that both
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-modules coincide with
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}[v^{-1}]
after tensoring with
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}[v^{-1}]
(by Corollary
7.34
for the first one, and by a standard property of the
V
-filtration for the second one). Hence for any
