m\in V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
there exists
\ell\geqslant 0
such that
v^{\ell}m\in F_{\alpha}^{0}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
. Let
p\in\mathbb{Z}
be such that
m\in F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
. Corollary
7.33
implies that Property
[
Sai88
, (3.2.1.1)]
holds for the filtration
F_{\alpha}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
, and thus the morphism
v^{\ell}:(F_{\alpha}^{q}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(F_{\alpha}^{q}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha-\ell}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v})
is an isomorphism for each
q
. It follows that

m\in F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\text{ and }v^{\ell}m\in F_{\alpha}^{0}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha-\ell}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\Longrightarrow m\in F_{\alpha}^{0}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v},

as was to be proved.
∎

7.i.
Nearby cycles and the monodromy filtration

We now consider the functor
\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\beta)}
(
\beta\in[0,1)
). The result of
[
Sai88
, Prop. 3.3.17]
implies then that, for each
\beta\in[0,1)
, the filtration naturally induced by the
\mathbb{Q}
-indexed filtration
F^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
on
\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\beta)}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
is equal to

F^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}(X,\DR\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\beta)}\EuScript{E}):=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}(X,F^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\DR\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\beta)}\EuScript{E})

(7.36)

and therefore has jumps at
\beta+\mathbb{Z}
at most. It is then enough to consider the filtration induced by
F_{\beta}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
on
\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\beta)}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
. Then, according to the previous results, we have

F_{\beta}^{p}\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\beta)}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}=\begin{cases}\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\beta)}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}&\text{if }p\leqslant 0,\\
0&\text{if }p>k.\end{cases}

Definition 7.37
.

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
k\geqslant 0
, the
spectral multiplicity function
is the function

\mathbb{Z}\ni p\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\mu_{\alpha}^{k}(p):=\dim\gr^{p}_{F_{\alpha}}\psi_{v}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}:=\sum_{\beta\in[0,1)}\dim\gr^{p}_{F_{\alpha}}\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\beta)}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}.

Lemma 7.38
\pointrait

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\mu_{\alpha}^{k}(p)=h_{\alpha}^{p,k-p}.

In particular,
\mu_{\alpha}^{k}(p)=0
for
p\notin[0,k]
.

Proof.

For
\beta\in[0,1)
, we have an isomorphism (see Corollary
7.33
):

v:(F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}\cap V_{\beta}\mathscr{H}^{k})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}(F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}\cap V_{\beta-1}\mathscr{H}^{k}).

(7.39)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\beta\in[0,1)}\dim F_{\alpha}^{p}\psi_{v,\exp(-2\pi{\mathrm{i}}\beta)}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\beta\in[0,1)}\dim F_{\alpha}^{p}\gr_{\beta}^{V}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}=\sum_{\beta\in(\alpha-1,\alpha]}\dim F_{\alpha}^{p}\gr_{\beta}^{V}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}

\displaystyle=\dim\frac{F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}}{F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha-1}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}}

\displaystyle=\dim\frac{F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}}{v(F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v})}\quad\text{(Corollary \ref{cor:propertiesF})}.

Since
V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
is
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-free for
\alpha\in[0,1)
, the
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-module
F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}
is
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-torsionfree, hence
\mathscr{O}_{\mathbb{C}_{v}}
-free, and the latter term is equal to
\rk(F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v}\cap V_{\alpha}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v})
, hence to
\rk(F_{\alpha}^{p}\mathscr{H}^{k}_{v})[v^{-1}]
, that is,
\dim F_{\alpha}^{{\mathrm{Yu}},p}H^{k}_{\mathrm{dR}}(U,\nabla)
, according to Corollary
7.34
. The result follows from
[
ESY15
, Cor. 1.4.8]
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

By Lemma
7.15
and (
7.21
), we can apply
[
Sab97
, Th. 5.3]
to the filtration given by (
7.36
). It remains to identify the latter with the irregular Hodge filtration. This follows from Theorem
9.1
below.
∎

8.
The
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{u}}
-module
\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})

We now focus on the
u
-chart. In this section, we will consider the
\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
-module
E^{f/u}(*H):=(\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}],{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}(f/u))
and we use the identification

E^{f/u}(*H)=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}},

which makes clear the twist of the
\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
-structure. We will denote for short
E=E^{f/u}(*H)
.

8.a.
The Brieskorn lattice of the
\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
-module
E^{f/u}(*H)

Let
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X}
denote the filtration of
\mathscr{D}_{X}
by the order of differential operators, and consider the Rees ring
R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{X}:=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k}F_{k}\mathscr{D}_{X}\cdot u^{k}
, which can be expressed in local coordinates as
\mathscr{O}_{X}[u]\langle u\partial_{x},u\partial_{y},u\partial_{y^{\prime}}\rangle
. It will be useful to extend it by adding the action of
u^{2}\partial u
. We obtain in this way a sheaf of rings
R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{X}\langle u^{2}\partial_{u}\rangle
, that we will denote by
G_{0}\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
. It is naturally filtered by the order with respect to the partials, a filtration that we denote by
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}G_{0}\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
.

Remark 8.1
.

The Rees construction is the same as that used in §
3
. However the notation for the extra variable used here is not the same as in §
3
since it will not play the same role. We will use both in §
9
.

The
Brieskorn lattice

G_{0}E
defined in
[
Sab99
,
§
1]
is the
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u]
-module

G_{0}E:=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{j}(F_{j}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\cdot u^{j}{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}},

(8.2)

and we set, for each
p\in\mathbb{Z}
,
G_{p}E=u^{-p}G_{0}E
. Then
G_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}E
is an increasing filtration of
E
indexed by
\mathbb{Z}
. Note that, if
\bigtriangledown
denotes the relative connection on
E
induced by the
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module structure, then
G_{0}E
is preserved by
u\bigtriangledown
. It is also preserved by the action of
u^{2}\partial_{u}
. In other words,
G_{0}E
is a
G_{0}\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
-module. For example, if
H=\varnothing
, we have

G_{0}E^{f/u}=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}.

Using the Rees module notation, we can also write

\big(G_{0}E,u\nabla)=\big((R_{F}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}}),u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f\big).

8.b.
The filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}G_{0}E^{f/u}(*H)

Although the function
f/u
does not extend as a map
X\times\mathbb{C}_{u}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we can nevertheless adapt in a natural way the definition given in
[
ESY15
, (1.6.1) & (1.6.2)]
for the case of the map
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Definition 8.3

(The filtration)
.

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
we set

\displaystyle F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E^{f/u}

\displaystyle=F_{p}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\big([(\alpha+p)P]\big)[u]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}},

\displaystyle F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E

\displaystyle=\sum_{q+q^{\prime}\leqslant p}u^{q}F_{q}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H)\cdot F_{\alpha+q^{\prime}}G_{0}E^{f/u}.

Lemma 8.4
\pointrait

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
, the filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}G_{0}E
is an
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}G_{0}\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
-filtration which satisfies the following properties.

(1)

F_{\alpha+p_{1}}G_{0}E\subset F_{\beta+p_{2}}G_{0}E
for all
p_{1},p_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\beta\in[0,1)
such that
\alpha+p_{1}\leqslant\beta+p_{2}
. Moreover,
F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E=0
for
p<0
.

(2)

The filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}G_{0}E
satisfies

F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E=F_{p}G_{0}\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle\cdot F_{\alpha}G_{0}E\,;

in particular, it is good with respect to
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}G_{0}\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
.

Proof.

Similar to that of Lemma
7.10
.
∎

We will give an expression of
F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E
in terms of the
V
-filtration. We set
G_{p}E=u^{-p}G_{0}E
and we identify
E[u^{-1}]
with
E^{vf}(*H)[v^{-1}]
, so that we can define the filtration
V_{\alpha+k}(E[u^{-1}])
as being the filtration
V_{\alpha+k}(E^{vf}(*H)[v^{-1}])
considered in
§
7.b
. Note that, since
v
is invertible on
E^{vf}(*H)[v^{-1}]
and since
V_{\alpha}(E^{vf}(*H)[v^{-1}])=V_{\alpha}(E^{vf}(*H))
for
\alpha\in[0,1)
, we have

V_{\alpha+k}(E[u^{-1}])=V_{\alpha+k}(E^{vf}(*H)[v^{-1}])=v^{-k}V_{\alpha}(E^{vf}(*H))=u^{k}V_{\alpha}E.

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
, we set

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E:=u^{p}\mathbb{C}[u](G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E)=\mathbb{C}[u](G_{0}E\cap u^{p}V_{\alpha}E),

(8.5)

where the intersection is taken in
E[u^{-1}]
. This is an
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(G_{0}\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle)
-filtration since
u\partial_{x_{i}}
sends
V_{\alpha}E
to
uV_{\alpha}E
, and so does
u^{2}\partial_{u}=-\partial_{v}
.

Lemma 8.6
\pointrait

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
p\in\mathbb{Z}
we have

F^{\prime}_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E=F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E.

Proof.

It will be similar to that of Lemma
7.15
. In the neighbourhood of a point of
X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}}
, Definition
8.3
gives
F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E=\sum_{q=0}^{p}u^{q}F_{q}\mathscr{O}(*H)[u]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}
, while a computation similar to that at the beginning of the proof of Lemma
7.15
gives
G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{0\leqslant q\leqslant p}F_{q}\mathscr{O}(*H)u^{q-p}\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}
, hence the result by multiplying the latter term by
u^{p}\mathbb{C}[u]
.

In the neighbourhood of a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, the inclusion
\supset
is proved exactly as in Lemma
7.15
. For the inclusion
\subset
, we use (
7.16
) with
\beta=\alpha
. Using similarly
v^{j}\partial_{v}^{j}
instead of
(v\partial_{v})^{j}
, and replacing
v^{j}
with
u^{-j}
, a term of
G_{p}E\cap V_{\alpha}E
in the sum (
7.16
) can be written as

h_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{c},\lambda,j,\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y_{J(\boldsymbol{c})},y^{\prime})x^{-[(\alpha+j){\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-\bf 1}u^{-j}{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}},

with
j\leqslant q^{\prime}:=\lambda+|\boldsymbol{a}|
and
q:=|\boldsymbol{c}|\leqslant p-q^{\prime}
. Note that
p-q-j\geqslant 0
. So each term in
u^{p}(G_{p}\cap V_{\alpha})
is a sum of terms

h_{\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{c},\lambda,j,\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},y_{J(\boldsymbol{c})},y^{\prime})\cdot x^{-[(\alpha+q^{\prime}){\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}-(q^{\prime}-j){\boldsymbol{e}})}u^{p-q-j}\cdot(u^{q}y^{-\boldsymbol{c}-\bf 1}){\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}

which all belong to
F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E
(Definition
8.3
), since
\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0
,
|\boldsymbol{a}|\leqslant q^{\prime}
hence, according to Lemma
7.18
,
|\boldsymbol{a}-(q^{\prime}-j){\boldsymbol{e}}|_{+}\leqslant j\leqslant q^{\prime}
for any
j
such that
0\leqslant j\leqslant q^{\prime}
.
∎

Remark 8.7
.

We conclude from the lemma that the filtration
F_{\alpha+p}\gr_{0}^{G}E
induced by
F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E
is nothing but the filtration induced by
u^{p}V_{\alpha}(E[u^{-1}])=V_{\alpha+p}(E[u^{-1}])
. Indeed, recalling that
uG_{0}=G_{-1}
, we have

\frac{\mathbb{C}[u](G_{0}E\cap u^{p}V_{\alpha}(E[u^{-1}]))}{uG_{0}E\cap\big(\mathbb{C}[u](G_{0}E\cap u^{p}V_{\alpha}(E[u^{-1}]))\big)}\\
=\frac{G_{0}E\cap u^{p}V_{\alpha}(E[u^{-1}])}{(uG_{0}E\cap u^{p}V_{\alpha}(E[u^{-1}]))+\big[G_{0}E\cap u^{p}V_{\alpha}(E[u^{-1}])\cap u\mathbb{C}[u](G_{0}E\cap u^{p}V_{\alpha}(E[u^{-1}]))\big]}\\
=\frac{G_{0}E\cap u^{p}V_{\alpha}(E[u^{-1}])}{G_{-1}E\cap u^{p}V_{\alpha}(E[u^{-1}])}

It follows that

\frac{F_{\alpha+p}\gr_{0}^{G}E}{F_{<\alpha+p}\gr_{0}^{G}E}\underset{\sim}{\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0ptu^{-p}\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u^{-p}}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}}\gr_{\alpha}^{V}\gr_{p}^{G}E=\gr_{p}^{G}\gr_{\alpha}^{V}E^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}),

and we conclude that
\gr_{\alpha+p}^{F}\gr_{0}^{G}E
can be computed from data in the
v
-chart.

8.c.
Proof of Theorem
6.5

We have

\DR_{X}E=\Big\{0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}]\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\\[-5.0pt]
\cdots\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Omega^{n}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}]\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0\Big\}.

It will be convenient to use the complex

\EuScript{DR}_{X}E:=\Big\{0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}]\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0ptu{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\\[-5.0pt]
\cdots\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0ptu{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Omega^{n}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}]\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0\Big\}.

Both complexes are obviously isomorphic by multiplying the
k
th term of the first one by
u^{k}
, a morphism that we denote by
u^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
.

The subcomplex
\DR_{X}G_{0}E
of
\DR_{X}E
is defined by

\DR_{X}G_{0}E:=\Big\{0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(R_{F}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\\
\cdots\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Omega^{n}_{X}\otimes(u^{-n}R_{F}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0\Big\}.

(8.8)

Similarly, the subcomplex
\EuScript{DR}_{X}G_{0}E
of
\EuScript{DR}_{X}E
is defined by

\EuScript{DR}_{X}G_{0}E:=\Big\{0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(R_{F}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0ptu{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\\
\cdots\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0ptu{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Omega^{n}_{X}\otimes(R_{F}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0\Big\}.

(8.9)

For example, if
H=\varnothing
, we obtain the complexes

\Big\{0\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u]\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\cdots\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}u^{-n}\Omega^{n}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u]\rightarrow 0\Big\}\\
\overset{\;\,\textstyle u^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}{\simeq}\Big\{0\rightarrow\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u]\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0ptu{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\cdots\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0ptu{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Omega^{n}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u]\rightarrow 0\Big\}.

The relative de Rham complex
\EuScript{DR}_{X}G_{0}E
is naturally filtered by

F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{DR}_{X}G_{0}E:=\Big\{0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{X}\otimes F_{\alpha+p+1}G_{0}\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots\Big\}.

(8.10)

The proof of Theorem
6.5
is obtained by adapting the proofs of
[
ESY15
, Cor. 1.4.5 & Prop. 1.7.4]
to the present situation. We add the parameter
u
and we consider the
u
-connection
u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f
. The natural inclusion morphism
\Omega_{f}^{k}(\alpha)[u]\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{k}_{X}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}E
factorizes through
\Omega^{k}_{X}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}F_{\alpha}G_{0}E
since
F_{\alpha}G_{0}E=F_{0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)([\alpha P])[u]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}
, and this shows that the filtered morphism of Theorem
6.5
is well-defined. To prove that it is a filtered quasi-isomorphism, we note that, for the analogue of
[
ESY15
, Prop. 1.7.4]
the ultimate step of the proof, after grading the complexes, is the same as in loc. cit., since the graded differential is
{\mathrm{d}}\log x^{-{\boldsymbol{e}}}
in both cases. Similarly, the arguments of
[
Yu14
]
used in the proof of
[
ESY15
, Prop. 1.4.2 & Cor. 1.4.5]
reduce the problem to proving a quasi-isomorphism with a graded differential which does not depend on
u
.∎

8.d.
Push-forward of the Brieskorn lattice

Let us consider the push-forward
\mathscr{H}^{k}_{u}
as obtained in the chart
\mathbb{C}_{u}
, that is,

\mathscr{H}^{k}_{u}=R^{k}q_{*}\DR_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{u}/\mathbb{C}_{u}}(\EuScript{E})\overset{\,\textstyle u^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}{\simeq}R^{k}q_{*}\EuScript{DR}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{u}/\mathbb{C}_{u}}(\EuScript{E}).

We set
\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}:=\Gamma(\mathbb{C}_{u},\mathscr{H}^{k}_{u})
, so that the above isomorphism becomes

\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}\simeq\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{X}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(*D)[u,u^{-1}],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)

(8.11)

We obviously have
\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}[u^{-1}]=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{v}[v^{-1}]
, and it is a free
\mathbb{C}[u,u^{-1}]
-module with connection.

Let us consider the
\mathbb{C}[u]
-module

\begin{split}G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}&=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{X}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\!\!(u^{-{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}R_{F}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}}),{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u)\big)\\
&\simeq\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{X}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\!\!(R_{F}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}}),u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\big)\\
&=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,\EuScript{DR}_{X\times\mathbb{C}_{u}/\mathbb{C}_{u}}(G_{0}\EuScript{E})\big)\quad\text{according to \eqref{eq:ccDRG0}.}\end{split}

(8.12)

For example, if
H=\varnothing
, we have

\displaystyle G_{0}\EuScript{E}^{f/u}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}},

\displaystyle G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(u^{-{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}\Omega_{X}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u],{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u)\big)

and

\displaystyle\simeq\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega_{X}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\big).

According to
[
Sai90
]
, we can apply the proposition in
[
Sab99
,
§
1]
to
\EuScript{E}[v^{-1}]
and get:

Proposition 8.13
\pointrait

For each
k
,
G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}
is a free
\mathbb{C}[u]
-module, hence is a
\mathbb{C}[u]
-lattice of
\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}
, and we have
\mathbb{C}[u,u^{-1}]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[u]}\penalty G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}\simeq\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{v}[v^{-1}]
by the isomorphism (
8.11
).∎

Remark 8.14

(Stability under
u^{2}\partial_{u}
)
.

The natural action of
u^{2}\partial_{u}
on
G_{0}E
induces an action on
u^{-k}\Omega_{X}^{k}\otimes G_{0}E
which defines an action of
u^{2}\partial_{u}
on the complex
\DR_{X}(G_{0}E)
, hence on its cohomology
G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}
(equivalently, a shifted action by
u^{2}\partial_{u}-ku
on
\Omega_{X}^{k}\otimes\penalty G_{0}E
, hence on
\EuScript{DR}_{X}(G_{0}X)
and on its cohomology). In other words, the action of
\partial_{u}
on
\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}
has a pole of order at most two when restricted to
G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}
.

For each
k
, we have a natural morphism (see (
8.10
))

\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,F_{\alpha+p}\EuScript{DR}_{X}G_{0}E\big)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,\EuScript{DR}_{X}G_{0}E\big)=:G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u},

(8.15)

whose image is denoted by
F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}
. The source of this morphism is a
\mathbb{C}[u]
-module of finite type because
q
is proper and the terms of the complex (
8.10
) are
\mathscr{O}_{X}[u]
-coherent. As already mentioned after Theorem
6.5
, (
8.15
) is injective for each
k
. The filtered
G_{0}\mathbb{C}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
-module
(G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u},F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u})
is the
(k-\dim X)
th push-forward of the filtered
G_{0}\mathscr{D}_{X}[u]\langle\partial_{u}\rangle
-module
(E,F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E)
.

8.e.
The case of cohomologically tame functions on affine varieties

In this subsection we use the Zariski topology on
U,X
and
X\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{u}
. We still denote by
\EuScript{E}
the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{u}}
-module
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{u}}(*\EuScript{D})\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}
and we make the abuse of identifying it with
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}]\cdot{\mathrm{e}^{f/u}}
(where
X
has its Zariski topology).

Assume that
U
is affine and that
f:U\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{A}^{\!1}
is a cohomologically tame function, in the sense of
[
Sab06a
,
§
8]
(see also
[
Kat90
, Prop. 14.13.3(2)]
for a weaker condition). In particular,
f
has only isolated critical points. Then
H^{k}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(U,{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)=0
unless
k=n:=\dim X
, and
\dim H^{n}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(U,{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)
is equal to the sum of the Milnor numbers at the critical points. The Brieskorn lattice
G_{0}(f)
is defined as
G_{0}(f):=\Omega^{n}(U)[u]\big/(u{\mathrm{d}}+\penalty{\mathrm{d}}f)\Omega^{n-1}(U)[u]
.

Proposition 8.16
\pointrait

Under this tameness assumption on
f
, the natural morphism of complexes

\boldsymbol{R}q_{*}\EuScript{DR}_{X\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{u}/\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{u}}(G_{0}\EuScript{E})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\boldsymbol{R}q_{*}(\Omega^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{U\times\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{u}/\mathbb{A}^{\!1}_{u}},u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)=(\Omega^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(U)[u],u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)

is a quasi-isomorphism, from which one deduces, through
\boldsymbol{H}^{n}(u^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
, an equality
G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}=u^{-n}G_{0}(f)
in

\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}\simeq\Omega^{n}(U)[u,u^{-1}]\big/(u{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\Omega^{n-1}(U)[u,u^{-1}].

Proof.

The natural morphism is induced by

(F_{k}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(F_{k}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*D)=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)=j_{*}\mathscr{O}_{U}

(where
j_{*}
is taken here in the Zariski topology). Through this morphism,
\boldsymbol{H}^{k}(u^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
corresponds to
u^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
termwise on the right-hand complex. Since
H^{k}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(U,{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)=0
unless
k=\dim X=n
, we also have
\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}=0
unless
k=n
, and since the
k
th cohomology of the left-hand complex is contained in
\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}
, we conclude that the left-hand complex has cohomology in degree
n
at most. We therefore obtain a morphism

G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}u^{-n}G_{0}(f),

(8.17)

whose localization with respect to
u
is an isomorphism, because
R_{F}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H)[u^{-1}]=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H)[u,u^{-1}]
and thus
(R_{F}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*H))(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u^{-1}]=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}]
, hence

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{H}^{k}_{u}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(X,(\Omega^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{X}(*D)[u,u^{-1}],{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u)\big)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{H}^{k}\big(U,(\Omega^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{U}[u,u^{-1}],{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u)\big)

\displaystyle=H^{k}(\Omega^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(U)[u,u^{-1}],{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f/u)\quad\text{($U$ affine).}

Both terms of (
8.17
) are
\mathbb{C}[u]
free of the same rank, hence the morphism (
8.17
) is injective, and we may regard it as an inclusion in
\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}
through the previous identification. The conclusion follows from the lemma below.
∎

Lemma 8.18
\pointrait

The morphism (
8.17
) is an isomorphism, in other words,
G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}=u^{-n}G_{0}(f)
in
\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}
.

Sketch of proof.

We will see in §
A.e
that the Brieskorn lattice
G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}
is identified with the Brieskorn lattice attached to the filtered
\mathscr{D}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
-module underlying the mixed Hodge module associated with
\mathscr{H}^{0}f_{+}\mathscr{O}_{U}
. On the other hand, it is shown in
[
Sab08
, §4.c]
that
G_{0}(f)
is identified to the Brieskorn lattice of the Hodge filtration of
\mathscr{H}^{0}f_{+}\mathscr{O}_{U}
shifted by
n
, which leads to the result.
∎

Recall (see
[
Sab06a
]
) that the
spectrum of
f
at infinity
is defined as the set of pairs
(\gamma,\delta_{\gamma})
, with
\gamma\in\mathbb{Q}
and
\delta_{\gamma}=\dim\gr_{\gamma}^{V}G_{0}(f)
. It is known (see loc. cit.) that
\delta_{\gamma}=0
unless
\gamma\in[0,n]
and that
\delta_{\gamma}=\delta_{n-\gamma}
(i.e., the spectrum is symmetric with respect to
n/2
).

Corollary 8.19
\pointrait

Under the previous assumptions, let us set
\gamma=\alpha+q
, with
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then we have

\delta_{\gamma}=\mu_{\alpha}^{n}(n-q)=h_{\alpha}^{n-q,q}=\dim\gr^{n-\gamma}_{F_{\mathrm{Yu}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}H^{n}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(U,{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f).

Proof.

We have isomorphisms

\gr_{\gamma}^{V}G_{0}(f)\xrightarrow[\sim]{\textstyle~u^{-n}~}\gr_{\gamma-n}^{V}G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}=\gr_{\alpha+q-n}^{V}G_{0}\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}\xrightarrow[\sim]{\textstyle~u^{n-q}~}\gr_{\alpha}^{V}G^{n-q}\boldsymbol{H}^{n}_{u}.\qed

Remarks 8.20
.

(1)

The duality
\delta_{\gamma}=\delta_{n-\gamma}
implies, together with the general duality statement of
[
Yu14
, Th. 2.2]
, that, if
U
is affine and
f
is cohomologically tame, we have

\dim\gr^{\lambda}_{F_{\mathrm{Yu}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}H^{n}_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(U,{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)=\dim\gr^{\lambda}_{F_{\mathrm{Yu}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}H^{n}_{{\mathrm{dR}},\mathrm{c}}(U,{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)\quad\forall\,\lambda.

(2)

Assume that
U=(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{n}
with coordinates
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
and that
f
is a convenient and non-degenerate Laurent polynomial (in the sense of Kouchnirenko
[
Kou76
]
). Then it is known that
f
is cohomologically tame. Moreover,

\displaystyle G_{0}(f)/uG_{0}(f)

\displaystyle=\Omega^{n}(U)\big/{\mathrm{d}}f\wedge\Omega^{n-1}(U)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{C}[x_{1}^{\pm 1},\dots,x_{n}^{\pm 1}]\big/(x_{1}\partial f/\partial x_{1},\dots,x_{n}\partial f/\partial x_{n})=\mathbb{C}[x^{\pm 1}]/J(f),

where the isomorphism is obtained by dividing by
{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}/x_{1}\!\wedge\!\cdots\!\wedge\!{\mathrm{d}}x_{n}/x_{n}
, and the filtration
V_{\gamma}\big(G_{0}(f)/uG_{0}(f)\big)
is identified with the Newton filtration
N_{\gamma}(\mathbb{C}[x^{\pm 1}]/J(f))
(see
[
DS03
, Th. 4.5]
). Therefore,

\dim\gr_{F_{\mathrm{Yu}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}^{\lambda}H_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(U,{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)=\dim\gr_{F_{\mathrm{Yu}}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}^{n-\lambda}H_{{\mathrm{dR}}}(U,{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}f)=\dim\gr_{\lambda}^{N}(\mathbb{C}[x^{\pm 1}]/J(f)).

(3)

Let
Y
be a toric Fano manifold.
4
4

4

We thank É. Mann, Th. Reichelt and Ch. Sevenheck for providing us with the necessary arguments.
Mirror symmetry associates with it a convenient and non-degenerate Laurent polynomial
f
, and the cohomology
H^{*}(Y,\mathbb{C})
is identified with
\mathbb{C}[x^{\pm 1}]/J(f)
graded by the Newton filtration (see
[
BCS05
]
). Since the cohomology is generated by divisor classes (see e.g.
[
Ful93
, §5.2]
), it is of Hodge-Tate type and the Hodge filtration reduces to the filtration by the degree of the cohomology. It follows from the previous results that the Hodge numbers of
Y
coincide with the irregular Hodge numbers associated to
f
. Such a mirror correspondence was one of the motivations of Kontsevich to introduce the complexes
(\Omega_{f}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}},u{\mathrm{d}}+v{\mathrm{d}}f)
.

9.
Relation with the irregular Hodge filtration of
\EuScript{E}^{(v{:}u)f}(*\EuScript{H})

In this section, we set
\EuScript{E}:=\EuScript{E}^{(v{:}u)f}(*\EuScript{H})
. We will compare the filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{E}
with the irregular Hodge filtration
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{E}
as defined in
§
5
, namely, we consider the case where
\EuScript{N}=\mathscr{O}_{\EuScript}{X}(*\EuScript{D})
(notation of
§
7.a
) with its differential
{\mathrm{d}}
twisted by the exponential of the rational function
vf:X\times\mathbb{P}^{1}_{v}=\EuScript{X}\mathrel{\text{- -}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{P}^{1}
. The module
{}^{\mathscr{F}_{\!\!f}}\!\!\mathscr{N}
considered in
§
5.b
is obtained here by gluing
\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}[*\EuScript{H}]
(notation of Proposition
3.3
) in the
v
-chart with
\mathscr{E}^{\tau f/uz}[*\EuScript{H}]
in the
u
-chart, and we will regard these modules algebraically with respect to
\tau
,
(v{:}u)
and
z
. We will use the notation introduced in
§
3
.

Theorem 9.1
\pointrait

For each
\alpha\in[0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H})

\displaystyle=F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{E}^{vf}(*\EuScript{H}),

\displaystyle F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}G_{0}\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})

\displaystyle=F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H})\cap G_{0}\EuScript{E}^{f/u}(*\EuScript{H}).

The proof of the theorem will be done in various steps. For the sake of simplicity, we will only treat the case where
H=\varnothing
.

{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
Firstly, one identifies
\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}
as a submodule of
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z]\cdot\penalty\mathrm{e}^{\tau vf/z}
and
\mathscr{E}^{\tau f/uz}
as a submodule of
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u,u^{-1},\tau,z]\cdot\penalty\mathrm{e}^{\tau f/uz}
. According to Proposition
3.4
, we may have a strict inclusion only near points of
P_{\mathrm{red}}\times\{v=0\}
and points of
\{f=0\}\times\{u=0\}
. For the latter set, the computation is much simplified because we only consider the intersection with
G_{0}\EuScript{E}
. For the former set, we will need explicit computations of the
V
-filtration entering the very definition of
\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}
in Proposition
3.3
.

{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
Secondly, one computes the terms
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}
(
resp.

V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau f/uz}
) of the
V
-filtration relative to
\tau=0
, in order to apply Proposition
5.6
. We will work analytically with respect to the variables of
X
and algebraically with respect to
\tau
,
(u:v)
and
z
.

9.a.
Computation in the
v
-chart

We use the algebraic version (with respect to
v,\tau,z
)
R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[v,\tau]\langle\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)
of
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}\times\mathbb{C}_{v}\times\mathbb{C}_{\tau}}
. Recall that
\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}
is a coherent
R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[v,\tau]\langle\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)
-submodule of
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau]\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau vf/z}
. We will set
\mathbf{e}=\mathrm{e}^{\tau vf/z}
.

Computation away from
P_{\mathrm{red}}

Since
\tau vf
is holomorphic, we have the equality
\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}=\mathscr{O}_{X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}}}[v,\tau]\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau vf/z}
. Then, from the relation
\eth_{\tau}\mathbf{e}=vf\mathbf{e}
, we conclude that
\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}
is already
(R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{X}[v,\tau]\langle\partial_{v}\rangle)
-coherent, and hence the
V^{\tau}
-filtration is given by
V_{k}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}=\tau^{\max(-k,0)}\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}
: by uniqueness of the
V^{\tau}
-filtration, it is enough to check the strictness of the
\gr_{k}^{V^{\tau}}\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}
, which is clear. Therefore, only
\alpha=0
is
relevant
. In particular,
V_{0}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}=\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}
. Hence, the quotient modulo
(\tau-z)\mathscr{E}^{\tau vf/z}
is equal to
E^{vf}[z]
.

On the other hand, we have
F_{\alpha+p}E^{vf}=E^{vf}
for any
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
p\geqslant 0
, and
F_{\alpha-1}E^{vf}=0
, that is,
R_{F}E^{vf}=E^{vf}[z]
.

Computation in a neighbourhood of
P_{\mathrm{red}}

Near a point of
P_{\mathrm{red}}
, let us set
g=1/f
, which is holomorphic in a neighbourhood of this point. In local coordinates we have
g=x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}
.

First step: computation of
\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}

By the very definition of Proposition
3.3
(
1
) we have, on this neighbourhood,
\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}=(\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[\tau,v,z]\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/gz})[*P_{\mathrm{red}}]
. Let
i_{g}:X\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}X\times\mathbb{C}_{t^{\prime}}
denote the graph inclusion of
g
and let
p:X\times\mathbb{C}_{t^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
denote the projection. Then, by definition of
[*P_{\mathrm{red}}]
,
i_{g,+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
is the
R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[t^{\prime},v,\tau]\langle\partial_{t^{\prime}},\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)
-submodule of
(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z])\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}
generated by
V_{1}^{t^{\prime}}
.

Lemma 9.2
\pointrait

The submodule
\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
is generated by
x^{-\bf 1}\mathbf{e}
as an
R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[v,\tau]\langle\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)
-module. Moreover,

\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}\cap(\tau-z)\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z]\cdot\mathbf{e}=(\tau-z)\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}.

(9.2
\,*
)

Proof.

Our first task is to compute the
V^{t^{\prime}}
-filtration of
(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z])\cdot\penalty\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k\geqslant 0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z](\eth_{t^{\prime}}^{k}\delta)\otimes\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}
. For
\alpha\in[0,1)
, let us set

\displaystyle(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle:=x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}\delta\otimes\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}

\displaystyle(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}

\displaystyle:=x^{-\lceil\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}\rceil}\delta\otimes\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}.

Then
(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}
satisfies the following equations:

\left\{\begin{aligned} \eth_{v}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}&=\frac{\tau}{g}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}\\
\eth_{\tau}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}&=\frac{v}{g}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}\\
\eth_{t^{\prime}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}&=x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-\bf 1}(\eth_{t^{\prime}}\delta)\otimes\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}-\frac{\tau v}{g^{2}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}\\
\eth_{x_{i}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}&=-\frac{e_{i}}{x_{i}}\Big(\eth_{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime}+\frac{(1+[\alpha e_{i}])z}{e_{i}}+t^{\prime}\eth_{\tau}\eth_{v}\Big)(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}.\end{aligned}\right.

(9.3)

As a consequence we have

t^{\prime}\eth_{x}^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}=(-{\boldsymbol{e}})^{\boldsymbol{e}}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{j=1}^{e_{i}}\Big(t^{\prime}\eth_{t^{\prime}}+\frac{(j+[\alpha e_{i}])z}{e_{i}}+t^{\prime}\eth_{\tau}\eth_{v}\Big)(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}.

Similarly for
(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
the last line of (
9.3
) reads

\eth_{x_{i}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}=-\frac{e_{i}}{x_{i}}\Big(\eth_{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime}+\frac{\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil z}{e_{i}}+t^{\prime}\eth_{\tau}\eth_{v}\Big)(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}

and we have

t^{\prime}\eth_{x}^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}=(-{\boldsymbol{e}})^{\boldsymbol{e}}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{j=0}^{e_{i}-1}\Big(t^{\prime}\eth_{t^{\prime}}+\frac{(j+\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil)z}{e_{i}}+t^{\prime}\eth_{\tau}\eth_{v}\Big)(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}.

We then easily deduce a Bernstein relation for
(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}
and for
(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
, showing that
(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}
belongs to
V^{t^{\prime}}_{1+\alpha}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{X}[v,\tau,z])\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}
and
(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
to
V^{t^{\prime}}_{<1+\alpha}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{X}[v,\tau,z])\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}
. We will now give an explicit expression of these modules.

We have

\displaystyle(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{X}[v,\tau,

\displaystyle z])\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k\geqslant 0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z](\eth_{t^{\prime}}^{k}\delta)\otimes\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k\geqslant 0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z]\eth_{t^{\prime}}^{k}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}\quad\text{(third line of \eqref{eq:xdeltae})}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k\geqslant 0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z]\eth_{t^{\prime}}^{k}t^{\prime k}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}\quad\text{($t^{\prime}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha})=g(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha})$}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k\geqslant 0}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},z](\eth_{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime})^{k}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}\ \text{(setting $v^{\prime}=v/g$, $\tau^{\prime}=\tau/g$)}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z]\quad\text{(setting $\eta=\eth_{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime}$, and $(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}1$)}.

We have a similar identification by using
(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
. Let us write the last line of (
9.3
) as

(\eth_{x_{i}}+e_{i}x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}-{\bf 1}_{i}}\eth_{v}\eth_{\tau})(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}=-\frac{e_{i}}{x_{i}}\Big(\eth_{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime}+\frac{(1+[\alpha e_{i}])z}{e_{i}}\Big)(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}.

For
\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}
and
\alpha\in[0,1)
, let us set (with the convention that a product indexed by the empty set is equal to one):

\begin{split}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(s,z)&=\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{j=1}^{a_{i}}\Big(s+\frac{(j+[\alpha e_{i}])z}{e_{i}}\Big),\\
p_{\boldsymbol{a},<\alpha}(s,z)&=\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{j=0}^{a_{i}-1}\Big(s+\frac{(j+\lceil\alpha e_{i}\rceil)z}{e_{i}}\Big).\end{split}

(9.4)

We then have

\displaystyle(\eth_{x_{i}}+e_{i}x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}-{\bf 1}_{i}}\eth_{v}\eth_{\tau})^{\boldsymbol{a}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle=(-{\boldsymbol{e}})^{\boldsymbol{a}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\eth_{t}^{\prime}t^{\prime},z)(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle(\eth_{x_{i}}+e_{i}x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}-{\bf 1}_{i}}\eth_{v}\eth_{\tau})^{\boldsymbol{a}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}

\displaystyle=(-{\boldsymbol{e}})^{\boldsymbol{a}}x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}p_{\boldsymbol{a},<\alpha}(\eth_{t}^{\prime}t^{\prime},z)(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}.

Let us set

\displaystyle U_{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\mathscr{O}_{X}[v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z]x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\eta,z)\subset\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z]

\displaystyle U_{<1+\alpha}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\mathscr{O}_{X}[v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z]x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}p_{\boldsymbol{a},<\alpha}(\eta,z)\subset\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z].

We thus have isomorphisms, by sending
\eta
to
\partial_{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle U_{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\textstyle~{}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}~}V^{t^{\prime}}_{0}R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[t^{\prime},v,\tau]\langle\partial_{t^{\prime}},\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle U_{<1+\alpha}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow[\sim]{\textstyle~{}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}~}V^{t^{\prime}}_{0}R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[t^{\prime},v,\tau]\langle\partial_{t^{\prime}},\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}

If we set
I(\boldsymbol{a})=\{i\mid a_{i}=0\}
for
\boldsymbol{a}\in\mathbb{N}^{\ell}
and if
I(\boldsymbol{a})^{c}=\{i\mid a_{i}\geqslant 1\}
denotes its complement in
\{1,\dots,\ell\}
, then every element in
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z]
can be written in a unique way as

\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\widetilde{h}_{\boldsymbol{a}}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}

(9.5)

with
\widetilde{h}_{\boldsymbol{a}}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)\in\mathbb{C}\{x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})}\}[v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z]
. Since
p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}
divides
p_{\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime},\alpha}
if
\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}\geqslant\boldsymbol{a}
, we deduce that each element of
U_{1+\alpha}
can be written as

\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\eta,z),

(9.6)

and the coefficient
\widetilde{h}_{\boldsymbol{a}}
of
x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}
in its decomposition (
9.5
) is
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\eta,z)
. By uniqueness, we conclude that an element written as (
9.5
) belongs to
U_{1+\alpha}
if and only if
p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\eta,z)
divides
\widetilde{h}_{\boldsymbol{a}}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)
. In particular, the decomposition (
9.6
) is unique.

We wish to identify
U_{1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}
with
V^{t^{\prime}}_{1+\alpha}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z])\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}
and
U_{<1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
with
V^{t^{\prime}}_{<1+\alpha}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z])\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}
. It is enough to check

(t^{\prime}\eth_{t^{\prime}}+(1+\alpha)z)^{m}U_{1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}\subset U_{<1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}\quad\text{for $m$ big enough}

and

U_{1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}/U_{<1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}\quad\text{has no $z$-torsion}

(see
[
Sab05
, Lem. 3.3.4 & §3.4.a]
). For the first point, we set

I_{\alpha}=\{i\mid\alpha e_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}\}\quad\text{and, for $\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0$,}\quad I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{a})=I_{\alpha}\cap I(\boldsymbol{a})\text{ and }I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{a})^{c}=I_{\alpha}\cap I(\boldsymbol{a})^{c}.

Then
(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}=x^{-{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
, and we have the relation

\displaystyle\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i\in I_{\alpha}}(\eth_{x_{i}}-e_{i}x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}-{\bf 1}_{i}}\eth_{v}\eth_{\tau})\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}

\displaystyle=(-{\boldsymbol{e}})^{{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}}(\eth_{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime}+\alpha z)^{\#I_{\alpha}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}

\displaystyle=(-{\boldsymbol{e}})^{{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}}\big(t^{\prime}\eth_{t^{\prime}}+(1+\alpha)z\big)^{\#I_{\alpha}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}.

For the torsion-free assertion, let us consider a section (
9.6
) of
U_{1+\alpha}
and let us decompose (in a unique way)
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)
as

h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{a})}}h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a}+{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)x^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},

where
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is holomorphic in its
x
-variables and polynomial in
v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z
. Then the decomposition (
9.6
) reads

\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{a})}}h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a}+{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\eta,z).

We now note that, for
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{a})}
, setting
\boldsymbol{b}=\boldsymbol{a}+{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
, we have

p_{\boldsymbol{b},<\alpha}(\eta,z)=(\eta+\alpha z)^{\#I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{b})^{c}}\cdot p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\eta,z).

The unique decomposition (
9.6
) can thus also be written uniquely as

\sum_{\boldsymbol{b}\geqslant 0}h^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{b},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{b})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z)x^{-\boldsymbol{b}}\frac{p_{\boldsymbol{b},<\alpha}(\eta,z)}{(\eta+\alpha z)^{\#I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{b})^{c}}}\cdot x^{{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}},

(9.7)

with
h^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{b},\alpha}=h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
, where
(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})
is defined by the following conditions:

\displaystyle a_{i}

\displaystyle=b_{i}\quad\text{if }i\notin I_{\alpha},

\displaystyle a_{i}

\displaystyle=b_{i}-1\text{ and }\varepsilon_{i}=0\quad\text{if }i\in I_{\alpha}\text{ and }b_{i}\geqslant 1,

\displaystyle a_{i}

\displaystyle=0\text{ and }\varepsilon_{i}=1\quad\text{if }i\in I_{\alpha}\text{ and }b_{i}=0.

The condition that a section
\eqref{eq:decompUab}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}=\eqref{eq:decompUab}\cdot x^{-{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
belongs to
U_{<1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
now reads

\forall\,b\geqslant 0,\quad(\eta+\alpha z)^{\#I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{b})^{c}}\text{ divides }h^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{b},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{b})},v^{\prime},\tau^{\prime},\eta,z).

It is therefore clear that a section of
U_{1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}
belongs, when multiplied by
z
, to
U_{<1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
if and only if it already belongs to
U_{<1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
. In other words,
U_{1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1+\alpha}\big/U_{<1+\alpha}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{<1+\alpha}
has no
z
-torsion.

We conclude that

\displaystyle V_{0}^{t^{\prime}}R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[t^{\prime},v,\tau]\langle\partial_{t^{\prime}},\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1}

\displaystyle=U_{1}\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1}

\displaystyle=V_{1}^{t^{\prime}}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z])\cdot(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1},

hence
i_{g,+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
is generated by
(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1}
. It follows that
\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
is generated by
x^{-\bf 1}\mathbf{e}
.

We will prove the analogue of (
9.2

∗

) after
i_{g,+}
, from which one deduces similarly (
9.2

∗

). We first notice that the equality

V^{t^{\prime}}_{1}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz})\cap(\tau-z)(i_{g,+}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[v,\tau,z])\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/t^{\prime}z}=(\tau-z)V^{t^{\prime}}_{1}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz})

immediately follows from the unique decomposition (
9.6
) of a local section of
V^{t^{\prime}}_{1}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz})
. To end the proof, it therefore suffices to produce a similar unique decomposition of local sections of
V^{t^{\prime}}_{1+k}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}):=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\eth_{t^{\prime}}^{j}V^{t^{\prime}}_{1}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz})
for any
k\geqslant 1
. This is obtained by writing

\eth^{k}_{t^{\prime}}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1}=x^{-k{\boldsymbol{e}}}\eth^{k}_{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime k}(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1}=x^{-k{\boldsymbol{e}}}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{j=0}^{k-1}(\eth_{t^{\prime}}t^{\prime}+jz)(\delta\otimes\mathbf{e})_{1},

giving rise to a formula similar to (
9.6
) for sections of
V^{t^{\prime}}_{1+k}(i_{g,+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz})
, which makes use of polynomials
p_{\boldsymbol{a},k}
(
k\geqslant 1
), derived from
p_{\boldsymbol{a},0}
like in
[
Sab97
, Lem. 4.7]
.
∎

Second step: computation of the
V^{\tau}
-filtration of
\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}

For
\alpha\in[0,1)
, let us set
\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}=\mathrm{e}^{\tau v/gz}/x^{[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]+{\bf 1}}
.

Lemma 9.8
\pointrait

The
V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\tau}
-filtration of
\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
satisfies

V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}=V_{0}^{\tau}R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[v,\tau]\langle\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\quad\forall\,\alpha\in[0,1).

Proof.

Since we are only interested in giving the formula for
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
, we can as well work with the localized module
\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}]
(see
[
Sab05
, Lem. 3.4.1]
). In such a way, we can write

\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}=x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]}(x^{-\bf 1}\mathbf{e})=x^{\lceil(1-\alpha){\boldsymbol{e}}\rceil}\tau^{-1}\eth_{v}(x^{-\bf 1}\mathbf{e}),

showing that
\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
is a section of
\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}]
. For
\alpha\in[0,1)
let us also set

\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}=\Big(\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i\in I_{\alpha}}x_{i}\Big)\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}=:x^{{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}

and, for
p\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle U_{\alpha+p}^{\tau}(\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}])

\displaystyle=\tau^{-p}V_{0}^{\tau}R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[v,\tau]\langle\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}

\displaystyle U_{<\alpha+p}^{\tau}(\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}])

\displaystyle=\tau^{-p}V_{0}^{\tau}R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[v,\tau]\langle\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha},

so that, clearly,

U_{<\alpha+p}^{\tau}(\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}])\subset U_{\alpha+p}^{\tau}(\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}]).

(9.9)

For
p\leqslant 0
, we will set
U_{\alpha+p}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}=U_{\alpha+p}^{\tau}(\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}])
and
U_{<\alpha+p}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}=U_{<\alpha+p}^{\tau}(\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}])
. We will prove that
U^{\tau}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}])
is the good
V^{\tau}
-filtration of
\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}[\tau^{-1}]
. It is enough to prove that
U_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}=V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
for
\alpha\in[0,1)
. The proof will be very similar to that of Lemma
9.2
, although with the variable
\tau
instead of the variable
t^{\prime}
.

By using (
9.9
), one first easily checks that
U_{\alpha-1}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}\subset U_{<\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
and

(\tau\eth_{\tau}+\alpha z)^{\#I_{\alpha}}U_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}\subset U_{<\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}.

Indeed, the first point follows from the relation
\tau\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}=x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}\eth_{v}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}=x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}-{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}}\eth_{v}\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}
, and the second one follows from the relation

(\tau\eth_{\tau}+\alpha z)^{\#I_{\alpha}}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}=\Big(\frac{(-1)^{\#I_{\alpha}}}{\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i\in I_{\alpha}}e_{i}}\Big)\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i\in I_{\alpha}}\eth_{x_{i}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}.

Due to the uniqueness of the
V^{\tau}
-filtration, the assertion of the lemma would follow from the property that
\gr_{\alpha}^{U^{\tau}}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
has no
z
-torsion. We will argue in a way similar to that of Lemma
9.2
by finding a suitable expression for the sections of
U_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
.

Let us decompose
\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z][v,\tau]
as
\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z][v,v\tau]\oplus\tau\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z][v\tau,\tau]
. Due to the relation
v\tau\mathbf{e}=x^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}\tau\eth_{\tau}\mathbf{e}
, we have isomorphisms of
\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z]
-modules

\begin{split}\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z][v,v\tau]\cdot\mathbf{e}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z,v][\tau\eth_{\tau}]\cdot\mathbf{e}\\
\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z][v\tau,\tau]\cdot\mathbf{e}&\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z,\tau]\langle\tau\eth_{\tau}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{e}\end{split}

(9.10)

given respectively by

v^{j}(v\tau)^{k}\mathbf{e}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}x^{k{\boldsymbol{e}}}v^{j}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{k-1}\big(\tau\eth_{\tau}-iz\big)\mathbf{e},\quad(v\tau)^{j}\tau^{k}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}x^{j{\boldsymbol{e}}}\tau^{k}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{j-1}\big(\tau\eth_{\tau}-iz\big)\mathbf{e}.

We thus obtain an isomorphism of free
\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z]
-modules:

\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z][v,\tau]\cdot\mathbf{e}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\big(\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z,v][\tau\eth_{\tau}]\oplus\tau\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},z,\tau]\langle\tau\eth_{\tau}\rangle\big)\cdot\mathbf{e}.

We can replace
\mathbf{e}
with
\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
or
\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}
in the above isomorphism. We will express
U_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
and
U_{<\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
as sub-
\mathscr{O}_{X}[z]
-modules of the right-hand side, and by using the generator
\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}
in both cases, to make the computation of the quotient module easier.

We note first that
U_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}=\mathscr{O}_{X}[v,z]\langle\eth_{x},\eth_{v},\tau\eth_{\tau}\rangle\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
, i.e., we can forget the action of
\tau
, since
\tau^{k}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}=x^{k{\boldsymbol{e}}}\eth_{v}^{k}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
. We have a similar assertion for
U_{<\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
.

From the relation

v^{\ell}\eth_{x}^{\boldsymbol{a}}\eth_{v}^{k}(\tau\eth_{\tau})^{j}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}=\begin{cases}{}\star x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}+(k-\ell){\boldsymbol{e}})}\tau^{k-\ell}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau}+(k-\ell)z,z)\cdot{}\\
\hskip 42.67912pt{}\cdot(\tau\eth_{\tau}+(k-\ell)z)^{j}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{\ell-1}(\tau\eth_{\tau}-iz)\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}&\text{if }k>\ell\geqslant 0,\\[5.0pt]
{}\star x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}v^{\ell-k}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau},z)(\tau\eth_{\tau})^{j}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=0}^{k-1}(\tau\eth_{\tau}-iz)\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}&\text{if }0\leqslant k\leqslant\ell,\end{cases}

for some nonzero constants
\star
and with
p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(s,z)
defined by (
9.4
), we conclude that, through the isomorphism (
9.10
),

U_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}=\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\mathscr{O}_{X}[z][v,\tau\eth_{\tau}]\cdot x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau},z)\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\\[-5.0pt]
{}+\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\sum_{n>0}\tau^{n}\mathscr{O}_{X}[z][\tau\eth_{\tau}]x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}+n{\boldsymbol{e}})}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau}+nz,z)\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}

Formula (
9.4
) shows that, if
\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime}\geqslant 0
, then
p_{\boldsymbol{a}^{\prime},\alpha}
divides
p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}
. It follows that any section of
U_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
can be written as

\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z)x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau},z)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\\[-5.0pt]
{}+\sum_{n>0}\tau^{n}\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}g_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,n}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a}+n{\boldsymbol{e}})},\tau\eth_{\tau},z)x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}+n{\boldsymbol{e}})}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau}+nz,z)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}

(9.11)

with
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}
holomorphic in its
x
-variables and polynomial in
v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z
, and
g_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,n}
holomorphic in its
x
-variables and polynomial in
\tau\eth_{\tau},z
.

Let us check that the decomposition (
9.11
) is unique. The coefficient
h^{(n)}
of
\tau^{n}
(
n\!\geqslant\!0
) is uniquely determined by the section. If
n=0
, the function
h^{(0)}\in\penalty\mathscr{O}_{X,0}[x^{-1},v,\eta,z]
decomposes uniquely as
\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}h^{(0)}_{\boldsymbol{a}}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v,\eta,z)x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}
. Thus
h^{(0)}_{\boldsymbol{a}}
must be divisible by
p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\eta,z)
and this determines uniquely
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v,\eta,z)
. We argue similarly for
n>0
and
h^{(n)}\in\mathscr{O}_{X,0}[x^{-1},\eta,z]
.

There is a similar decomposition for sections of
U_{<\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
, by replacing
p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau},z)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
with
p_{\boldsymbol{a},<\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau},z)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}
. In order to check whether a section (
9.11
) belongs to
U_{<\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
, we replace
\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
with
x^{-{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}}\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}
. Let us decompose (in a unique way)
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z)
as

h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a})},v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z)=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{a})}}h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a}+{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})},v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z)x^{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}},

where
h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
is holomorphic in its
x
-variables and polynomial in
v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z
. We have a similar decomposition for
g_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,n}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a}+n{\boldsymbol{e}})},\tau\eth_{\tau},z)
. Then the decomposition (
9.11
) reads

\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{a})}}\hskip-14.22636pth_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a}+{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})},v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z)x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}+{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau},z)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}\\
{}+\sum_{\boldsymbol{a}\geqslant 0}\sum_{n>0}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{a}+n{\boldsymbol{e}})}}\hskip-14.22636pt\tau^{n}g_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,n}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{a}+n{\boldsymbol{e}}+{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})},\tau\eth_{\tau},z)\cdot{}\\[-5.0pt]
{}\cdot x^{-(\boldsymbol{a}+n{\boldsymbol{e}}+{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})}p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau}+nz,z)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}.

We now note that, for
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\{0,1\}^{I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{a})}
, setting
\boldsymbol{b}=\boldsymbol{a}+{\bf 1}_{I_{\alpha}}-\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
, we have

p_{\boldsymbol{b},<\alpha}(s,z)=(s+\alpha z)^{\#I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{b})^{c}}\cdot p_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha}(s,z).

The unique decomposition (
9.11
) can thus also be written uniquely as

\sum_{\boldsymbol{b}\geqslant 0}h^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{b},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{b})},v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z)x^{-\boldsymbol{b}}\frac{p_{\boldsymbol{b},<\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau},z)}{(\tau\eth_{\tau}+\alpha z)^{\#I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{b})^{c}}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}\\
+\sum_{b\geqslant 0}\sum_{n>0}\tau^{n}g^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{b},\alpha,n}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{b}+n{\boldsymbol{e}})},v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z)x^{-(\boldsymbol{b}+n{\boldsymbol{e}})}\frac{p_{\boldsymbol{b},<\alpha}(\tau\eth_{\tau}+nz,z)}{(\tau\eth_{\tau}+(n+\alpha)z)^{\#I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{b})^{c}}}\cdot\mathbf{e}_{<\alpha}

(9.12)

with
h^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{b},\alpha}=h_{\boldsymbol{a},\alpha,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}
, where
(\boldsymbol{a},\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})
is defined by the following conditions:

\displaystyle a_{i}

\displaystyle=b_{i}\quad\text{if }i\notin I_{\alpha},

\displaystyle a_{i}

\displaystyle=b_{i}-1\text{ and }\varepsilon_{i}=0\quad\text{if }i\in I_{\alpha}\text{ and }b_{i}\geqslant 1,

\displaystyle a_{i}

\displaystyle=0\text{ and }\varepsilon_{i}=1\quad\text{if }i\in I_{\alpha}\text{ and }b_{i}=0,

and similarly for
g^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{b},\alpha,n}
. The condition that a section (
9.12
) belongs to
U_{<\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
now reads

\displaystyle\forall\,b\geqslant 0,\quad(\tau\eth_{\tau}+\alpha z)^{\#I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{b})^{c}}

\displaystyle\text{ divides }h^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{b},\alpha}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{b})},v,\tau\eth_{\tau},z),

\displaystyle\forall\,b\geqslant 0,\forall\,n>0,\quad(\tau\eth_{\tau}+(n+\alpha)z)^{\#I_{\alpha}(\boldsymbol{b})^{c}}

\displaystyle\text{ divides }g^{\prime}_{\boldsymbol{b},\alpha,n}(x_{I(\boldsymbol{b}+n{\boldsymbol{e}})},\tau\eth_{\tau},z).

and

It is therefore clear that a section (
9.12
) of
U_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
belongs, when multiplied by
z
, to
U_{<\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
if and only if it already belongs to
U_{<\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
. In other words,
\gr_{\alpha}^{U^{\tau}}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
has no
z
-torsion.
∎

Third step: End the proof of Theorem
9.1
in the
v
-chart

We will now use the expression of Lemma
9.8
to regard
V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}
as an
\mathscr{O}_{X}[v,\tau,z]
-submodule of
\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},v,\tau,z]\cdot\penalty\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
. We can write (locally on
X
near
P
):

V_{0}^{\tau}(R_{F}\mathscr{D}_{X}[v,\tau]\langle\partial_{v},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)=\mathscr{O}_{X}[v,\tau,z]\langle\eth_{x},\eth_{y^{\prime}},\eth_{v},\tau\eth_{\tau}\rangle

and we notice that the action of
\tau\eth_{\tau}
on
\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
is equal to that of
v\eth_{v}
, so we can forget
\tau\eth_{\tau}
. We will also forget
(y^{\prime},\eth_{y^{\prime}})
which plays no significant role. Recall that the variables
x
are indexed as
x_{1},\dots,x_{\ell}
. Working now within
\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},v,\tau,z]\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
, we have by induction on
|\boldsymbol{a}|
,

(x\eth_{x})^{\boldsymbol{a}}\eth_{v}^{c}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\equiv z^{|\boldsymbol{a}|+c}x^{-c{\boldsymbol{e}}}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{a}|}q_{\boldsymbol{a},c,j}(x)x^{-j{\boldsymbol{e}}}v^{j}\bigg)\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\mod(\tau-z)\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},v,\tau,z],

where
q_{\boldsymbol{a},c,j}(x)
is some polynomial and
q_{\boldsymbol{a},c,|\boldsymbol{a}|}(x)
is a nonzero constant. From this one concludes that there exist polynomials
r_{\boldsymbol{a},c,j}(x,z)
with
r_{\boldsymbol{a},c,|\boldsymbol{a}|}(x,z)
constant, such that

\eth_{x}^{\boldsymbol{a}}\eth_{v}^{c}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\equiv z^{|\boldsymbol{a}|+c}x^{-(|\boldsymbol{a}|+c){\boldsymbol{e}}}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{|\boldsymbol{a}|}r_{\boldsymbol{a},c,j}(x,z)x^{(|\boldsymbol{a}|-j){\boldsymbol{e}}-\boldsymbol{a}}v^{j}\bigg)x^{-[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]-{\bf 1}}\mathbf{e}\\[-5.0pt]
\mod(\tau-z)\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},v,\tau,z],

and since for
0\leqslant j\leqslant|\boldsymbol{a}|
we have
|\boldsymbol{a}-(|\boldsymbol{a}|-j){\boldsymbol{e}}|_{+}\leqslant j
(see the end of the proof of Lemma
7.15
), the coefficient of
v^{j}
belongs to
F_{j}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})([(\alpha+p)P])
with
p=|\boldsymbol{a}|+c
. Using that

\displaystyle(\tau-z)V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}

\displaystyle=(\tau-z)\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}\cap V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}\quad\text{(see\kern 3.06668pt\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{E-S-Y13}{}{}, Proof of Prop.\,3.1.2]})}

\displaystyle=(\tau-z)\mathscr{O}_{X}[x^{-1},v,\tau,z]\cap V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}\quad\text{(Lemma \ref{lem:calculEtauvfhb})},

we conclude that the coefficient of
z^{p}
in the
\gr\big(V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}/(\tau-z)V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau v/gz}\big)
(graded with respect to the
z
-adic filtration) is contained in
F_{\alpha+p}E^{vf}
, so
F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\alpha+p}E^{vf}\subset F_{\alpha+p}E^{vf}
, according to Remark
5.7
.

In order to obtain the reverse inclusion, we remark that, for
|\boldsymbol{a}|+c=p
fixed,
x^{-\boldsymbol{a}}v^{|\boldsymbol{a}|}\mathrm{e}^{vf}/x^{[(\alpha+p){\boldsymbol{e}}]+\bf 1}\in F_{|\boldsymbol{a}|}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})([(\alpha+p)P])v^{|\boldsymbol{a}|}\mathrm{e}^{vf}
is equal, up to a nonzero constant and modulo
\sum_{j<|\boldsymbol{a}|}F_{j}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})([(\alpha+p)P])v^{j}\mathrm{e}^{vf}
, to the class of
\eth_{x}^{\boldsymbol{a}}\eth_{v}^{c}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}
. We conclude by induction on
|\boldsymbol{a}|
, the case where
|\boldsymbol{a}|=0
being clear.∎

9.b.
Computation in the
u
-chart

Computation away from
P_{\mathrm{red}}

We have
F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E^{f/u}=G_{0}E^{f/u}=\mathscr{O}_{X\smallsetminus P}[u]\mathrm{e}^{f/u}
for
p\geqslant 0
. We set similarly
\mathbf{e}=\mathrm{e}^{\tau f/uz}
.

Lemma 9.13
\pointrait

With respect to the inclusion
\mathscr{E}^{\tau f/uz}\subset\mathscr{O}_{X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}}}[u,u^{-1},\tau,z]\cdot\mathbf{e}
, we have
\mathbf{e}\in\mathscr{E}^{\tau f/uz}
.

Proof.

The question is local near a point of
\{f=0\}
, since otherwise we have equality in the previous inclusion, according to Proposition
3.4
, and it amounts to proving that
\mathbf{e}\in V_{1}^{u}(\mathscr{O}_{X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}}}[u,u^{-1},\tau,z]\cdot\mathbf{e})
, so we are reduced to computing the order of
\mathbf{e}
with respect to the
V^{u}
-filtration.

Let us first assume that the divisor
\{f=0\}
has normal crossings. Let us choose local coordinates
x_{1},\dots,x_{n}
such that
f(x)=x^{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
with
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in\mathbb{N}^{n}
(a local setting not to be confused with that of §
7.a
). From the relation
u\eth_{u}\mathbf{e}=-(\tau f/u)\mathbf{e}
we obtain

\eth_{x_{i}}(f\mathbf{e})=\frac{m_{i}z}{x_{i}}\,x^{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\mathbf{e}+\frac{m_{i}}{x_{i}}\,\frac{\tau f}{u}\,x^{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\mathbf{e}=-m_{i}(u\eth_{u}-z)x^{{\boldsymbol{m}}-{\bf 1}_{i}}\mathbf{e},

and iterating the process we find

\eth_{x}^{{\boldsymbol{m}}}(f\mathbf{e})=(-{\boldsymbol{m}})^{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{n}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{j=1}^{m_{i}}(u\eth_{u}-jz/m_{i})\cdot\mathbf{e}.

Since
f\mathbf{e}=u\eth_{\tau}\mathbf{e}
, this gives a Bernstein relation for
\mathbf{e}
showing that
\mathbf{e}\in V_{<0}^{u}(\mathscr{O}_{X\smallsetminus P_{\mathrm{red}}}[u,u^{-1},\tau,z]\cdot\mathbf{e})
.

When
\{f=0\}
is arbitrary, the proof proceeds exactly like in
[
Kas76
]
. We work locally near a point of
\{f=0\}
and we choose a projective birational morphism
\pi:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
which is an isomorphism away from
\{f=0\}
and such that
f^{\prime}:=f\circ\pi
defines a normal crossing divisor. Using the global section
\mathrm{e}^{\tau f^{\prime}/uz}
of
V_{<0}^{u}\mathscr{E}^{\tau f^{\prime}/uz}
(first part of the proof), one constructs a global section
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
of
\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}V_{<0}^{u}\mathscr{E}^{\tau f^{\prime}/uz}
which coincides with
\mathbf{e}
away from
\{f=0\}
. This is done by using the global section
{\bf 1}_{\mathscr{X}\mathchoice{\longleftarrow}{\leftarrow}{\leftarrow}{\leftarrow}\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}
of
\mathscr{R}_{\mathscr{X}\mathchoice{\longleftarrow}{\leftarrow}{\leftarrow}{\leftarrow}\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}[u,\tau]\langle u\eth_{u}\rangle
. Because
\mathscr{E}^{\tau f^{\prime}/uz}
underlies a mixed twistor module,
\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau f^{\prime}/uz}
is strictly specializable along
u=0
and we have
V_{<0}^{u}\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau f^{\prime}/uz}=\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}V_{<0}^{u}\mathscr{E}^{\tau f^{\prime}/uz}
. Therefore,
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
is a section of
V_{<0}^{u}\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\mathscr{E}^{\tau f^{\prime}/uz}
, and thus satisfies a non-trivial Bernstein equation of the form

\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{\beta<0}(u\eth_{u}+\beta z)^{\nu_{\beta}}\cdot\mathbf{e}^{\prime}=uP(x,u,\eth_{x},\eth_{\tau},u\eth_{u})\cdot\mathbf{e}^{\prime}.

We conclude that
\mathbf{e}
satisfies the same equation away from
\{f=0\}
, hence everywhere, since
\mathscr{O}_{X}[u,u^{-1},\tau,z]
has no
\mathscr{O}_{X}
-torsion.
∎

Due to the relation
f\tau\eth_{u}\mathbf{e}=-\tau^{2}\eth_{\tau}^{2}\mathbf{e}
we conclude that

V_{0}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau f/uz}\supset V_{0}^{\tau}R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{(X\smallsetminus P)}[u,\tau]\langle\partial_{u},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)\cdot\mathbf{e}\supset\mathscr{O}_{X\smallsetminus P}[u,\tau,z]\cdot\mathbf{e}.

Then, computing modulo
(\tau-z)\mathscr{E}^{\tau f/uz}
,
F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{irr}}E^{f/u}
contains
\mathscr{O}_{X\smallsetminus P}[u,z]\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/u}=G_{0}E^{f/u}
, hence
F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E^{f/u}=F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E^{f/u}
away from
P_{\mathrm{red}}
.

Remark 9.14
.

The explicit computation of
F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{irr}}E^{f/u}
in the neighbourhood of
f=\penalty 0
would be more complicated, and restricting to
G_{0}E^{f/u}
allows us to avoid this computation. Let us however note that, in the neighbourhood of the smooth locus of
f^{-1}(0)
, an explicit formula for
F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{irr}}E^{f/u}
can be obtained from Lemma
9.8
by setting there
g=u
and
v=f
. Since the order of the pole at
u=0
is one, the only interesting
\alpha
is zero, and the result is:

F_{p}^{\mathrm{irr}}E^{f/u}=\frac{1}{u^{p+1}}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{p}\mathscr{O}_{X}[u]\frac{f^{k}}{u^{k}}\Big)\cdot\mathrm{e}^{f/u}\quad\text{($f$ smooth)}.

This formula extends in a natural way to
F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{irr}}E^{f/u}(*H)
, provided that moreover
f^{-1}(0)
has no common component with
H
and that
f^{-1}(0)\cup H
has normal crossings.

Computation near
P_{\mathrm{red}}

The computation is similar to, and even simpler than, the computation done in the
v
-chart. Indeed, due to Proposition
3.4
, we have
\mathscr{E}^{\tau/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z}=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u,u^{-1},\tau,z]\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z}
, and there is no need for an analogue of Lemma
9.2
. We will consider the variable
u
as part of the
x
-variables, and the divisor
u=0
of
\EuScript{X}
(see
§
1.b
). For
\alpha\in[0,1)
, we set
\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}=\mathrm{e}^{\tau/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z}/ux^{[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]+\bf 1}
. The following lemma is similar to Lemma
9.8
.

Lemma 9.15
\pointrait

The
V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}^{\tau}
-filtration of
\mathscr{E}^{\tau/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z}
satisfies

V_{\alpha}^{\tau}\mathscr{E}^{\tau/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z}=V_{0}^{\tau}R_{F}(\mathscr{D}_{X}[u,\tau]\langle\partial_{u},\partial_{\tau}\rangle)\cdot\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\quad\forall\,\alpha\in[0,1).

∎

We also obtain

\eth_{x}^{\boldsymbol{a}}\eth_{u}^{b}\mathbf{e}_{\alpha}\in z^{|\boldsymbol{a}|+b}F_{|\boldsymbol{a}|+b}\big(\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u,u^{-1}]\big)\big([(\alpha+|\boldsymbol{a}|+b)\EuScript{P}]\big)\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z}\\
\mod(\tau-z)\mathscr{E}^{\tau/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z},

where
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\big(\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u,u^{-1}]\big)
is the filtration by the order of the pole along
\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}}
. Moreover, the coefficient of
(ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}})^{-(|\boldsymbol{a}|+b)}\cdot(ux^{[\alpha{\boldsymbol{e}}]+\bf 1})^{-1}\cdot\mathrm{e}^{\tau/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}z}
is a nonzero constant. It follows that

F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{irr}}E^{1/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}=F_{p}\big(\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u,u^{-1}]\big)\big([(\alpha+p)\EuScript{P}]\big)\cdot\mathrm{e}^{1/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}.

Intersecting with
G_{0}E^{1/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}=\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})[u]\mathrm{e}^{1/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}
gives

F_{\alpha+p}^{\mathrm{irr}}E^{1/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}\cap G_{0}E^{1/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}=F_{p}\mathscr{O}_{X}(*P_{\mathrm{red}})\big([(\alpha+p)P]\big)[u]\cdot\mathrm{e}^{1/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}=F_{\alpha+p}G_{0}E^{1/ux^{{\boldsymbol{e}}}}.

This ends the proof of Theorem
9.1
.∎

9.c.
Another approach of Theorem
9.1
at the de Rham level

Let us assume that the zero divisor
f^{-1}(0)
of
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
is smooth, that it has no component in common with
D
, and that
f^{-1}(0)\cup D
still has normal crossings. We have a filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E
(by using the formula given in Remark
9.14
in the
u
-chart). Then the proof of Theorem
9.1
gives in fact the equality
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E=F^{\mathrm{irr}}_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}E
.

Let
\pi:\widetilde{\EuScript}{X}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\EuScript{X}
be a projective birational morphism such that
vf
extend as a morphism
\widetilde{vf}:\widetilde{\EuScript}{X}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
and
\widetilde{\EuScript}{D}:=\pi^{-1}(\EuScript{D})
is a normal crossing divisor in
\widetilde{\EuScript}{X}
. The pole divisor of
vf
is
\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}}
and that of
\widetilde{vf}
, that we denote by
\widetilde{\EuScript}{P}_{\mathrm{red}}
, is contained in
\pi^{-1}(\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}})
. We denote by
\widetilde{\EuScript}{H}
the remaining components of
\widetilde{\EuScript}{D}
. The construction of
[
ESY15
]
produces a filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}\EuScript{E}^{\widetilde{vf}}(*\widetilde{\EuScript}{H})
. Note that

\pi_{+}\EuScript{E}^{\widetilde{vf}}(*\widetilde{\EuScript}{H})=\mathscr{H}^{0}\pi_{+}\EuScript{E}^{\widetilde{vf}}(*\widetilde{\EuScript}{H})=\EuScript{E}^{(v:u)f}(*\EuScript{H})=:\EuScript{E}.

By Theorem
1.3
, the push-forward
\pi_{+}\big(\EuScript{E}^{\widetilde{vf}}(*\widetilde{\EuScript}{H}),F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}\EuScript{E}^{\widetilde{vf}}(*\widetilde{\EuScript}{H})\big)
is strict, since
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{Del}}\EuScript{E}^{\widetilde{vf}}(*\widetilde{\EuScript}{H})=F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{irr}}\EuScript{E}^{\widetilde{vf}}(*\widetilde{\EuScript}{H})
, and produces the filtration
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}^{\mathrm{irr}}\EuScript{E}
, which is nothing but
F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\EuScript{E}
by Theorem
9.1
in the present setting. The strictness of the push-forward implies a quasi-isomorphism at the de Rham level:

F_{\alpha}^{p}\DR\EuScript{E}\simeq\boldsymbol{R}\pi_{*}F_{\mathrm{Del},\alpha}^{p}\DR\EuScript{E}^{\widetilde{vf}}(*\widetilde{\EuScript}{H}),

(9.16)

where, as usual, we set for a filtered
\mathscr{D}
-module
(\EuScript{M},F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\EuScript{M})
,

F^{p}\DR\EuScript{M}=\{F_{-p}\EuScript{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{1}\otimes F_{-p+1}\EuScript{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots\}.

We will show how to recover the quasi-isomorphism (
9.16
) for a suitable modification
\pi:\widetilde{\EuScript}{X}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\EuScript{X}
by a direct computation. This will give, in the present setting, a proof of the degeneration at
E_{1}
of the spectral sequence attached to the hypercohomology of
\boldsymbol{H}^{k}(\EuScript{X},F_{\alpha+{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\DR\EuScript{E})
which only relies on
[
ESY15
]
for
\widetilde{vf}:\widetilde{\EuScript}{X}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and not on the finer results of Theorem
1.3
. However, the identification at the level of filtered
\mathscr{D}
-modules, and not only at the level of filtered de Rham complexes, is needed for the application to Kontsevich bundles given in Theorem
1.11
.

Let us set, for each
p
and
\alpha\in[0,1)
,

F_{{\mathrm{N}},\alpha}^{p}\DR\EuScript{E}=\Big\{\Omega_{\EuScript}{X}^{p}(\log\EuScript{D})([\alpha\EuScript{P}])\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}(vf)\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}(vf)}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Omega_{\EuScript}{X}^{p+1}(\log\EuScript{D})([(\alpha+1)\EuScript{P}])\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots\Big\}[-p].

Such a filtration already appeared in
[
Yu14
]
in the study of the toric case, where the notation
F^{\lambda}_{\mathrm{NP}}(\nabla)
was used (NP for Newton polygon).

Lemma 9.17
\pointrait

The natural morphism
F_{{\mathrm{N}},\alpha}^{p}\DR\EuScript{E}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F_{\alpha}^{p}\DR\EuScript{E}
is a quasi-isomorphism.

Proof.

Let us prove the lemma in the
v
-chart for instance (the proof in the
u
-chart is similar), and let us assume that
H=\varnothing
for the sake of simplicity, so that
\EuScript{D}=\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}}
(the general case is obtained by a Kunneth formula). Recall that
n=\dim X
. Everything below is thus only valid on
X\times\mathbb{C}_{v}
. Consider the following complexes, with differentials induced by
{\mathrm{d}}+{\mathrm{d}}(vf)
:

\Phi_{1}(p):\Big\{\Omega^{p}_{\EuScript}{X}(\log\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}})\big([\alpha\EuScript{P}]\big)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{p+1}_{\EuScript}{X}(\log\EuScript{P}_{\mathrm{red}})\big([(\alpha+1)\EuScript{P}]\big)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\cdots\\
